## ABG — ASBURY AUTOMOTIVE GROUP INC
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.78
**Sector**: Consumer Cyclical  |  **Industry**: Auto & Truck Dealerships
**Filing**: 0001144980-26-000051_10K.txt  (2026-02-20)

### Summary
ABG is a -29% drawdown name where the FORENSIC tape directly REJECTS the SHORT thesis: CEO David Hult put $1.03M of personal cash into open-market purchases on 2026-03-06 (post-Q4 print), independent director DiSantis added at $202 in March AND $182 in May (scaling in through the bottom), and 10%+ holder Abrams Capital absorbed $10.6M of stock at $225 after the Herb Chambers deal. P:S ratio 11.6x with ZERO CEO/CFO sales in 12 months. Business is honestly disclosed as moderately impaired (recurring ~$140M annual franchise-rights impairments, -9% same-store new-vehicle revenue in Q1'26, integration drag from Herb Chambers driving SG&A +12%, $442M Real Estate Facility balloon maturing Dec 2026) but covenants are clean, leverage 3.2x within target range, EY clean opinion, $780M revolver unused. Reported earnings flatter the underlying organic decline via divestiture gains ($80M FY25, $126M Q1'26), so headline 'growth' overstates true operating trend — but issuer discloses these line-items transparently, no fraud or accounting deception. Verdict: LONG-tier. The insider-vs-narrative test is the strongest signal Signal OS can produce and ABG is the clearest insider-conviction setup in the forward-test universe. Severity: 4 PASS / 5 MODERATE / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 0 UNVERIFIABLE.

### R/f/M tuples (9)

#### [ABG-01-insider-buying-CEO]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Management commitment to capital allocation strategy and confidence in the franchise after FY2025 results (10-K filed 2026-02-20).
- **f**: Parse Form 4 XML for code-P (open-market purchases) by named executives between Feb 2026 10-K filing and cutoff 2026-05-25.
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse)`
- **M_value**: CEO David W. Hult bought 5,000 shares open-market on 2026-03-06 (split 1,604 @ $204.73 + 3,396 @ $205.63 = $1.027M) — TWO weeks after Feb 20 10-K filing, into the drawdown. Independent director B. Christopher DiSantis bought 500 sh on 2026-03-10 @ $202.30 ($101K) and added 157 sh on 2026-05-20 @ $182.31 ($29K) — i.e., bought AGAIN after the Apr 28 Q1 print and through May, scaling INTO weakness. Prior to filing window, Abrams Capital Management (10%+ holder) bought 46,952 sh on 2025-07-30 @ $225.16 ($10.57M) holding 2.16M shares. Aggregate 12mo open-market purchases $11.73M vs sales $1.01M = 11.6x P:S ratio. Zero CEO/CFO sales in the 12mo window.
- **interpretation**: STRONG honest-insider tape. The CEO using personal cash post-Q4 earnings, plus repeat director buys scaling in, plus a 10%+ holder reloading, is the textbook 'management aligned with shareholders' signal. In the Signal-OS framework this is the cleanest LONG-bias evidence: zero asymmetric selling, zero clustered pre-bad-news selling, and the most senior officer voluntarily took risk at $204-205 when the stock was already off ~20%. Not RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE — opposite.

#### [ABG-02-insider-vs-calendar]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider transactions timed independently of capital events / non-public information.
- **f**: Cross-reference Form 4 transaction dates against the 8-K calendar (capital events, earnings, divestitures, acquisitions) for 2025-2026.
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: Capital event calendar: Herb Chambers $1.76B acquisition closed 2025-07-21 (8-K 2025-07-21); $546.5M Real Estate Facility drawn same day. ABRAMS open-market buy 2025-07-30 — 9 days AFTER the largest acquisition in company history was announced (informed institutional capital absorbing the post-deal weakness). CEO Hult buy 2026-03-06 — 14 days after 10-K filing (Feb 20) and 29 days after Q4 earnings 8-K (Feb 5). DiSantis 2026-03-10 and 2026-05-20 buys are AFTER Q4 and Q1 earnings, respectively — i.e., outside informational asymmetry windows. NO insider sales in the 30-day windows prior to either the Q4 (Feb 5) or Q1 (Apr 28) prints. Only sales in 12mo window are routine Aug/Nov 2025 by SVP CHRO (Milstein), SVP Counsel (Calloway), and two directors — all small ($100-285K) and well-separated from material events.
- **interpretation**: Timing is clean — buys clustered AFTER bad-news disclosures (i.e., once info is public), not before. No evidence of clustered selling pre-disclosure. This RAISES confidence that insiders believe the drawdown is overdone.

#### [ABG-03-impairment-recurrence]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "During the year ended December 31, 2025, we recognized asset impairment charges of $141.0 million associated with manufacturer franchise rights ... compared to $149.5 million ... during the year ended December 31, 2024."
- **f**: Verify trend of recurring non-cash impairments across years; check whether this is a one-time event or chronic.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 7 narrative + Note 7)`
- **M_value**: Three-year impairment history: 2025 $141.0M, 2024 $149.5M, 2023 $117.2M. Cumulative $407.7M over three years — i.e., management is consistently overpaying for franchise rights in M&A (LHM 2021, Koons 2023, Herb Chambers 2025) and writing them down annually. This is a recurring, not episodic, charge.
- **interpretation**: Disclosure is honest (charges are quantified and the audit committee disclosed them as a critical audit matter), but the chronic nature of the impairments indicates an M&A engine that systematically overstates synergies. Not fraud — the issuer is transparent. But the franchise-rights asset base ($1B+) remains at risk and 2026 could see another round given the underperforming stores cited in Note 7.

#### [ABG-04-organic-growth-stalled]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Capital allocation strategy presented as additive: "Accelerate same store growth and guest experience through technology investment" (Item 1 strategy).
- **f**: Cross-check 10-K and Q1 2026 10-Q same-store revenue/gross-profit deltas to test whether organic growth supports the strategy claim.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 7 same-store tables + Q1 10-Q same-store tables)`
- **M_value**: FY2025 same-store new vehicle units +3%, but same-store new vehicle gross profit -8% (-$49M) on margin compression. Same-store used vehicle revenue -4% (-$185M). FY2025 reported net-income growth +14% to $492M is driven by a $80.2M divestiture gain + Herb Chambers contribution + lower impairment, not organic. Q1 2026 is starker: same-store new vehicle revenue -9% (-$187M); same-store domestic new vehicle gross profit -31%; Q1 income from operations -17%; SG&A +12% ($54M). Reported Q1 net income +42% to $187.8M ONLY because of a $125.8M divestiture gain — backing it out, pre-tax income ex-divestiture is ~$125M vs $171M Q1'25 (-27%).
- **interpretation**: The reported headline numbers are 'good' but the underlying organic business is in moderate decline (used vehicle softness, new vehicle margin normalization, SG&A creep from Herb Chambers integration). Issuer discloses the divestiture gain prominently — not deceptive, but the gap between headline EPS and same-store operating EPS is material and likely explains some of the drawdown.

#### [ABG-05-leverage-and-2026-maturity]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "The Company's transaction-adjusted net leverage ratio was 3.2x as of December 31, 2025, compared to 2.9x as of December 31, 2024. We believe our cash position and borrowing capacity, combined with our current and expected future cash generation capability, provide us with financial flexibility ..."
- **f**: Verify the maturity wall and refinancing exposure — cross-check current maturities and the specific 2026 maturing facility.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K balance sheet + Note 12 debt schedule)`
- **M_value**: Current maturities of long-term debt jumped from $114.7M (2024) to $479.2M (2025). Aggregate maturity schedule: 2026 $484.4M, 2027 $42.4M, 2028 $610.2M, 2029 $837.7M. The 2026 maturity is dominated by the 2021 Real Estate Facility ($442.1M balloon, 5-yr from Dec 17, 2021 = due Dec 2026). Total debt $3.59B, leverage drifted from 2.9x → 3.2x (above target 2.5-3.5x range, near top). $1.57B floor plan facility on top. Q1 2026: all covenants compliant. Refinancing the $442M RE facility at higher rates than 2021 (when SOFR was ~0%) will pressure interest expense — already +5% YoY in 2025 ($187.5M total other interest).
- **interpretation**: Leverage is within target but at the high end after Herb Chambers, and the $442M 2026 RE-facility balloon is the most concrete near-term risk. Issuer discloses this transparently in the maturity table. Not severe — ABG has revolver capacity ($925M total, only $120M drawn at 12/31/25 = ~$780M unused), liquidity is intact, and they generated $492M of net income with $80M+ of divestiture cash. But the refi at 5-6% SOFR vs 1.55% spread originally will compress earnings and is part of the drawdown narrative.

#### [ABG-06-buyback-at-elevated-price]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Information about the shares of our common stock that we repurchased during the quarter ended December 31, 2025 is set forth below ... Total 212,308 shares" averaged ~$235.
- **f**: Cross-check Q4 2025 + Q1 2026 buyback execution prices vs cutoff price (~$172 estimated from -29.4% drawdown peak) for capital-allocation discipline.

Combined ~890K shares for ~$197M at avg ~$221, well above the cutoff-period implied price. Remaining authorization $175.9M at 12/31/25. Buybacks were aggressive THROUGH the drawdown but at price points materially above where the stock trades at cutoff.
- **interpretation**: ABG bought back at the wrong prices — they didn't anticipate further weakness. However, this is the SAME signal as the CEO Form 4 purchase: management put company AND personal cash to work because they believed the stock was undervalued at $200-235. This is consistent with management conviction, not value destruction by complacency. Mild negative for capital allocation discipline; no governance red flag.

#### [ABG-07-Herb-Chambers-integration]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "The Herb Chambers acquisition increased the Company's footprint in the northeast region of the United States." Strategic narrative implies accretion.
- **f**: Mode B (implied verification) — given the $1.76B deal closed July 2025, look for evidence of dilution/integration drag in subsequent quarters.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 2026 10-Q MD&A — SG&A and same-store decomposition)`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 SG&A +$54M (+12%) YoY largely driven by Herb Chambers operating expenses being included for full quarter (deal closed Jul 21, 2025 so Q1'25 has zero contribution). Reported gross profit only +0.4% ($726.9M vs $724.2M) — i.e., the $1.76B acquisition is delivering minimal incremental gross profit in Q1, suggesting integration drag and/or that the prior-period base was unusually strong. Income from operations DOWN $40.4M in Q1 2026.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: the Herb Chambers narrative is framed as additive but the math shows it added cost faster than gross profit in Q1. Could be one-quarter integration noise (deal-related amortization, severance, system migration) or could be structural. The 10-Q does not explicitly call this out, which is a mild Mode-B gap. Still in the 'normal post-acquisition' band, not severe.

#### [ABG-08-covenant-compliance]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "We were in compliance with all of our covenants as of March 31, 2026."
- **f**: Verify by checking 2023 SCF and Real Estate Facility covenants (minimum fixed charge coverage, max lease-adjusted leverage) against actual ratios.

Auditor (EY) issued clean opinion with critical audit matter on impairment testing only.

#### [ABG-09-no-dividend-policy]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "We have not paid any dividends since 2008."

## DEI — Douglas Emmett, Inc.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.65
**Sector**: Real Estate  |  **Industry**: REIT - Office
**Filing**: 0001364250-26-000011_10K.txt  (2026-02-20)

### Summary
DEI is genuinely distressed (78.0% office occupancy down 700bps from 2021; FFO -14.5% YoY; net loss in Q1 2026; 100% Return-of-Capital dividend tax classification; $1.18B 2026 debt maturity wall) BUT the disclosure quality is high and management has put substantial personal capital on the line. Form-4 evidence is the dominant signal: zero insider sales in 18 months, CEO Jordan Kaplan bought $998k at $10.18 three trading days after Q4-2025 earnings, EVP/GC bought $492k in November post-Q3, director $592k in June 2024 — total $2.08M open-market purchases in 18 months with ZERO sales. Per AGENT_PROMPT guidance for Form 4 evidence: 'open-market code-P purchases by C-suite or directors, sized meaningfully, into drawdown — note this explicitly even if the rest of the disclosure is distressed.' This is the exact opposite of the IONQ short-thesis pattern. The honesty-alpha framework is designed to catch lying-during-distress; DEI is distressed-but-not-lying with strong insider commitment. Verdict: NEUTRAL with LONG bias on the insider signal.

### R/f/M tuples (8)

#### [DEI-01-occupancy-trend]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Office Occupancy Rate as of December 31, 2025 was 78.0% (versus 79.2% 2024, 81.0% 2023, 83.7% 2022, 84.9% 2021). Multifamily Occupancy 98.0% (10-K Item 7).
- **f**: Mode-A: confirm stated occupancy against subsequent 10-Q reported numbers (Q1 2026 10-Q filed 2026-05-08).
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-Q 0001364250-26-000032)`
- **M_value**: 10-Q reports March 31, 2026 office occupancy 77.5% (down 50bps QoQ), Q1 2026 average office occupancy 77.7% (down 50bps from 78.2% FY25 avg). Office Leased Rate ticked up 50bps to 80.9%. Multifamily Occupancy 98.1%.
- **interpretation**: 10-K disclosed occupancy honestly; subsequent 10-Q shows continued, monotonic deterioration in office occupancy — fifth consecutive year of declines (~700bps since 2021). Trend is consistent and unhidden, but the operational signal is clearly deteriorating. The 50bps QoQ slip with Leased Rate +50bps suggests signed-not-commenced is building but execution is slow. Not a SEVERE disclosure issue — DEI quantifies it cleanly each quarter — but the underlying business is genuinely impaired.

#### [DEI-02-FFO-decline]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY2025 FFO was $295.3M, down $50.2M / -14.5% from $345.5M in 2024. 'Our FFO was adversely impacted by the effects of inflation and higher interest rates during 2025 and 2024.' (10-K MD&A)
- **f**: Mode-A: cross-check the -14.5% FFO decline against the components (interest expense + NOI) and against Q1 2026 trajectory.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K MD&A + 10-Q income statement)`
- **M_value**: Interest expense FY25 $266.7M vs FY24 $229.4M (+16.3%); FY25 Same-Property Office NOI $478.2M vs $487.2M (-1.9%); Q1 2026 net (loss) attributable to common -$2.5M (vs +$39.8M Q1 2025) and Q1 EPS -$0.02 vs +$0.24. Interest expense Q1 2026 cash $57.3M vs $57.8M (flat) — net loss driven primarily by absence of $47.2M JV-consolidation gain that boosted Q1 2025.
- **interpretation**: FFO declining ~15% per year is real distress, but the issuer discloses drivers cleanly: rising interest expense as swaps roll off (effective rate on fixed/swapped debt only 4.39% currently, vs market 5-6%) and lower office NOI. Q1 2025 vs Q1 2026 comp is distorted by a one-time non-cash gain. Not a Mode-B catch — the issuer is transparent; the business itself is impaired.

#### [DEI-03-dividend-quality]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Total / Weighted Average dividend for 2025 was $0.76 per share; '$ 0.19 — — 100.0% — Return of Capital' for each quarter (10-K Note 11 / Dividends unaudited classification).
- **f**: Mode-B: GIVEN dividend was held at $0.76/yr through a 14.5% FFO decline, derive coverage ratio — what does retention look like vs CapEx + debt amortization needs?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K & 10-Q cash flow statements + balance sheet)`
- **M_value**: FY25 dividends paid to common ~$127M (4× $31.8M); FY25 basic EPS $0.09; cash from operations $386.9M vs CapEx $265.3M = $121.6M free cash before refi → dividend roughly fully consumes FCF. 100% of 2025 dividends classified as Return of Capital (not Ordinary Income), confirming there is no taxable REIT income behind them. Dividend was already cut in 2023 from $0.28 to $0.19. Cash on B/S $357M vs $1.18B of 2026 maturities.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B catch. The 100% Return-of-Capital tax classification is a quiet, structurally honest tell that current distributions exceed REIT taxable income — the dividend is being funded out of depreciation cushion + asset basis, not earnings. With $1.18B of 2026 maturities, refi expense uncertainty, continued occupancy decline, and FCF approximately equal to dividend obligation, there is meaningful tail-risk of another dividend cut. The issuer is not required to disclose 'dividend at risk' — they meet REIT distribution minimums via Return of Capital — but the structural reality is that the payout is unsupported by current earnings.

#### [DEI-04-debt-maturity-wall]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Future Principal Payments: '2026 $ 1,181,987 [thousand]' / four term loans maturing Aug-Nov 2026 totaling $1.18B at SOFR + ~1.20-1.25% (currently floating, no swap, no cap). (10-K Note 8 + 10-Q Note 6)
- **f**: Mode-B: derive refinancing risk. GIVEN 21% of total debt rolls in 7-10 months at current market rates and the issuer is in an actively distressed office sector, what is the rate gap?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K debt schedule + interest expense run-rate)`
- **M_value**: Three large 2026 maturities: $415M (Aug 15), $366M (Sep 19), $400M (Nov 1) at SOFR+~1.25% (currently floating). Weighted average annual interest rate on fixed/swapped portion 4.39% (10-K) / 4.41% (10-Q). New term loans issued Aug 2025 ($941M of Fannie Mae loans) were priced at 4.80% fixed → so refis are inflicting +40-60bps incremental cost. Cash on hand $357M = covers only 30% of 2026 maturity stack.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B catch but not SEVERE: the maturity wall is large and refi spread is rising, but DEI demonstrated capability in Aug 2025 ($941M Fannie Mae closing replacing $930M of old loans). Fannie Mae multifamily funding is reliable; office refis are more uncertain. The 2028 maturity stack ($1.31B) is the larger tail risk. Issuer discloses the table fully — not a hidden item — but a 21% rollover at +50bps cost is real interest-expense headwind ahead.

#### [DEI-05-tenant-diversification-vs-2026-expiries]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Top tenant William Morris Endeavor is 2.8% of Annualized Rent; top 5 tenants combined 8.6%; 'a diverse mix of industries' (10-K Item 1).
- **f**: Mode-A confirms diversification; Mode-B derives: given strong diversification, what is the lease-expiration cliff in 2026 that issuer doesn't headline?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K In-Service Office Lease Expirations table)`
- **M_value**: 566 leases expiring in 2026 covering 2,044,738 SF (11.7% of total) representing $94.0M annualized rent (14.6% of total office rent). 2027 expirations are 12.9% of SF / 16.5% of rent. Tenant base is genuinely diverse: top 5 = 8.6%, top tenant 2.8%, 51% of leases < 2,500 SF.
- **interpretation**: Mode-A passes cleanly. Tenant diversification is unambiguously strong — no single tenant exposure risk, broad industry mix (legal 19.7%, FS 16.8%, RE 13.3%, healthcare 9.9%). The 2026/2027 expiration cliff is disclosed in standard format. With current Leased Rate 80.9% > Occupancy Rate 77.5%, signed-not-commenced (~422k SF) provides some backfill. Submarket rent gap (current $47.65 PSF vs at-expiration $53.68 PSF in 2026 leases) suggests mark-to-market potential rather than mark-to-market loss.

#### [DEI-06-impairment-absence]  severity = **UNVERIFIABLE**
- **R**: 'Impairment losses — —' for FY2025 and FY2024 (10-K NOI reconciliation table); accumulated deficit grew to -$1,539,710 thousand by Q1 2026 (10-Q balance sheet).
- **f**: Mode-B: given Gross Investment in Real Estate $12.86B, accumulated D&A $4.13B, net carrying value $8.73B — and given office sector cap-rate expansion has been brutal (peer impairment cycles 2023-2025) — derive whether absence of impairments is credible.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K NOI reconciliation + 10-K Schedule III)`
- **M_value**: Zero impairment in FY25, FY24, FY23 (per NOI reconciliation). Schedule III shows several wholly-owned properties with encumbrances exceeding book value (e.g. The Landmark Los Angeles $240M encumbrance vs $331M total carrying value but $0 land cost — entirely in 'Cost Capitalized Subsequent'). Sherman Oaks Galleria carries $300M encumbrance on $476.9M gross / $263.5M net carrying value. Construction loan at Wilshire Bundy has 80%+ LTV implied. Accumulated deficit $1.54B is a function of historic GAAP losses & dividends-in-excess-of-earnings, not necessarily an impairment trigger.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B candidate. Zero impairments on $8.7B net real estate during a brutal office cycle (peers SLG, VNO, BXP, KRC have all recorded impairments 2023-2025) is suspicious. However, DEI's portfolio is highly concentrated in 'premier' Westside LA submarkets that have held up better than CBD office, and impairment under ASC 360 is binary (undiscounted-cash-flow test) so even significant fair-value declines don't trigger writedowns if held-for-use. Without third-party appraisal data (cap-rate observable transactions in DEI submarkets), I cannot adjudicate this Mode-A. Flag as UNVERIFIABLE — not a SEVERE call but a watch item.

#### [DEI-07-form4-insider-conviction]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: 'We had seven holders of record of our common stock on February 13, 2026.' — incidental; the substantive insider-trading signal is in the 26 Form 4 filings filed Jan 2024 - May 2026.
- **f**: Form 4 m-source: parse non-derivative transaction codes for all reporting persons since Jan 2024; classify code P (open-market purchase) vs code S (open-market sale) vs code A (grant).
- **M_source**: `form4 (xml parsing of accession 0001378405-26-000003 + 0001993146-25-000003 + 0001993146-25-000003 + 0001993146-24-XXX; n=29 form4/4-A filings pre-cutoff)`
- **M_value**: Code-P open-market PURCHASES: 4 transactions, $2.08M aggregate. CEO Jordan Kaplan bought 98,000 sh @ $10.18 on 2026-02-13 ($998k, 3 days post Q4 earnings). EVP/GC Michele Aronson bought 42,126 sh @ ~$11.70 on 2025-11-14 and 2025-11-17 ($492k, week after Q3 earnings). Director William Simon Jr bought 45,000 sh @ $13.15 on 2024-06-10 ($592k). Code-S open-market SALES: ZERO. Code-A grants: 26 LTIP grants on 2024-12-12 & 2025-12-15 (routine annual NEO/director awards, all derivative LTIP units).
- **interpretation**: Form 4 evidence STRONGLY supports management commitment. Zero discretionary sales by any insider in 18 months — extraordinary for a public company with >30 reporting persons and ongoing LTIP vesting. CEO Kaplan's $998k open-market buy at $10.18 (near 52-wk low, three trading days after publicly releasing Q4-2025 results = blackout window open) is the highest-quality bullish signal in this framework. C-suite open-market purchases of nearly $1.5M total into the -28.8% drawdown, executed in post-earnings open windows (not blackout-evading insider trades), is the EXACT OPPOSITE of the IONQ/distress-pattern this framework was designed to catch. Per AGENT_PROMPT explicit guidance: 'note this explicitly even if the rest of the disclosure is distressed.'

#### [DEI-08-share-repurchase-absence]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 'Repurchases of Equity Securities None.' (10-K Item 5) — no buyback in FY25 despite 28.8% drawdown. Last buyback was 2023: 9.1M shares at $12.03 avg.
- **f**: Mode-B: GIVEN insider buying at $10.18 is signaled as undervalued AND there is $357M cash, why is the company not buying back stock?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 5 + cash flow statement)`
- **M_value**: FY23 repurchase $109.1M / 9.1M shares (avg $12.03). FY24 zero. FY25 zero. Q1 2026 zero. Cash $357M but $1.18B of 2026 debt maturities; the company is preserving cash for refi liquidity, not capital return. Dividends of $127M/yr consume most of FCF.
- **interpretation**: Mild tension with DEI-07: if stock is cheap enough for CEO personal buying at $10.18, why not corporate buying? Answer (Mode-B inference): $1.18B 2026 maturity wall + Fannie/CMBS lenders penalize aggressive corporate buybacks. The capital-allocation choice is rational. Note that the 2023 buyback was at $12.03 — current ~$10 is cheaper. The gap between insider personal action (buying) and corporate action (preserving liquidity) is information-consistent: insiders see undervaluation but the entity needs balance-sheet defense. Mild negative on disclosure (not a SEVERE call).

## FMC — FMC Corporation
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.55
**Sector**: Basic Materials  |  **Industry**: Agricultural Inputs
**Filing**: 0000037785-26-000041_10K.txt  (2026-02-27)

### Summary
FMC presents a mixed honesty-alpha profile. Strong POSITIVE signals: (1) three independent directors made open-market PURCHASES totaling ~$482K at $13.60-13.83 post-dividend-cut and post-10-K, with Director Michael F. Barry's $250K buy five days after the FY25 10-K being the strongest single Form-4 tell; (2) clean insider-timing (all buys are post-disclosure, zero pre-disclosure selling, only mechanical code-F vest withholdings); (3) honest, comprehensive disclosure of the $1.36B goodwill writedown, India $522M held-for-sale charges, and the 86% dividend cut. NEGATIVE signals (Mode B catches): (1) TWO covenant amendments in four months with the second one suspending leverage testing entirely Q1-Q3 2026 AND moving revolver to secured — the strongest single bear signal; (2) Q1'26 deterioration (revenue -4%, net loss -$281M) contradicts the FY25 10-K's stabilization narrative; (3) Rynaxypyr post-patent transition under-quantified; (4) absent CEO/CFO personal buying despite director conviction. Verdict: LONG-tier (honesty-alpha framework rewards transparent distress with insider buy-side validation), but smaller position size than ABG due to (a) escalating covenant pressure, (b) no operator-level insider buying, and (c) Q1'26 trajectory.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [FMC-01-insider-purchases-code-P]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied management commitment to balance-sheet repair and post-patent Rynaxypyr strategy following a 69.7% drawdown, $1.36B goodwill writedown, dividend cut to $0.08, and two consecutive covenant amendments.
- **f**: Mode B — parse Form 4 XML for code-P (open-market purchases) by named directors/officers across the drawdown window (Oct 2025 to cutoff 2026-05-25).
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse)`
- **M_value**: Three independent directors executed code-P open-market PURCHASES into the drawdown: (1) Director Michael F. Barry bought 18,072.191 shares on 2026-03-04 at $13.83 = $250,058 — five days after the FY25 10-K disclosed the $1.36B goodwill writedown, India $522M charges, the 86% dividend cut, and Amendment No. 5 to the credit facility; (2) Director John Mitchell Raines bought 7,000 shares on 2025-11-13 at $13.77 = $96,390 — about two weeks after the Q3'25 print and after the Oct 2025 dividend cut to $0.08; (3) Director Carol Anthony Davidson bought 10,000 shares on 2025-11-05 at $13.5957 = $135,957 — same window. Aggregate director open-market purchases since the dividend cut: ~$482,405 across three independent directors at prices $13.60-13.83. ZERO open-market purchases by CEO Brondeau, CFO Sandifer, or any C-suite officer. Insider sales in the same window: only code-F (tax withholding on vest) at $14.62 (2026-02-23) and $15.56 (2026-04-28). No code-S (discretionary sales) by officers since Scanlan EVP-HR $128K sale on 2025-09-29 at $34 — long before the worst news. Officer/director equity awards (code A) granted at fair value (FY25 PSU vesting and 2026 PSU grants Feb 19, 2026 priced at $14.62-15.56 — restricted to vest 2027-2029).
- **interpretation**: MIXED but tilted POSITIVE — three independent directors stepped up with material personal cash ($250K, $136K, $96K) AT levels $13.60-13.83, post-cut, post-impairment, post-covenant-amendment. That is honest insider conviction. However, the ABSENCE of any code-P purchase by CEO Brondeau (68 years old, also Chairman) or any C-suite officer is the negative tell — the people closest to the operating reality have NOT put personal cash on the line. This pattern (directors yes, operators no) is the classic 'governance-aware capital allocation' signal, not the 'operators see undervaluation' signal that ABG and similar names show. Net: moderate positive, not a slam-dunk LONG tape.

#### [FMC-02-insider-vs-calendar]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider transactions timed independently of capital events / non-public information.
- **f**: Cross-reference Form 4 transaction dates against the 8-K calendar (capital events, earnings, divestitures, covenant amendments) for 2025-2026.
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: Capital event calendar: dividend reduced to $0.08 announced October 2025 (8-K 2025-10-29); Q3'25 earnings 8-K 2025-10-29; Subordinated Notes 8.45% 2055 issued May 27, 2025 ($750M); Amendment No. 5 to revolver (leverage cap → 6.00x) executed Dec 2025; FY25 10-K filed 2026-02-27 with $1.36B goodwill writedown disclosure; Amendment No. 6 to revolver (leverage testing suspended Q1-Q3 2026, cap → 6.75 thereafter, security/guarantors added) executed April 16, 2026 — disclosed in Q1'26 10-Q filed April 30, 2026. Davidson buy 2025-11-05 and Raines buy 2025-11-13 are 7-15 days AFTER the Q3 earnings 8-K (Oct 29) and the dividend-cut announcement — i.e., after material info was public, not before. Barry buy 2026-03-04 is 5 days after the FY25 10-K (Feb 27) — also after disclosure, not before. CRITICALLY: no insider sales of any kind in 30-day windows before either the Q3'25 print, the Q4'25/FY25 print, or the April 2026 Amendment No. 6. The only sales are code-F tax-withholding on PSU vests on 2026-02-23 (price $14.62), which are mechanical, not discretionary.
- **interpretation**: Insider timing is CLEAN. Buys post-bad-news, not pre. No clustered selling ahead of either covenant amendment, the dividend cut, or the goodwill writedown. This contradicts a 'liar' or 'pump-and-dump' pattern. The drawdown looks like genuine business deterioration disclosed honestly, not concealed.

#### [FMC-03-goodwill-writedown-magnitude]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "We recorded a $1,356.2 million write-off of our remaining goodwill balance in connection with the impairment test." (Item 7 + Note 4)
- **f**: Mode A — verify the writedown was fully recognized; Mode B — assess whether the remaining-goodwill phrasing signals zero residual cushion for future impairment.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 7 + Note 4 + cash flow recon)`
- **M_value**: Confirmed: $1,356.2M goodwill writedown taken in Q4 2025 — 100% of remaining goodwill. Combined with $522M India held-for-sale write-down and $271.5M restructuring charges, total non-cash charges = ~$2.15B in 2025. Net loss attributable to stockholders $2,238.9M (vs net income $341.1M in FY24). Adjusted EBITDA $842.7M (down 7%) — implies pre-impairment business is still cash-generative. Revenue $3,467.4M down 18% (down 8% ex-India). Cash $584.5M at YE25 (up from $357.3M YE24) but $577.3M is foreign-subsidiary cash, requiring repatriation planning. The writedown size relative to enterprise value at cutoff is enormous (~50% of pre-drawdown market cap).
- **interpretation**: Honesty signal is clean — the writedown is FULL and final (no remaining goodwill to be impaired later). But the magnitude and the disclosure that the trigger was 'significant decrease in our stock price during the fourth quarter' means the auditor forced the writedown after the market repriced — i.e., management did NOT proactively recognize the impairment, the market did. The business itself is severely impaired: Rynaxypyr (formerly the moat) is now post-patent, India business is being sold at writedown values, and management's response (Project Foundation + Project Focus) is yielding only $165M in cost benefits against $2B+ in charges.

#### [FMC-04-covenant-amendments-escalating]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "In December 2025, the Company amended its credit agreement to provide additional financial flexibility given current market challenges." (Note 12, FY25 10-K)
- **f**: Mode B — given Amendment No. 5 in Dec 2025, look for evidence of further amendments needed before cutoff.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 2026 10-Q Note on Credit Facility)`
- **M_value**: Amendment No. 5 (Dec 2025): max leverage ratio raised to 6.00x through Sep 30, 2026, stepping down to 3.75x by Q4 2028; min interest coverage lowered to 2.00x through Jun 30, 2027. Dividend increase restrictions added. FOUR MONTHS LATER, Amendment No. 6 (April 16, 2026, disclosed in Q1'26 10-Q): leverage ratio test SUSPENDED for Q1-Q3 2026, then capped at 6.75x through Dec 2027, stepping down to 3.75x by Q1 2029. Min interest coverage lowered to 2.00x through Jun 30, 2027 stepping to 3.50x by Q1 2029. CRITICALLY, the April 2026 Amendment also added subsidiary guarantors and granted security interests — i.e., the revolver went from unsecured to SECURED. This is a meaningful credit-quality step down.
- **interpretation**: TWO covenant amendments in four months, with the second one MORE LENIENT than the first and also requiring SECURITY/GUARANTORS, signals the Dec 2025 amendment was inadequate. The company had to come back to lenders within one quarter and ask for: (a) complete suspension of leverage testing for three quarters, (b) a higher long-run cap (6.75x vs 6.00x), and (c) collateral. This is the strongest single Mode-B catch — the issuer's Dec 2025 narrative implied 'we have addressed this', but Q1'26 reality required another, more invasive amendment. RED-FLAG_NEGATIVE-adjacent. Combined with the $586M of 2026 debt maturities (per FY25 10-K maturity table — $1.4 and $1.6 unamortized + $586M maturing within one year), the balance-sheet pressure is real and accelerating.

#### [FMC-05-dividend-cut-86pct]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "In light of the challenges that the company is facing and to further prioritize debt reduction, the Board of Directors in October 2025 made the decision to reduce the quarterly dividend to $0.08 per share." (FY25 10-K)
- **f**: Mode A — verify the magnitude and prior level; Mode B — assess whether the cut was sized to reflect distress.
- **M_source**: `self_text + dividend history`
- **M_value**: Prior quarterly dividend was $0.58/share (since 2024); reduced to $0.08/share starting January 2026 — a 86% cut. Annualized savings ~$250M ($2.00/share x ~125M sh outstanding). January 2026 dividend payment totaled $10.0M (vs ~$72.5M at the prior rate). This dividend was uninterrupted since 2006. Combined with the new credit-agreement restriction on dividend increases (Amendment No. 5), this is effectively a deep cut with no near-term path to restoration.
- **interpretation**: Disclosure is honest and proportionate, but the SEVERITY of the cut (86%) — bigger than the typical 50% "signaling cut" — implies management/board see this as a multi-year balance-sheet problem, not a one-year cyclical dip. The fact that the cut was decided in October 2025 (before the goodwill writedown was even taken) suggests management was already braced for the worst. Consistent with the covenant-amendment pattern: management is in defensive mode. Note: a dividend cut combined with covenant relief combined with insider PURCHASES (directors only) is the classic 'kitchen-sink reset' setup, which historically has positive forward returns IF the underlying business stabilizes.

#### [FMC-06-rynaxypyr-post-patent-cliff]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Our 2026 operational priorities... include... managing our post-patent Rynaxypyr active strategy." (FY25 10-K) and "lower pricing to diamide partners, pricing actions on branded Rynaxypyr active" (Q1'26 10-Q).
- **f**: Mode B — verify that Rynaxypyr generic competition is now reflected in price and volume; check whether the pricing actions are voluntary or forced.
- **M_source**: `self_text (FY25 10-K + Q1'26 10-Q MD&A)`
- **M_value**: Rynaxypyr (chlorantraniliprole) was FMC's largest single product, ~$1.5B+ revenue at peak — protected by composition-of-matter patents that began expiring 2022-2026 across jurisdictions. FY25 10-K language is reactive ("managing" post-patent) rather than offensive. Q1'26 10-Q confirms: revenue down 4% YoY to $758.6M driven by "lower pricing to diamide partners" (i.e., FMC sells active to licensees Syngenta, Sumitomo, ISK at lower prices because generics are now in market) AND "pricing actions on branded Rynaxypyr active" (i.e., FMC is cutting its own branded price to defend share). Volume was actually up YoY in EMEA/NA — i.e., the issue is PRICE, not VOLUME, which is the diagnostic of generic entry. Mode B catch: management does NOT quantify what % of revenue is now post-patent vs still-protected, nor what the run-rate Rynaxypyr revenue is post-generic.
- **interpretation**: The structural issue is acknowledged but under-quantified. Investors cannot model post-patent ARR for the largest molecule without disclosure of (a) jurisdiction-by-jurisdiction patent expiry, (b) generic share already taken, (c) license-fee step-down from Syngenta/Sumitomo. This is a Mode-B disclosure gap that matters more than the 10-K admits, but it is consistent with industry norm (Corteva and Bayer also obscure their molecule-level exposure). New active ingredients in the pipeline (Isoflex, fluindapyr, Dodhylex, rimisoxafen) are mentioned but not quantified.

#### [FMC-07-india-divestiture-execution]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "In July 2025, the Board of Directors approved a plan to divest the Company's commercial business in India... The sale process is underway and is expected to conclude in 2026." (FY25 10-K)
- **f**: Mode A — verify the divestiture is being executed; Mode B — assess whether the write-down values imply a forced sale.
- **M_source**: `self_text (FY25 10-K + Q1'26 10-Q held-for-sale disclosures)`
- **M_value**: Held-for-sale accounting triggered Q3 2025. $522M of one-time charges and write-downs taken in 2025 to position the India business for sale, including $521.7M classified under 'India held for sale business' in the adjusted EBITDA reconciliation. Q1'26 10-Q: sale process still ongoing, no buyer announced as of filing date 2026-04-30. The company plans to retain a supply agreement post-sale (i.e., keep manufacturing in India, sell only the commercial/distribution business). 10-K says India was ~10% of revenue in 2024.
- **interpretation**: Disclosure is honest about the size of write-downs but does NOT quantify the expected sale proceeds or net cash impact. A 10-month sale process with no buyer announced (as of cutoff) for an emerging-market commercial business written down by $500M+ is consistent with a difficult, possibly distressed sale. Mode B gap: management does not disclose the bid range or buyer universe. Material because the proceeds are earmarked for debt paydown — a delayed/lower sale undermines the deleveraging narrative.

#### [FMC-08-q1-2026-deterioration]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Revenue of $758.6 million for the three months ended March 31, 2026 decreased $32.8 million, or approximately 4 percent." (Q1'26 10-Q)
- **f**: Cross-check whether Q1'26 results confirm or contradict the FY25 10-K's '2026 priorities' framing of stabilization.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1'26 10-Q MD&A)`
- **M_value**: Q1'26: revenue $758.6M (-4% YoY), net loss attributable $281.3M (vs net loss $15.5M Q1'25) = a $265.8M deterioration. Increase driven by $112.1M tax-valuation-allowance charge (Switzerland) + $59.2M increase in restructuring/other charges. Underlying operating performance is masked by these items, but the trajectory is negative. Q1 is seasonally the largest quarter for FMC (NH spring application season) so a 4% decline + tax charges + restructuring is a poor leading indicator.
- **interpretation**: Q1'26 does NOT confirm the '2026 stabilization' framing from the 10-K. Revenue is still declining, and the company is taking more restructuring charges plus a non-cash but signaling tax-valuation-allowance increase (a Swiss valuation allowance typically signals doubt about future taxable profits in that jurisdiction — a forward-looking negative). The April Amendment No. 6 was likely triggered by Q1 deterioration.

#### [FMC-09-ceo-tenure-and-succession]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: CEO Pierre R. Brondeau (age 68) listed as "Chief Executive Officer (24-present, 10-20); Chairman of the Board (10-present); Executive Chairman of the Board (20-21)" — i.e., he was re-instated as CEO in 2024.
- **f**: Mode B — given the 'CEO transition' restructuring charges in 2024 and the back-to-back distress signals, assess governance posture.
- **M_source**: `self_text (FY25 10-K executive officer section + restructuring note)`
- **M_value**: Mark Douglas was the prior CEO; Brondeau returned as CEO in 2024 (after serving as CEO 2010-2020, then Executive Chairman 2020-21). 2024 restructuring includes "$55.8 million in severance and employee separation charges, including costs associated with the CEO transition." Brondeau is now 68 years old and combines CEO + Chairman roles. The FY25 10-K does NOT disclose a succession plan, search process, or named president/COO successor. Q1'26 10-Q confirms Brondeau still in role. CFO Andrew Sandifer is also relatively long-tenured.
- **interpretation**: Mode B disclosure gap: a 68-year-old founder-style CEO recalled to fix the business, no named successor, and a combined Chairman+CEO role at a company in covenant distress is a governance concern. Not fraud — but limits the pipeline of forward credibility if Brondeau's recovery plan does not deliver in 2026. The director buys ($482K aggregate) without ANY CEO/CFO open-market buying suggests even the board is hedging — endorsing the company but not yet endorsing the operating team's confidence.

## GO — Grocery Outlet Holding Corp.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: n/a
**Sector**: Consumer Defensive  |  **Industry**: Grocery Stores
**Filing**: 0001771515-26-000015_10K.txt  (2026-03-04)

### Summary
GO is the ANTI-CALM: a textbook ABG-pattern positive insider signal stacked against a genuinely impaired and litigated business. Six named insiders - CEO Potter ($2.4M), Lead Director Ragatz ($2.8M), Chairman Lindberg ($1.6M, reversing prior selling), and 3 other directors - clustered 15 open-market code-P purchases for $7.92M in the 21-day window after the Mar 4, 2026 10-K and Mar 2 Optimization Plan adoption, at $5.72-$7.06/sh into a 57% drawdown. Total trailing-24mo sell:buy dollar ratio is near-parity at 1.08, with the sales dominated by pre-Potter Lindberg 10b5-1 plan disposals and recent Mar 9 RSU tax-withholding (not discretionary). Operating cash flow remained positive ($52.6M Q1 FY26) and leverage is well within covenants (~1.67x vs 3.25x cap). Offsetting negatives are real: cumulative $307M of 2-quarter goodwill impairments expose the FY25 charge as a partial step-down, a NEW Mar 16, 2026 class action targets current management's restructuring narrative, Optimization Plan costs were revised upward 43%, $15.5M IO bad-debt write-off signals model stress, and comparable store sales turned -1.0% on a smaller post-closure denominator. VERDICT: LONG-tier. The insider Form-4 evidence is the strongest signal under the framework and directly contradicts the distressed price action; the impairment/restructuring/litigation overhang is largely backward-looking and quantified, while the forward setup is a new CEO + refreshed board + cash-flow-positive operating engine + aligned insider conviction at the bottom.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [GO-01-insider-cluster-purchases-into-collapse]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: management/board have ordinary risk preferences and no asymmetric conviction in the post-impairment plan; insider transactions should be neutral or modestly net-selling around large impairment + restructuring announcements.
- **f**: Mode B - parse Form 4 XML for ALL non-derivative transactions for trailing 24 months pre-cutoff; isolate open-market PURCHASES (code P) by named insiders by date and dollar size; cluster vs the Mar 2-4, 2026 Optimization Plan adoption + 10-K filing dates; compare against the Liberato/Cavanaugh class action coverage period (Nov 2023-Oct 2024) to test whether buying is pre- or post-litigation-overhang clarification.
- **M_source**: `form4 (EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse, 91 Form-4 filings, 110 transactions) + insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K calendar)`
- **M_value**: MASSIVE coordinated insider open-market buying cluster in the two weeks AFTER the Mar 4 10-K filing and Mar 2 Optimization Plan adoption: between Mar 6 and Mar 27, 2026, 15 separate code-P transactions purchased an aggregate 1,299,905 shares for ~$7.92M at prices ranging $5.72-$7.06 per share - deep into the 57% drawdown. Breakdown by buyer: (1) Erik D. Ragatz (Lead Independent Director) bought 324,000 sh across 5 days for $2.82M (the largest buyer); (2) Jason J. N. Potter (President & CEO, hired Feb 3, 2025) bought 398,905 sh across 3 days for $2.41M; (3) Eric J. Lindberg, Jr. (Chairman, former CEO, third-generation family) bought 275,000 sh for $1.64M on Mar 9; (4) Jeffrey York (Director) bought 120,000 sh for $698K on Mar 9; (5) Carey F. Jaros (Director) bought 40,000 sh for $250K on Mar 10; (6) John E. Bachman (Director) bought 16,000 sh for $103K on Mar 6. This is the ABG-pattern - operating-management + board scaling in during weakness AFTER bad news disclosed. Earlier Code-P buys (Nov 2024-May 2025) totaled an additional $4.93M, mainly Ragatz ($2.30M in Nov 2024-May 2025) plus Alterman ($297K), MILLER (CFO, $117K), Jaros ($57K), Bachman ($159K). Total Code-P 24mo: $12.86M. Total Code-S 24mo: $13.83M - sell:buy ratio is essentially 1.08:1 (NEAR PARITY). Code-S sales are dominated by Lindberg ($11.77M in 2024-Q1 2025 at $13-$19, well before the deepest leg), all under pre-existing 10b5-1 plans tied to historical option exercises (code-M conversions visible Aug-Sep 2024). The Mar 9, 2026 code-S sales by Potter, Miller, Wilson, Thompson, and Bortner totaling ~$499K all occurred on the same date as RSU vesting (preceded by code-A grants) and are tax-withholding sales, NOT discretionary selling. Notably: Lindberg, who had been the dominant seller throughout 2024, RE-ENTERED on the buy side ($1.64M Mar 9, 2026) - a generational signaling reversal.
- **interpretation**: This is the framework's TEXTBOOK honesty-alpha POSITIVE signal - the opposite of the CALM pattern. CEO Potter (new operator hired Feb 2025) and Lead Independent Director Ragatz are putting $2.4M and $2.8M of personal capital to work in open-market purchases at $5.72-$7.06 within days of the 10-K filing and after a $158M Q1 goodwill impairment was already locked in. Lindberg (the prior dominant seller and family principal) reversed direction. Six different insiders coordinated buying in a 21-day window post-disclosure. The framework reads this as informed-insider POSITIVE-conviction evidence: insiders have access to the post-Optimization-Plan operating outlook and put real capital to work at the bottom. This is not a passing PASS - it is an actively confirmatory POSITIVE signal that should partially offset other distressed signals.

#### [GO-02-goodwill-impairment-cadence]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY2025 (filed Mar 4, 2026): 'During the fourth quarter of fiscal 2025, we performed our annual impairment evaluation of goodwill, which indicated that the fair value of the Company was lower than its carrying value, resulting in the recognition of a non-cash impairment charge of $149.0 million during fiscal 2025.'
- **f**: Mode B - given an FY2025 Q4 goodwill impairment of $149M was already booked, verify whether the issuer's framing (one-time non-cash charge tied to Q4 stock price) is supported by the Q1 FY26 follow-through. A second impairment in the immediately subsequent quarter would falsify the 'one-time event' narrative.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q Note 3 Goodwill + Q1 FY26 MD&A Recent Trends section, filed May 13, 2026)`
- **M_value**: Q1 FY26 10-Q discloses ANOTHER $158.0M goodwill impairment recognized in Q1 FY26, this time tied to 'a triggering event ... as a result of a decline in our stock price, necessitating an interim goodwill impairment evaluation' performed as of Apr 4, 2026. Combined cumulative 2-quarter goodwill impairment: $149M + $158M = $307M. Goodwill balance dropped from $633.8M (Jan 3, 2026) to $475.8M (Apr 4, 2026) after the Q1 FY26 charge alone; cumulative impairment from acquisitions (mainly the Apr 2024 United Grocery Outlet/Bargain Barn acquisition for which the Company paid $62M, but goodwill carry stretched higher post-business combination accounting) is materially understated by the 10-K's framing.
- **interpretation**: The 10-K's annual-cycle 'fourth quarter goodwill impairment' framing implies normalized cyclical review, but the Q1 FY26 follow-on impairment 70 days later proves the goodwill was still over-valued at Jan 3, 2026. Mode B catch: the 10-K's note on the FY25 charge does NOT alert readers that a triggering event was likely already crystallizing into Q1 FY26 (the stock had collapsed further by Mar 4, 2026 filing date). This is a SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY because the issuer recognized $158M MORE impairment in the very next quarter - the FY25 charge was insufficient. Note that current goodwill of $475.8M still carries acquisition premium from the UGO purchase that the issuer continues to defend; risk of an additional impairment in FY26 is non-trivial if comparable store sales remain negative.

#### [GO-03-optimization-plan-cost-escalation]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 8-K filed Mar 4, 2026 (Item 2.05): 'The Company estimates that it will incur between $14 million and $25 million in net total restructuring charges in fiscal 2026 related to the Optimization Plan ... bad debt expense of between $11 million and $14 million and cash expenses of between $2 million and $3 million ... net restructuring charges for the Lease Exits of between $1 million and $8 million.'
- **f**: Verify the issuer's restructuring cost estimate against the Q1 FY26 actual run-rate and the updated estimate range in the Q1 FY26 10-Q (filed 10 weeks later).
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q Note 12 + MD&A Recent Trends), cross-referenced against 8-K filed Mar 4, 2026`
- **M_value**: Q1 FY26 10-Q UPDATED estimate: 'between $20 million and $27 million in net total restructuring charges in fiscal 2026 and fiscal 2027' - the range expanded both at the low end (from $14M to $20M, +43%) and the period (now through Q1 FY27, not just FY26). Bad debt expense actually incurred Q1: $15.5M (already exceeds the upper end of the $11-14M Mar 4 estimate). The cash cost range for Lease Exits also widened to $49-60M (previously stated as $49-60M cash, $1-8M net charge - now confirmed at $49-60M cash). Optimization Plan was sized at 36 stores in Mar 4 8-K, 27 closed in Q1 FY26 plus 9 more in Q2 FY26. Q1 actual restructuring charge: $18.2M, already at 91% of low-end of MAR estimate.
- **interpretation**: 10 weeks after the initial cost estimate, the range was revised upward and extended in time. Bad debt actually incurred in Q1 alone exceeded the entire FY26 estimate range. Not severe because the issuer transparently updated the estimate in the Q1 10-Q and the dollar magnitudes are not solvency-threatening (~$25M on a $4.7B sales base), but the framework's signaling concern is that the initial Optimization Plan cost was understated and the actual operational execution risk is higher than originally framed.

#### [GO-04-secondary-class-action-emerging]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY2025 Note 13 Commitments and Contingencies discusses only the Jan/Mar 2025 Liberato/Cavanaugh class actions and two stockholder derivative lawsuits - all related to the Aug 2023 ERP implementation period. Filed Mar 4, 2026.
- **f**: Mode B - given the existing class action is still pending (motion to dismiss hearing Mar 19, 2026), verify whether the stock-price collapse + Optimization Plan + Q1 impairment have triggered a NEW class action specifically targeting current management.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q Note 9 Commitments and Contingencies, filed May 13, 2026)`
- **M_value**: On March 16, 2026 - 12 days after the 10-K was filed - a SECOND federal securities class action lawsuit was filed in N.D. Cal against the Company and 'certain of its CURRENT officers' (the 2026 Class Action). Allegations: defendants violated federal securities laws by 'making materially false and misleading statements and/or failing to disclose material adverse facts regarding the Company's growth strategy, financial performance, and the effectiveness of its restructuring plan.' This is a NEW litigation overhang specifically targeting the Potter/Miller management team and the FY2025 Restructuring Plan / FY2026 Optimization Plan disclosures - exactly the topics framed positively in the 10-K. Plus two additional stockholder derivative actions filed Mar 20 and Mar 26, 2026 in Delaware (one already dismissed without prejudice Apr 17, 2026, one still pending in Chancery), plus a fourth derivative on Apr 13, 2026 in N.D. Cal against current directors and officers. None of this litigation was foreseeable from the 10-K's Note 13 - the 10-K was filed Mar 4, 2026 and the new wave of lawsuits started 12 days later.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch on the litigation pipeline. The 10-K reasonably disclosed the 2025-vintage class action (pre-Potter era, ERP-related), but a new class action and 3 new derivative suits materialized within 6 weeks of the 10-K filing, this time targeting current management's restructuring claims. The 'effectiveness of restructuring plan' allegation is corroborated by the Optimization Plan being expanded (Mode B catch on GO-03). The legal exposure is real and the prior class action is still at motion-to-dismiss stage with $0 accrued. This is SEVERE because it is a fresh, specific, fact-pattern litigation against current management that was effectively pre-conditioned by the disclosures the issuer made in the 10-K.

#### [GO-05-CEO-transition-and-strategy-pivot]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Item 1A Risk Factor: 'In fiscal 2024 and fiscal 2025, we completed thorough and external searches for several new executive officers, including a new Chief Financial Officer and a new President and Chief Executive Officer. We utilized interim officers to serve in such capacities for a period of time as well.' Employment Agreement of Jason Potter effective Feb 3, 2025 (Exhibit 10.17).
- **f**: Verify the magnitude of leadership churn and the strategic discontinuity it represents (3rd-generation family CEO Lindberg -> Interim Lindberg -> External CEO Potter); cross-reference with the timing of major impairments and restructuring charges.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Risk Factors and Exhibit list) + 10-K Note on Related Party Transactions`
- **M_value**: Leadership sequence: (1) Eric J. Lindberg, Jr. (third-generation family principal, CEO since 2006) departed CEO role; (2) Lindberg returned as Interim President and CEO for portions of fiscal 2024 and Q1 fiscal 2025 - confirming a CEO vacancy in late 2024 / early 2025; (3) Jason Potter (external, ex-The Fresh Market) became President and CEO effective Feb 3, 2025; (4) Christopher M. Miller (CFO) joined; (5) Multiple new EVPs (Chief Operations Officer Chikkala, Chief Stores Officer Burke) onboarded in 2024-2025. The Optimization Plan was adopted Mar 2, 2026 - 13 months after Potter's start, consistent with a new-CEO 'kitchen sink' strategic reset.  Related party leases: GO continues to lease 6 store locations from Lindberg-family entities ($X.X million in lease payments per 10-K Item 13 / proxy reference).
- **interpretation**: The CEO transition + ERP fallout + restructuring sequencing is honestly disclosed in the 10-K but the cumulative governance risk is under-quantified. The 13-month gap between Potter's hire and the Optimization Plan suggests strategic indecision - and the $149M FY25 + $158M Q1 FY26 goodwill impairments are concentrated in the post-Potter period when an outside CEO would be expected to undertake exactly this kind of strategic write-down. Mode B catch: the framework reads the impairment cadence as Potter taking a 'kitchen sink' charge to clear the deck, which is consistent with the March 2026 CEO+director buying cluster (GO-01). Moderate not severe because this fact pattern is well-disclosed and arguably bullish for forward execution.

#### [GO-06-comparable-store-sales-trajectory]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY2025: 'Comparable store sales increased by 0.5% in fiscal 2025 on a 52-week basis, driven by a 1.6% increase in the number of transactions partially offset by a 1.1% decrease in average transaction size.'
- **f**: Verify the comp trajectory against Q1 FY26 actuals.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q MD&A) cross-referenced with 10-K MD&A`
- **M_value**: Q1 FY26 comparable store sales DECLINED 1.0% (driven by 3.1% decrease in average transaction size, partially offset by a 2.1% increase in number of transactions). Note also that comp store sales calculation EXCLUDES the 36 stores closed under the Optimization Plan (newly added exclusion starting Q1 FY26) - meaning the reported -1.0% is the BETTER set of stores. Including closed stores, the comp would be materially worse. Transaction-size compression (-3.1%) is accelerating from FY25's -1.1%; SNAP/EBT exposure (9% of net sales in FY25) was hit by the Q4 FY25 U.S. government shutdown.
- **interpretation**: 10-K framed FY25 +0.5% comp as a stabilization story; Q1 FY26 -1.0% on a smaller (post-closure) store base confirms the underlying weakness is worse than presented, with transaction-size deceleration tripling vs FY25. The exclusion of closed stores from the comp denominator is technically defensible but understates the headline. SNAP exposure is the key macro hook. Moderate because the data is honestly disclosed in the Q1 10-Q.

#### [GO-07-credit-facility-and-leverage-buildup]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: 10-K FY2025: '$273.8 million was outstanding [on senior term loan] as of January 3, 2026 ... $220.0 million was outstanding [on revolver] in aggregate as of January 3, 2026 ... in compliance with all applicable financial covenant requirements.' Covenants: Total Net Leverage Ratio maximum 3.25 to 1.00 after Dec 31, 2025; Consolidated Interest Coverage Ratio minimum 1.75 to 1.00.
- **f**: Verify covenant compliance trajectory at cutoff and quantify cushion erosion.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 6 Long-term Debt + Q1 FY26 10-Q Note 6) + cash-flow trajectory`
- **M_value**: Total funded debt: $493.8M (term $273.8M + revolver $220.0M) at Jan 3, 2026. Adjusted EBITDA FY25 = $254.3M, so Total Net Leverage Ratio approximately = ($493.8M - $69.6M cash) / $254.3M = 1.67x - well within the 3.25x cap, with substantial cushion (~$378M of EBITDA-equivalent headroom). Q1 FY26 Adjusted EBITDA $43.1M vs $51.9M Q1 FY25 (-17% Y/Y); Adjusted net income $4.6M vs $13.0M (-65% Y/Y) - adjusted figures still positive but compressing. Revolver borrowed in FY25 net +$30M, suggesting working-capital draw to fund Optimization Plan cash outflows. Q1 FY26 cash and equivalents: $59.0M (down from $69.6M at Jan 3, 2026). Q1 FY26 operating cash flow +$52.6M was solid.
- **interpretation**: Leverage is real (~1.7x on adjusted EBITDA, $493.8M of total debt) but well-cushioned vs the 3.25x covenant. Operating cash flow remained positive ($52.6M Q1 FY26) despite the $180M GAAP net loss (most of the loss is non-cash goodwill impairment). The 2023 Credit Agreement matures Feb 2028 - refinancing risk exists, but not acute. Liquidity is adequate: $59M cash + $174.9M revolver capacity = ~$234M total liquidity at Q1 FY26. Net cash provided by operating activities was $52.6M in Q1 FY26 - the operating business is cash-flow positive once impairment is backed out. This supports the insiders' purchasing thesis (GO-01).

#### [GO-08-IO-receivables-impairment-as-business-model-stress]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K and Q1 FY26 10-Q: Independent Operator (IO) model - 529 of 570 stores at Jan 3, 2026 operated by IOs; IO commissions are 50% of store gross profit; IOs typically fund initial capital via IO Notes from the Company.
- **f**: Mode B - given the IO model is the issuer's competitive moat, quantify the bad-debt expense on IO Notes / receivables that occurred in connection with the Optimization Plan as a leading indicator of IO-cohort stress.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q Note 12 Optimization Plan + Note 1 estimated credit losses on amounts advanced to IOs)`
- **M_value**: Q1 FY26 bad-debt expense in connection with Operator Agreement Terminations under the Optimization Plan: $15.5M increase to the provision for IO notes and IO receivables reserves - meaning IOs at the 36 closure stores plus 'certain other store locations' had outstanding loans/receivables to the Company that the Company now expects to write off. This is a leading indicator that 36+ stores' IOs were financially under-water, and the Company is bearing the credit loss. Future Optimization Plan estimate: bad debt expense $16M for FY26 + an additional ~$0M-$3M cash IO-termination expense.  Comparing to 529 active IO stores, 36 closures = ~6.8% of the IO base in one cohort wave.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch - the IO model is celebrated in the Business section as a competitive moat but the IO-credit exposure is under-quantified in the 10-K. ~7% of the IO base is being terminated with associated bad-debt write-offs of ~$15-16M, which is the first hard signal that the IO model has structural underperformers requiring the parent to bear loan losses. If macro conditions worsen (SNAP cuts, comp store decline accelerating), more IO terminations are possible. Moderate not severe because the Optimization Plan is bounded (36 stores) and the financial magnitude is manageable on a $4.7B sales base.

#### [GO-09-ERP-disruption-tail]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K and Q1 FY26 10-Q: 'We implemented system upgrades in August 2023, and as a result, we and our IOs experienced significant disruptions to our ordering, inventory planning and management and payment processing during the remainder of fiscal 2023 through fiscal 2024 and into fiscal 2025.'

Q1 FY26 10-Q repeats nearly identical language. The ERP system was implemented Aug 2023; the issuer has said the disruption persisted through FY25 (29+ months later) and the residual remediation continues into FY26. The Liberato/Cavanaugh class actions allege misleading statements about the ERP transition for Nov 2023 - Oct 2024 - i.e., ~14 months of allegedly misleading disclosures. The reservation that 'we continue to work' on tools and visibility implies the remediation is NOT fully complete at Q1 FY26 - 30+ months post-implementation.
- **interpretation**: ERP disruption is the originating root cause of the entire FY24-FY26 stress arc (impairments, restructurings, class actions, comp decline). The issuer's framing presents the disruption as substantially behind it, but Q1 FY26 language ('continue to work to further improve') reveals continuing remediation. Mode B catch: the framework reads this as a partially under-disclosed ongoing operational risk.

## KMPR — Kemper Corporation
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.48
**Sector**: Financial Services  |  **Industry**: Insurance - Specialty Personal Auto / Life
**Filing**: 0000860748-26-000014_10K.txt  (2026-02-11)

### Summary
KMPR is a -52.8% drawdown specialty-auto insurer where the FORENSIC tape produces a MIXED but tilting-honest verdict. STRONG long-bias signal: 8 open-market code-P purchases by 6 distinct insiders ($1.50M, P:S 86:1 in the drawdown window), including a 25,000-share director Parker buy at $36.82 in Nov 2025 (during interim-CEO vacuum, after he sold at $62 in Aug 2024) and a 5-insider Aug 7 2025 cluster buy after the Q2 print. CEO Lacher's exit was forced (without-cause, $5.72M severance) but he sold ZERO open-market in 12 months and retained 167K shares; no clustered pre-bad-news insider selling. SEVERE operational underdelivery: Q1 2026 Specialty P&C combined ratio jumped to 106.7% from 92.7%, segment Adjusted NOI collapsed from $97.9M to $0.1M, consolidated swung to a $1.7M loss vs $99.7M income. The 10-K's framing of reserve pressure as a commercial-auto/bodily-injury issue understated breadth — Q1 2026 reveals California personal-auto severity is now the dominant headwind. Auditor (Deloitte) clean, no going-concern, ICFR effective, no restatements, no SC 4.02 — honest distress, not accounting fraud. Mode-B gap: no insider buying since Q1 2026 print, despite drawdown deepening — insiders' mid-2025 conviction has NOT been reasserted at lower prices. Capital position adequate (RBC headroom, $2.6B equity, $944M debt) but $250M revolver reduction on May 4 narrows flexibility. Verdict: NEUTRAL with mild LONG tilt. The insider buying through November 2025 is the strongest Signal OS countersignal in this name, but the absence post-Q1 print prevents a full LONG-tier rating; the underwriting deterioration is real and severe; honesty grade is HIGH. Severity: 3 PASS / 4 MODERATE / 2 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 0 UNVERIFIABLE.

### R/f/M tuples (9)

#### [KMPR-01-insider-purchases-cluster]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied management/board confidence after Q2 2025 print and through CEO transition: no explicit issuer claim, but standard governance positioning during a leadership void.
- **f**: Parse Form 4 XML for code-P (open-market PURCHASES) across the 12-month window pre-cutoff; cross-reference with capital events.
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse, n=99 Form 4 filings, n=136 non-deriv txns)`
- **M_value**: Eight code-P open-market purchases by SIX distinct insiders, totaling $1,501,380 since 2025-01-01. Director Stuart B. Parker bought 25,000 shares @ $36.82 on 2025-11-10 ($920,500, +120% to his prior holdings — 20,717 -> 48,152 net) DURING the interim-CEO period after Lacher's Oct 14 departure. On 2025-08-07 (one trading day after the Q2 print + $500M new buyback authorization), a coordinated cluster of FIVE insiders bought open-market: directors Laderman ($148K @ $49.37), Canida ($98K @ $48.96), Gorevic ($98K @ $49.14), EVP Kemper Life Flint ($50K @ $49.90/$50.33), all 2,000-3,000 share sized. On 2025-06-10, directors Paracchini ($31K @ $61.71) and Laderman ($156K @ $62.24) bought. Code-S sales over the same 12-month window: only $17,469 (Director McKinney 504 sh @ $34.66 in Feb 2026 — likely a Rule 10b5-1 trickle). The earlier 2024-08-21 Parker sale of 30,000 sh @ $61.74 ($1.85M) is OUTSIDE the drawdown window and was reversed: Parker REBOUGHT 25,000 sh at $36.82 (40% below his sale price) in Nov 2025. P:S ratio in drawdown window = 86:1.
- **interpretation**: STRONG honest-insider tape. Multiple directors and one named EVP put personal cash into open-market purchases AFTER the Q2 print revealed deterioration AND AFTER the Lacher CEO ouster. A 25,000-share Parker buy at $36.82 is an unusually sized director purchase that would only make sense if the director believed intrinsic value is meaningfully above the post-drawdown price. The cluster on 2025-08-07 (5 insiders same day) suggests Board members felt the stock was attractive at $49 post-Q2. The fact that Parker scaled UP into the drawdown (Aug 2024 sold at $62, Nov 2025 bought back 25k at $37) is the cleanest forensic counter-signal to a SHORT thesis. None of the named C-suite (CEO Lacher, current Interim CEO Evans, CFO Camden, CIO Boschelli, EVP Hunton) sold open-market in 12 months; Lacher's only liquidation event was a Sep 10 2025 option exercise where 87% of shares were withheld for taxes — he still held 167K shares at departure.

#### [KMPR-02-insider-vs-calendar-timing]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider transactions timed without information asymmetry.
- **f**: Cross-reference Form 4 transaction dates against the 8-K calendar (CEO ouster, $500M buyback auth, ASR, retention awards, earnings prints, reserve development disclosures).
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: Capital-event calendar: 2025-08-05 Q2 print + $500M new buyback authorization + dividend declared (8-K); 2025-08-13 $150M ASR with GS (8-K); 2025-10-14 CEO Lacher ousted (8-K filed 10-15); 2025-10-22 Chief Claims Officer Sanders departed (8-K 10-24); 2025-11-04 retention awards for CFO Camden ($775K), CIO Boschelli ($675K), EVP Hunton ($775K) (8-K 11-10); 2025-12-16 Lacher separation finalized $5.72M severance. Insider P transactions: Jun 10 + Aug 7 (two days AFTER public Q2 print, NOT within an undisclosed-info window) + Nov 10 (after Q3 print on Nov 5, after Lacher ouster, after retention-award announcement). All P buys are clustered AFTER public bad-news disclosure — i.e., insiders absorbing weakness in the open window, not selling ahead of it. No code-S transactions in any 30-day pre-earnings window. Parker's only sale (Aug 2024) was 12 months before the drawdown peak and predated the CEO ouster by 14 months. CEO Lacher's Sep 10 2025 option exercise was timed 35 days before his Oct 14 termination — but the F-tranche (85,608 sh) was MANDATORY tax withholding, not voluntary selling, and he retained 167,345 shares (worth ~$5M at cutoff) on his way out.
- **interpretation**: Timing is CLEAN. Buys clustered AFTER bad news, not before. The most natural lying-detector signal — clustered insider SELLING ahead of CEO ouster or ahead of Q1'26 reserve blowup — is absent. Combined with the Mode-A buying tuple, this raises confidence the drawdown is overdone in the market's view but not in insiders' view.

#### [KMPR-03-specialty-pc-Q1-collapse]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K narrative (filed 2026-02-11): "Adjusted Consolidated Net Operating Income $225.5M" for FY2025 vs $381.5M FY2024, attributed to "evolving loss patterns and higher defense costs associated with attorney-represented bodily injury coverages" in commercial auto.
- **f**: Verify whether disclosed loss-cost pressures have abated or worsened by Q1 2026; compute combined ratio change.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 2026 10-Q MD&A Specialty P&C — combined ratio table + segment NOI)`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 Specialty P&C combined ratio 106.7% vs 92.7% Q1 2025 — a 14-point deterioration that puts the segment UNDERWRITING AT A LOSS. Underlying combined ratio 106.2% vs 92.2% (current-year non-cat ratio 84.0% vs 70.9%). Specialty P&C Adjusted NOI collapsed to $0.1M vs $97.9M in Q1 2025 (-$97.8M). Earned premiums down $77M YoY in segment due to lower personal-auto volumes AND a $28.0M Florida Statutory Profit Limit Refund. Cause: "higher claim severity and frequency primarily related to bodily injury and property damage coverages in California." Adverse prior-year reserve development $3.6M in Q1'26 vs $0.7M Q1'25. Consolidated Q1'26 net loss $(1.7)M attributable to Kemper vs net income $99.7M (-$101.4M YoY). For FY2025 the comparable adverse PYD was $76.0M (vs $29.8M FY2024). So the loss-cost pressure DISCLOSED in the 10-K as bodily-injury-in-commercial-auto has MIGRATED to CALIFORNIA PERSONAL AUTO and is the new dominant headwind.
- **interpretation**: The 10-K disclosure framed reserve pressure as a Commercial Auto / bodily-injury issue. The Q1 print proves the underwriting deterioration is BROADER than disclosed and now reaches the core Specialty Personal Auto book (89% of premiums) via California severity/frequency. A combined ratio of 106.7% in the company's main segment is an operating event that justifies most of the -52.8% drawdown. The 10-K narrative was not deceptive — it disclosed the loss-cost pressure honestly — but it understated the geographic and product breadth. Underwriting losses are the operational reason for the drawdown; this is correctly punished by the market.

- **R**: 8-K 2025-10-15: "Joseph P. Lacher, Jr. will depart from his role as President and Chief Executive Officer of the Company, effective October 14, 2025... C. Thomas Evans, Jr., age 66, was appointed by the Board as interim President and Chief Executive Officer."

Capital adequacy is NOT a near-term concern (RBC headroom, $944M debt vs $2.6B equity, $1.25B goodwill intact, no covenant trips), but the financial flexibility is narrower than 12 months ago.

This is the Mode-B GAP: the insider-purchase tape from June-November 2025 is NOT repeating in 2026 after Q1's underwriting collapse. Even Parker (the $920K Nov 2025 buyer) did NOT add. The implication: insiders thought the stock was attractive at $36-50 in mid-2025 (when the issue was 'reserve correction for commercial auto'), but they have NOT scaled in at the lower current prices when the issue is 'California personal-auto underwriting at 106% combined.' This is a meaningful absence-of-signal.

Three possible readings: (1) blackout windows constrain timing (May 8 was just 1 day after Q1 8-K and 2 days after annual meeting — director quiet-period typically extends 24-48 hours post-earnings, then opens); (2) insiders also surprised by Q1 California severity and are re-underwriting their conviction; (3) Parker's Nov 2025 buy was idiosyncratic, not a board-wide signal. The PRESENCE of strong insider buying through 2025 is still a POSITIVE Signal OS finding, but the ABSENCE post-Q1 should temper the LONG-tier conviction.

### Summary
MNRO is a -28.8% drawdown forensic CONTRADICTION. The fundamentals are honestly impaired (three consecutive years of declining sales, FY25 GAAP net loss, 145-store closure, $14.8M closure charge, two consecutive covenant-relief amendments, $18.5M of AlixPartners turnaround fees, $736M goodwill exceeding total equity, dividends paid out of retained earnings) but the FORM-4 TAPE is the strongest LONG-bias signal in the forward-test universe: Carl Icahn deployed $84.7M open-market over Nov 4-7, 2025 to build a ~14.8% stake at avg $19.08, the company's NEW (March 2025) CEO Peter Fitzsimmons used $502K of personal cash to buy at $19 in Feb 2026 immediately after his permanent-CEO appointment, and the board responded to Icahn by adopting a 17.5%-trigger Rights Plan calibrated 2.7 pp above his then-current stake. ZERO open-market insider sales in the 12-month window — the four May 15, 2026 Form 4s clustering ten days before cutoff are all code-F tax-withholding on routine RSU vests (carefully disambiguated). Q3FY26 shows the first positive comparable-store-sales print (+1.2%) and operating income +87% YoY ($18.6M), validating the turnaround thesis Icahn is buying. Mode-B catches: (i) Rights Plan framed as generic shareholder protection is in fact a targeted Icahn pill; (ii) recurring covenant relief is chronic distress; (iii) $18.5M AlixPartners fee is material cash burn the 10-K does not foreground; (iv) goodwill carrying value at $736M against shareholders' equity of $605M is conditional on turnaround success. Verdict: LONG-tier. Insider conviction (Icahn $84.7M + CEO $502K + zero sells) plus a real Q3 fundamental inflection outweighs the entrenchment overhang. Severity: 4 PASS / 5 MODERATE / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 0 UNVERIFIABLE.

However, the issuer has not been forced to cut the dividend (kept at $0.28 for both Q3 and Q4 FY26), management asserts compliance, and the facility was right-sized ($600M → $500M). Mode B observation: the dividend persistence is itself a covenant-stress signal because the Fifth Amendment specifically modifies the dividend cushion (0.50x → 0.25x), meaning even lenders concede dividend headroom was binding.

Operating income FY25 $12.6M vs FY24 $71.4M (implied) — operating income collapsed -82.4% YoY per 10-K language. FY25 net LOSS $5.2M (diluted EPS -$0.22) vs FY24 net income $37.6M. Adjusted EPS FY25 $0.48 vs FY24 $1.33 (-64%). Comp store sales: FY25 -5.3% (unadjusted) / -3.5% (selling-day adjusted) — also accelerated from FY24.

The reality is three consecutive years of accelerating decline ending in a GAAP net loss. This is the empirical basis for the -28.8% drawdown and Icahn's interest. Not red-flag (issuer not hiding anything) but materially impaired.

#### [MNRO-06-goodwill-vs-impairment-pattern]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note: 'We recorded $20.8 million in store impairment costs in fiscal 2025... goodwill balance was $736 million as of March 29, 2025.' Issuer states qualitative goodwill assessment determined no impairment.
- **f**: Mode B — given $736M of goodwill against $605M of total equity (Q3 FY26 balance) plus an entity that produced net loss in FY25, assess whether the goodwill carrying value is at risk.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note + Q3 10-Q balance sheet)`
- **M_value**: Goodwill $736.4M (unchanged Q3 FY26). Shareholders' equity $604.9M Q3 FY26 — i.e., goodwill EXCEEDS total equity by $131M. Tangible book value is materially negative. FY25 store-level impairment $24.4M (already taken on long-lived/operating-lease assets, NOT goodwill). FY25 net loss, FY24 net income only $37.6M. Issuer ran qualitative goodwill test and determined fair value of reporting unit (one operating segment) exceeded carrying value — but the discounted-cash-flow test is identified as a 'critical audit matter' in PwC opinion. With 145 stores already closed and 9-month FY26 operating income of $25M (annualized run-rate ~$33M, well below pre-COVID), an EBITDA multiple ~10x supports ~$330M of enterprise value attributable to goodwill — risk-adjusted, goodwill could be impaired by $200-400M in a downside case.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: issuer discloses goodwill is the largest asset and that the impairment test is a critical audit matter, but does NOT disclose how close the headroom is. A future goodwill impairment of $100-400M is plausible if turnaround stalls — it would NOT be a cash event, but would wipe out tangible book and could trigger sentiment selling. Investors should treat the $736M as conditional. Not severe yet because management has been honest about the test and PwC signed off.

In connection with Mr. Fitzsimmons' appointment, the Company also entered into a consulting agreement with AlixPartners, LLP.'
