Sign-on bonus $200K + $500K restricted stock (one-year vest) for forfeited prior-employer compensation. Mode B insider-vs-calendar: Fitzsimmons' Form 4 PURCHASES on 2/3/2026 + 2/5/2026 happened within ONE WEEK of the Jan 28 Q3 print AND only ~60 days after he signed his permanent employment agreement — i.e., immediately after his lock-up assumptions reset, he bought.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: $18.5M to AlixPartners over 8 months is a very large turnaround consulting bill (~1.5% of revenue, ~75% of FY25 operating income). This is appropriate for the situation (145-store closure, covenant amendment, activist arrival) but represents real cash burn that the 10-K does not foreground. Offsetting: the Fitzsimmons Form-4 buys post-permanence are a strong commitment signal — a turnaround consultant typically EXITS once the assignment is done; he chose to stay and put personal cash in. Mild negative on the cost line; modest positive on incentive alignment.

#### [MNRO-08-dividend-persistence]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**

Yield at ~$17 cutoff price = $1.12 / $17 = 6.6%. Class C Preferred holders also receive equivalent dividend (~$337K / qtr).

The 6.6% yield is part of the LONG case. Not severe — Q3 trajectory (op income +87% YoY) suggests earnings will catch up to dividend run-rate, and Icahn would be aligned with continued payments (he holds 4.4M shares = ~$5M/yr personal income). Watch for dividend cut as a future negative trigger if Q4 doesn't continue improving.

Categories driving Q3 comp gain: front end/shocks, tires (per MD&A). These are core (non-discretionary) auto-service categories. 9-month operating income $25.2M of which $18.6M came in Q3 alone — i.e., Q3 contributed 74% of YTD operating income.

Combined with the AlixPartners-led cost program (SG&A Q3 $83.8M vs $94.8M YoY = -11.6%), Q3 has the math signature of a turnaround that is actually working. #### [MNRO-10-form4-may-2026-not-bearish]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Cluster of four Form 4s filed May 15, 2026 — ten days before cutoff — could suggest pre-bad-news executive selling.

- **M_value**: All four May 15, 2026 Form 4s (Mulholland EVP, D'Ambrosia EVP+CFO, Donovan Sr.VP-CIO, Hawryschuk VP-Fin/Ops) report ONLY code-F transactions on 5/9/2026 ($17.72) and 5/12/2026 ($16.16). Code F = 'Payment of exercise price or tax liability by delivering or withholding securities incident to the receipt, exercise or vesting of a security issued in accordance with Rule 16b-3'. NOT a discretionary sale. Sizes 116-760 shares per insider = tax slugs on May annual RSU vest. The Form 4s are also amendments/Section 16 catch-ups covering the multi-year history of identical May/June/July RSU vest events. No code-S, no code-D voluntary disposition.
- **interpretation**: Critical Mode-A disambiguation. A naive reader of the EDGAR filing index would see 'four executives filed Form 4s ten days before the cutoff' as bearish clustering. The XML parse rules this out completely — every transaction is code F (tax-withholding on a routine RSU vest cycle that has been occurring at the same May/Jun/Jul cadence since 2022). No open-market sells anywhere in the 12-month window from C-suite. This INCREASES confidence in the Icahn+CEO bullish read.

Wilkins Jr. — 370 sh @ $27.95 = $10,343 on 2025-11-26; (6) Director Ann D. Rhoads — 750 sh @ $28.27 = $21,206 on 2025-12-10. Item 9B explicitly states no 10b5-1 trading arrangement was adopted in Q4 2025 — these are DISCRETIONARY. Two officer/director sales ENTIRE pre-cutoff window (Widder 2025-11 $112K; Buechler 2023-02 $729K). Sale-to-purchase ratio ex-Carlyle = $0.84M / $1.61M = 0.52x (purchases EXCEED sales). Carlyle Group dumped $447M across 104 sales but ALL in 2024 (lock-up release / 10%-holder distribution, not informed-insider behavior).
- **interpretation**: Strongest Mode-B BUY signal in the forward-test cohort to date. CEO Blaser bought 34,040 shares totaling $750K personally — including a $502K purchase 5 days before Q3 earnings (a deliberate signal during a blackout-opening) and a $249K follow-on 6 days after Q4 earnings. CFO Busky added $204K. Director Strobeck (a former hedge-fund founder per public bio) put on $240K in a single open-market trade. SIX named insiders bought in a 92-day window with zero officer/director SALES. This is the canonical 'insider-conviction-at-trough' pattern Signal-OS expects to see in a true distressed turnaround. Inverse of the MOH pattern (CEO sold $28M pre-guidance-cut); aligned with the ABG pattern (CEO bought $1M into drawdown).

#### [QDEL-02-bristol-county-securities-class-action]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note 18 Commitments and Contingencies: 'In April 2024, a purported stockholder of the Company filed a putative class action complaint... captioned Bristol County Retirement System v. QuidelOrtho Corporation, et al.... asserts claims for violations of Sections 10(b) and 20(a) of the Exchange Act and Rule 10b-5 promulgated thereunder related to statements regarding sales of the Company's COVID-19 diagnostic tests and the 510(k) submission for its SAVANNA RVP4 assay.'
- **f**: Mode A: confirm the filing exists. Mode B: cross-reference the SAVANNA RVP4 narrative against the actual 2025 outcome (June 2025 SAVANNA development discontinuation).
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 18) + edgar_fts (Bristol County docket ref) + claim_evolution (SAVANNA RVP4 → SAVANNA Exit)`
- **M_value**: Three suits pending: (1) Bristol County (securities class action, April 2024); (2) Whitfield derivative (4/25/2024); (3) Pinkney derivative (6/21/2024). Defendants filed motion to dismiss April 4, 2025 — still pending as of 10-K date. The Bristol County complaint targets SAVANNA RVP4 510(k) submission statements; in June 2025 the company DISCONTINUED SAVANNA platform development entirely after 'results of the SAVANNA RVP4X clinical trial' — i.e., the trial outcome was bad enough to kill the entire platform. $149.7M asset impairment / write-off in 2025 was recorded as SAVANNA Exit charge. The discontinuation post-dates the class period and directly validates the plaintiffs' core theory (the SAVANNA submission/development was problematic). No loss accrual disclosed.
- **interpretation**: The litigation existence is honestly disclosed. The MODE-B finding is that the SAVANNA exit itself ($149.7M write-off, plan to discontinue announced 9 months after Bristol County was filed) corroborates the substance of the plaintiffs' allegation. This is the SYMPTOM-confirms-claim pattern. Counter-weighting: management discontinued cleanly, took the charge, and the new CEO Blaser (who joined post-issue) was not named in the Bristol County complaint — the named executives are 'three of its current and former executives,' meaning the issues predate current leadership. This is litigation risk but does NOT indict the current C-suite executing the turnaround.

#### [QDEL-03-goodwill-impairment-trail]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K MD&A: 'During fiscal years ended 2025 and 2024, we recognized non-cash goodwill impairment charges of $700.7 million and $1.8 billion, respectively.' Q1 2026 10-Q balance sheet shows Goodwill = $0.
- **f**: Mode A — quantify the cumulative goodwill destruction relative to the original Ortho Clinical Diagnostics acquisition price; cross-check against any management commentary on capital-allocation discipline.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 8 Goodwill + balance sheet trail)`
- **M_value**: Goodwill trajectory: FY23 ~$2.47B → FY24 $649.5M (after $1.8B impairment) → FY25 $0 (after $700.7M impairment). TOTAL goodwill impairment over two fiscal years = $2.52B. Combined with $56.9M (FY24) + $9.7M (FY25) asset impairments + $149.7M SAVANNA Exit asset write-off = ~$2.74B in non-cash impairment across two years. The Ortho Combinations closed in May 2022 at a transaction value of ~$6B (the merger that created QuidelOrtho). FY24 net loss $2,052M and FY25 net loss $1,131.8M are essentially fully explained by impairments, but the underlying business: FY25 revenue $2,651.9M vs FY24 $2,594.9M ex-COVID degradation; Adj EBITDA FY25 (segment sum approx $807M North America + 51.7+85.6+74.7+62.8 = roughly $1,082M before corporate -$534M = ~$548M underlying).
- **interpretation**: Honest disclosure (the impairments are taken plainly) but the magnitude is the SYMPTOM. The 2022 Ortho merger has been written down to zero goodwill — i.e., the entire premium paid in that deal is gone. This is a capital-allocation indictment of the prior leadership team. Current CEO Blaser joined as President-CEO in May 2024 (per signature pages); his open-market purchases post-date the bulk of the impairment trail, which is consistent with him pricing the residual business as undervalued at $21-24 after the impairments are 'in the rear view.' Honesty-alpha framework note: the bad news is detected because management honestly disclosed it — this is NOT alpha by itself; the alpha is in the Mode-B Form 4 conviction signal (QDEL-01) given the impairments are sunk.

#### [QDEL-04-savanna-platform-discontinuation]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K: 'in June 2025, we announced plans to discontinue our SAVANNA platform development as a result of several factors, including the results of the SAVANNA RVP4X clinical trial.' SAVANNA Exit charges in 2025: Asset impairments/write-off $149.7M + Employee terminations $1.7M + Other costs $12.7M = $164.1M.
- **f**: Mode B — given SAVANNA was the company's flagship next-gen molecular platform and the subject of the Bristol County securities suit, look for evidence of how aggressively the company is pivoting away vs. preserving optionality.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 6 Restructuring + Q1 10-Q + claim_evolution: SAVANNA pipeline history)`
- **M_value**: Total Optimization Plan pre-tax charges $100M through 2027; net cost savings ~$50M; SAVANNA Exit substantially complete by 1H 2027. Q1 2026 restructuring/integration/other charges only $4.4M (vs $16.1M Q1'25) — pace of restructuring decelerating, suggesting the bulk of the SAVANNA write-down hit in FY25. The discontinuation freed engineering and capex to redirect to existing platforms (SOFIA, VITROS, IB Manager). Q1 2026 revenue ex-Grifols (joint business terminated Jan 1 2026) shows continued underlying weakness but the SAVANNA distraction is removed.
- **interpretation**: Strategic reset, taken decisively. The SAVANNA write-down is the largest single piece of bad news in the FY25 disclosure but it's a one-time event and management has fully reset the cost base around it. Mode-B catch: the Bristol County complaint specifically pointed at SAVANNA RVP4 510(k) representations, and the trial results that triggered discontinuation align with plaintiffs' thesis. But for forward economics, the SAVANNA exit is largely behind — operating cash flow in 2025 was still $105M despite the write-offs, and the company managed to refinance $3.4B of debt in Aug 2025.

#### [QDEL-05-credit-refinancing-and-covenant-headroom]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note 9 Borrowings: 'On August 21, 2025, we entered into the Credit Agreement... Pursuant to the Credit Agreement, the Lenders provided us with (i) a $1.15 billion Term Loan A, (ii) a $100.0 million DDTL Term Loan A, (iii) a $1.45 billion Term Loan B and (iv) a $700.0 million Revolving Credit Facility.' 'We were in compliance with the financial covenants as of December 28, 2025.' Q1 2026 10-Q: 'We were in compliance with the financial covenants as of March 29, 2026.'
- **f**: Mode B — given the new Credit Agreement was struck while the company was mid-impairment-cycle, characterize covenant levels and the relationship to current EBITDA. Compare to the MOH covenant-relaxation pattern.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 9 + Q1 10-Q Note 7)`
- **M_value**: Covenants struck Aug 21, 2025: max Consolidated Leverage Ratio = 4.50x (years 1-3) stepping to 4.25x; min Consolidated Interest Coverage Ratio = 3.00x. UNCHANGED from prior covenant levels — NO covenant relaxation between FY25 and Q1 2026. Term Loan A bears 6.87% effective; Term Loan B at 8.43%. Total drawn debt approximately $2.78B; revolver availability $596.6M; cash $169.8M = approximately $766M total liquidity. FY25 Adjusted EBITDA segment-level North America $807M + other segments roughly $275M = ~$1,082M, minus corporate ~$534M = ~$548M consolidated Adj EBITDA. Net leverage proxy: $2.78B drawn - $169.8M cash = $2.61B / $548M EBITDA = ~4.77x. This is ABOVE the 4.50x covenant on a simple calc, suggesting Credit Agreement EBITDA definition includes addbacks (restructuring, integration, impairments) that bring covenant leverage down — the company says they ARE compliant. Q1 2026 interest expense $51.1M annualized $204M = ICR ~$548M/$204M = 2.69x — BELOW the 3.00x covenant on a simple calc. Again, the covenant calc almost certainly adds back items, but headroom is thin.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B catch but DIFFERENT from MOH: QDEL did NOT have to negotiate a covenant relaxation. They struck a new Credit Agreement on standard 4.50x / 3.00x terms in Aug 2025 (during the deep impairment cycle) and they remain in compliance through Q1 2026. Simple-math leverage is tight (~4.8x gross), implying the covenant calc relies on EBITDA addbacks — but the lenders accepted the credit at standard terms. This is NEUTRAL evidence: not a tailwind, but not a red flag either. Contrast: MOH had to drop ICR from 3.0 to 1.75 just days before the 10-K. QDEL's covenants held at 3.0x ICR / 4.5x leverage and management asserts compliance both at fiscal year-end and Q1 2026.

This is a CLEAN remediation cycle — a known prior issue that was addressed within one fiscal year.

This removes one of the standard distressed-issuer concerns. Combined with the new credit agreement holding standard covenants, the SAVANNA exit being a one-time event, and the heavy insider buying, the operational risk profile is materially better than the headline -66.5% drawdown would suggest.

#### [QDEL-07-q1-2026-revenue-deterioration]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Q1 2026 10-Q MD&A: 'Total revenues were $619.8 million for the three months ended March 29, 2026, compared to $692.8 million for the three months ended March 30, 2025.' Operating loss $31.8M Q1'26 vs operating income $32.6M Q1'25. Net loss widened to $91.8M from $12.7M. Adjusted EBITDA $242.3M vs $309.7M (-22%).
- **f**: Mode A — verify the Q1 2026 deterioration is driven by transitory items (Grifols Joint Business termination, weak respiratory season) vs. structural.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 10-Q income statement + Outlook + Note 3 Revenue)`
- **M_value**: Revenue drop drivers per management: (1) Grifols Joint Business terminated Jan 1 2026 — $15.9M Q1'25 pre-tax profit eliminated; (2) Respiratory weak — 'influenza-like illness visits down by approximately 30%' per CDC vs Q1'25 (lower SOFIA SARS / QUICKVUE volume); (3) China — Jiangxi Volume-Based Procurement program announced Jan 2026 on dry chemistry test strips, no impact yet but pricing-pressure risk; (4) NA segment EBITDA fell from $234.3M to $169.4M (-28%). Q1 typically the strongest respiratory quarter, so a weak Q1 sets a difficult full-year setup. Customer concentration unchanged at 11% one customer in NA segment. Interest expense $51.1M Q1'26 vs $40.0M Q1'25 (+28%) reflecting higher rates on refinanced debt.
- **interpretation**: Real fundamental deterioration in Q1 2026. The respiratory weakness is exogenous (CDC data on flu season) but the Grifols termination is structural — recurring profit gone permanently. Interest expense up sharply post-refinancing. However: (i) Q1 is seasonally strongest for respiratory, so it understates non-respiratory trends; (ii) the EMEA segment grew (+4% revenue, +22% Adj EBITDA), Latin America +20%, Other +5% — geographic diversification is helping; (iii) the operating loss is small ($31.8M) relative to FY25 op income ~$166M ex-impairments. Insider buyers (Blaser, Busky, Strobeck) made their purchases BEFORE the Q1 print, so they were not reacting to a Q1 beat — they bought into the known weakness.

## SCVL — SHOE CARNIVAL, INC.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.7
**Sector**: Consumer Cyclical  |  **Industry**: Apparel Retail / Family Footwear
**Filing**: 0001193125-26-126279_10K.txt  (2026-03-26)

### Summary
SCVL is a -34% drawdown family-footwear retailer with a STRUCTURALLY SOUND balance sheet (21 consecutive years debt-free, $130M cash, current ratio 3.8, 12 consecutive years of dividend increases including a 13.3% bump in March 2026, 56 consecutive quarters paid) and a STRATEGICALLY VOLATILE operating record (Shoe Station rebanner pivot launched March 2025, board-unanimous name change to Shoe Station Group in November 2025, CEO Worden terminated 'without cause' February 2026 with $4.8M cash + 168K share severance, strategic review reversal May 2026 — both banners now declared permanent and independent). The KEY signal is the Form 4 tape: in 12mo, the cohort showed ONE open-market PURCHASE (EVP-CFO Kerry Jackson, code P, $500K @ $16.13 on 2026-04-02, 5 trading days after the 10-K filing and BEFORE the Q1 FY26 GAAP loss/impairment disclosure he had foreknowledge of) and ONE trivial sale (Director Guthrie $50K). Net 12mo open-market flow = +$450K NET BUYING. 24mo extends this: Chairman Weaver and wife each bought 285,500 sh @ $33.91 = $9.68M apiece on 2024-12-06 (combined $19.4M). The Weavers' quarterly gifts of 166,666 sh each are wealth-transfer/charitable structures (zero-price transactions), NOT divestment. Aggregate 24mo named-officer/founder open-market flow: +$19.86M buying vs $50K trivial selling — i.e., insiders are NET BUYERS into the drawdown. This is the OPPOSITE of the typical distressed-name insider pattern (EAT had $33M net selling into its -25% drawdown, CBRL had absence of buying with activist pressure). FY26 guidance ($1.40-1.60 Adj EPS, $1.125-1.147B sales) implies continued YoY EPS declines but the Q1 print ($0.23 Adj EPS) hit the implied run-rate. Severity: 4 PASS / 0 UNVERIFIABLE / 5 MODERATE / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG. The moderates capture (i) strategy reversal credibility cost, (ii) 4-yr consecutive comp-sales declines now into Yr 5, (iii) CEO termination characterized as 'no disagreement' despite without-cause severance triggers, (iv) FY26 back-half implied still-negative YoY, (v) inventory-driven margin compression. None of the moderates rise to SEVERE because management has not lied — the disclosures are complete and the negative facts are quantified. Verdict: LONG-tier — the insider PURCHASE pattern (CFO + Chairman + wife net $19.4M buying with $50K trivial selling) into a drawdown is the single highest-information signal in the playbook, the balance sheet is unimpaired (no covenant stress, no going-concern, no maturity wall, $0 long-term debt for 21 years), and the strategic reset under a returning interim CEO with 28-year company tenure is a known-and-disclosed event whose worst-case is already priced into the -34% drawdown. The framework's role here is to NOT confuse 'distressed operating quarter' with 'lying issuer'; SCVL is the former without being the latter.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [SCVL-01-form4-cfo-open-market-purchase-into-drawdown]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: FY25 10-K (filed 2026-03-26) and Q1 FY26 8-K/press release (filed 2026-05-21) frame Sifford's interim-CEO return as a 'comprehensive review of strategic direction and capital deployment' with FY26 guidance reaffirmed (Net sales $1.125-1.147B, Adj EPS $1.40-1.60, GP margin ~34%) and the conclusion that 'Shoe Carnival and Shoe Station each serve distinct consumer segments... best positioned to operate both banners as permanent, independent components of our portfolio.' Implied claim: insider economic behavior should be consistent with this commitment narrative — particularly the named CFO Kerry Jackson (recently re-appointed to the EVP-CFO role on 2025-09-28 after 27-year prior tenure).
- **f**: Parse EDGAR ownership.xml for every Form 4 filed 2024-05-25 through 2026-05-25. Classify by transaction code (P/S/A/F/G/M). Mode B: given a -34% drawdown, mid-strategy pivot, sudden CEO termination, and impairment charges, what does the insider tape say about executive conviction?
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse, 42 Form 4 filings in 24mo / 29 transactions in 12mo)`
- **M_value**: 12mo transaction-code decomposition (29 ND tx): 14 A (RSU grants), 8 G (gifts by Weavers), 5 F (tax-withhold on vest), 1 P (purchase), 1 S (sale). ONE open-market PURCHASE: EVP-CFO W. Kerry Jackson bought 31,000 sh @ $16.13 = $500,030 on 2026-04-02, two trading days after FY25 10-K filing (3/26) and seven business days after his appointment confirmation in role. Post-transaction beneficial ownership: 215,529 sh. ONE small open-market SALE: Director Andrea R. Guthrie sold 2,477 sh @ $20.21 = $50,048 on 2025-10-15 (likely tax-related, single retail-sized lot). 24mo lookback adds two prior P's: Chairman Wayne Weaver and his wife Delores Weaver each bought 285,500 sh @ $33.91 = $9.68M apiece on 2024-12-06 (combined $19.4M; post-tx beneficial ownership 4,173,529 sh each — joint 10%+ holders). NO C-suite/director sales of any meaningful size in 12mo other than Guthrie's $50K lot. The Weavers are ALSO gifting 166,666 sh per quarter (1,333,328 sh / 12mo total) — pattern consistent with charitable/family trust planning, NOT economic divestment (no sale proceeds, no premium realization).
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch — directly contradicts a SHORT thesis. The most economically meaningful 12mo insider action is a CFO open-market PURCHASE (code P) of $500K at $16.13 — into the drawdown, into a stock that had fallen ~50%+ from its prior peak, AFTER his prior 27-year tenure made him intimately familiar with the business, and DAYS after the 10-K disclosed $24M of Fiscal 2025 rebanner losses, the CEO termination ($4.8M cash + 168K shares severance), the Worden departure, and the impending Q1 FY26 GAAP loss. This is a high-information open-market purchase by the principal accounting officer. Combined with the chairman's $19.4M purchase 16 months earlier into a similar drawdown ($33.91 entry), the 10%+ founder family is also adding, not exiting (the gifts are wealth transfers within family/charitable structures, not monetizations — note the $0 transaction prices). Asymmetry test: aggregate 12mo insider open-market dollar flow is +$500K NET BUYING vs $50K trivial sale = $450K net positive. This is the OPPOSITE pattern of typical distressed-name insider behavior (EAT had $33M net selling into its drawdown; CBRL had absence of buying). PASS — Form 4 evidence supports management commitment to the strategy.

#### [SCVL-02-insider-vs-calendar-cfo-purchase-timing]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: 10-K (3/26/2026): discloses $24.1M FY25 rebanner-related Operating Income impact ($0.66/share dilution), CEO Worden termination 2/24/2026 with $4.8M cash + 168,184 share severance (estimated $0.20-0.22/share Fiscal 2026 EPS drag), Fiscal 2026 guidance of $1.40-1.60 Adj EPS (vs $1.90 FY25 GAAP EPS), and 'strategic review' completion. Implied claim: a senior executive with full inside-information access (the CFO, designated principal accounting and principal financial officer) buying immediately post-10-K is a high-information signal about forward earnings — the CFO has signed off on the books and seen the forward forecast.
- **f**: Mode B — cross-reference the 2026-04-02 Jackson purchase against capital events: 10-K filed 2026-03-26 (T-5 business days), Q1 FY26 GAAP loss earnings release 2026-05-21 (T+34 calendar days). Is the buy clustered with the close of the 10-K blackout window? Does it occur before or after the announced impairment / strategic-review writeoffs are publicly known?
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 tx date 2026-04-02 + 10-K 2026-03-26 + Q1 FY26 8-K 2026-05-21)`
- **M_value**: Timeline: 10-K filed 2026-03-26 (Thu); typical issuer 10-K blackout window closes 1-2 trading days after release; CFO Jackson's purchase date 2026-04-02 (Thu) is 5 business days after the 10-K filing — i.e., immediately at first available trading window post-blackout. The purchase price $16.13 implies stock had already absorbed the 10-K disclosure including the $24M rebanner loss, CEO severance accruals, FY26 guidance compression, and merchandise-inventory build to $439.6M (+14% YoY). The purchase is therefore made AFTER the CFO had finalized the 10-K (signed it as principal accounting officer), AFTER FY26 guidance was set, and BEFORE the Q1 FY26 GAAP loss / $13.6M strategic-review impairment charge was publicly disclosed (announced 5/21/2026 — only the CFO sees the Q1 numbers in early April). Jackson PURCHASED with foreknowledge of an imminent GAAP loss quarter and additional impairment charges. The fact that he STILL bought, knowing this, is a strong forward-confidence signal — i.e., the impairment was front-loaded clean-up, not a deteriorating-trend leading indicator.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch with the opposite sign of typical insider_vs_calendar findings. In CBRL/EAT cases, the insider_vs_calendar signal flagged sells clustered with bullish disclosure; here it flags a BUY clustered ahead of a known-coming bad-news quarter. The CFO had visibility into Q1 FY26's $13.6M impairment + CEO severance + GAAP loss when he chose to deploy ~$500K of personal capital. Most C-suite executives who anticipate bad news sit on their hands; Jackson bought. The presumption of innocence is rebuttable — possible explanations include (a) genuine conviction in the long-run thesis post-strategic-review, (b) signaling-driven purchase to support stock price near the new low, (c) lockup/contractual cleanup requirement (no evidence of this — the filing shows direct open-market acquisition). Whatever the motive, the Form-4 narrative does not match the 'distress' read of the -34% drawdown. PASS.

#### [SCVL-03-rebanner-strategy-reversal-honesty]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K (3/26/2026): 'During Fiscal 2025, we initiated a Shoe Station rebanner growth strategy... we rebannered 101 stores, consisting of 73 Shoe Carnival stores and all 28 Rogan s stores. At the end of Fiscal 2025, Shoe Station bannered stores represented approximately 34% of our total store fleet.' '11/13/2025 8-K: Board of Directors has unanimously voted to change the Company's name to Shoe Station Group, Inc... anticipated consolidation to one banner by the end of Fiscal 2028.' Q1 FY26 PR (5/21/2026): 'Our review confirmed that the Shoe Carnival and Shoe Station banners each serve distinct consumer segments, and that the Company is best positioned to operate both banners as permanent, independent components of our portfolio.' The Fiscal 2026 rebanner plan was REDUCED from previously announced (full consolidation) to '21 stores during the first half of Fiscal 2026 while this evaluation is completed.' The proposed corporate name change to Shoe Station Group remains on the June 2026 agenda but the underlying strategy it represented has been abandoned.
- **f**: Mode A — verify the strategic-direction reversal disclosure quality. Mode B — given a strategy reversal of this magnitude within 6 months of board unanimous endorsement, what management-credibility and impairment-trigger implications follow that the issuer did NOT articulate?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K + 11/13/2025 8-K + 5/21/2026 8-K)`
- **M_value**: Fiscal 2025 rebannered 101 stores (73 SC + 28 Rogan's) into Shoe Station banner at cost of $37.1M capex + $24.1M Operating-Income impact ($0.66/sh EPS drag). Board unanimously approved name change to Shoe Station Group, Inc. on 11/13/2025 framed as 'consolidation to one banner by end of Fiscal 2028.' On 2/24/2026 — 103 days later — CEO Worden departed ('not due to any disagreement'). On 5/21/2026, new interim CEO Sifford announced the strategic review concluded with the OPPOSITE strategy: 'two permanent independent banners.' Q1 FY26 impairment of $8.3M against rebanner assets confirms the prior capex is now partly stranded. The board's 11/13/2025 'unanimous' endorsement of one-banner consolidation was reversed within 6 months. Both impairments and CEO departure are linked to the strategic pivot but management's framing ('not due to disagreement') is implausible at face value — a 27-month CEO does not depart in this fact pattern absent disagreement on strategic direction. Disclosure is technically complete (the reversal is fully disclosed) but the issuer's verbal framing minimizes the credibility cost: the company spent ~$37M of capex + $24M of operating-income + $11.9M of after-tax Q1 impairment + a CEO severance to execute a strategy it has now abandoned.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch on disclosure completeness vs framing. The actual numerical disclosure is honest — 10-K acknowledges the rebanner Operating Income drag, Q1 PR acknowledges the impairment, 11/13/2025 announcement is on file, current name-change vote remains on the June 2026 agenda. But the narrative arc — full-throated strategy launch in March 2025, board-unanimous name change in November 2025, complete reversal in May 2026 — represents a real management-credibility hit. The 'departed not due to disagreement' framing for Worden is inconsistent with the strategy reversal. Honesty-alpha read: the disclosures are clean but the underlying strategic execution is not, and the issuer chooses words to soften that fact. Note: this credibility cost is mitigated by Sifford's prior 19+ year tenure at the company (he was CEO 2012-2019) — he is not a stranger taking over a failing strategy, he is a returnee with deep institutional knowledge.

#### [SCVL-04-comparable-sales-decline-5y-trend]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K Selected Financial Data: 'Comparable stores net sales: -5.6% (FY25), -3.9% (FY24), -8.8% (FY23), -11.1% (FY22), +35.3% (FY21).' Net sales: $1,135M / $1,202M / $1,176M / $1,262M / $1,330M for FY25-FY21. Q1 FY26 press release: 'Comparable store sales declined 2.1 percent.' Management framing in 5/21/2026 PR: 'Shoe Carnival narrowed its year-over-year sales decline meaningfully compared to Fiscal 2025 trends.'
- **f**: Mode A — verify the multi-year comp-sales decline pattern. Mode B — what is the cumulative top-line erosion vs the FY21 peak, and what does the trajectory imply about a normalized-earnings denominator?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Selected Financial Data + Q1 FY26 PR)`
- **M_value**: Five-year comp sales trajectory: FY21 +35.3% / FY22 -11.1% / FY23 -8.8% / FY24 -3.9% / FY25 -5.6% / Q1 FY26 -2.1%. Net sales nominal: FY21 $1,330M peak → FY25 $1,135M (-14.7% cumulative, or -3.9% CAGR). Operating income trajectory: FY21 $207.7M → FY25 $66.8M (-67.8%). Op margin: FY21 15.6% → FY25 5.9%. Gross margin: FY21 39.6% → FY25 36.6% (~300bps compression, with FY26 guided to ~34%, another 260bps). Comparable stores net sales have been negative for FOUR consecutive years (FY22-FY25). Q1 FY26 -2.1% is a deceleration of the decline rate but not a return to positive comps. Shoe Carnival banner specifically: 'high single-digit declined' (per 10-K MD&A). Shoe Station banner specifically: '+low-single-digit' in FY25 and -3.1% in Q1 FY26 (turned negative). The 'meaningful improvement' framing in the Q1 PR is technically correct (less-bad is improvement) but masks that BOTH banners are now in negative territory.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch on framing. The data is fully disclosed (Selected Financial Data table is complete and accurate, comp sales decomposition is in the MD&A). The issuer's language — 'meaningfully' improving trend, Shoe Station 'continues to demonstrate industry-leading performance' — is technically defensible but downplays the broader truth: this is a four-consecutive-year comp-decline business now extended into year five, with both banners now negative in Q1 FY26 for the first time on record. The FY26 sales guidance of $1.125-1.147B implies flat or slightly down sales (consistent), but a normalized earnings power assessment should use Adj EPS $1.40-1.60 (FY26 guide) as a near-term anchor, not the prior $2-3 range. The drawdown to ~$13-14 area at the cutoff prices in much of this deceleration. The single-store-economics question — 'are we at trough comp run-rate or still descending?' — is not answered by management's framing.

#### [SCVL-05-dividend-12yr-streak-and-buyback]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: FY25 10-K (3/26/2026) + 12/10/2025 8-K + Q1 FY26 PR: '$50 million share repurchase program for up to $50 million of our common stock through December 31, 2026 ...quarterly cash dividend increased from $0.150 to $0.170 per share, an increase of 13.3%, for the first quarter of Fiscal 2026.' 'The new Fiscal 2026 annualized rate represents... the 12th consecutive year the Company increased its quarterly dividend rate.' '56 consecutive quarters' of dividend payment. Q1 FY26: $7M of buybacks executed (390,492 shares) + $5M of dividends — $12M returned to shareholders. $43M remaining repurchase authorization.

- **M_value**: Dividend: Q4 FY24-Q4 FY25 quarterly $0.15/sh (total $0.60/yr), raised to $0.17/sh ($0.68 annualized) for Q1 FY26. 12th consecutive annual increase, 56 consecutive quarters paid. FY25 cash dividends $16.7M; FY26 run-rate ~$18.6M ($0.68 × ~27.4M shares). Buyback: Dec 2024 program ($50M) authorized — NO repurchases in FY25 (executed zero). New $50M Dec 2025 program effective 1/1/2026 — Q1 FY26 executed 390,492 sh / $7M @ avg $17.93, leaving $43M remaining for balance of FY26. Balance sheet: $130.7M cash + marketable securities at FYE25, $0 long-term debt for 21 consecutive years, $99M available credit facility, current ratio 3.8. Cash flow from ops Q1 FY26 +$32.7M YoY improvement. Inventory $417.2M at Q1 FY26-end ($-22M vs YE FY25 already, on track for guided $50-65M reduction). The capital-return program ($25M+ annualized dividend + buyback execution potential up to $50M) against Adj EPS guidance of $1.40-1.60 (implying ~$38-44M of net income on ~27M shares) is sustainable but not lavish — the buyback execution rate will be constrained by FCF, not authorization.
- **interpretation**: Capital structure is GENUINELY strong — 21 consecutive years debt-free, 12 consecutive years dividend increases, 56 consecutive quarters paid, current ratio 3.8, $130M cash. Dividend raise of 13.3% in March 2026 (paid 4/20/2026) was a board-level confidence vote into the drawdown. The lack of buyback execution in FY25 (zero shares despite $50M authorized) was conservative — preserving liquidity through the rebanner investment — and execution started in Q1 FY26 once the strategic review concluded. This is the structural opposite of a distressed-retail name (cf. JOAN, BBBY, EXPR). PASS.

#### [SCVL-06-ceo-termination-severance-q1-loss-mechanics]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K (3/26/2026) Subsequent Events: 'On February 24, 2026, Mark J. Worden departed from his position as President and Chief Executive Officer... Mr. Worden's departure was not due to any disagreement... Payments to Mr. Worden included 168,184 shares of our common stock issued to Mr. Worden for the settlement of outstanding equity awards whose vesting accelerated upon his termination and will include a cash payment of $4.8 million. These payments, net of accruals... will have an estimated effect on Net Income per Diluted Share for Fiscal 2026 of approximately $0.20 to $0.22.' Q1 FY26 PR: 'Pretax charges of $13.6 million ($11.9 million after-tax, or $0.43 per diluted share)... comprised of $5.3 million related to the CEO Transition and $8.3 million related to the strategic review, including impairment of store locations and write-offs of rebanner-related and corporate fixed assets.'
- **f**: Mode A — reconcile the disclosed CEO severance ($4.8M cash + 168,184 shares + estimated $0.20-0.22 EPS impact) against the Q1 FY26 actual recognized charges. Mode B — does the cash + share severance suggest 'without cause' termination (typical executive employment agreement trigger)?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K subsequent events + Q1 FY26 PR + 2/25/2026 8-K Item 5.02)`
- **M_value**: Termination type: 'without cause' per the 10-K — 'we are providing Mr. Worden with the payments required to be made to him under the terms of the Employment Agreement upon a termination without cause.' 168,184 shares of accelerated vesting were issued (at the 2/24/2026 stock price ~$17-18 implies ~$3M of share value, total package ~$7.8M). $5.3M of Q1 charges relate to CEO transition (severance + accruals); separately the 10-K estimated $0.20-0.22/sh FY26 dilution, which on 27.4M shares = ~$5.5-6.0M after tax — consistent with the $5.3M pretax/$5.6M after-tax recognized in Q1 (small reconciliation difference, immaterial). The strategic-review impairment ($8.3M = $6.3M after tax = $0.23/sh) is on rebanner-related and corporate fixed assets — i.e., the capex from the FY25 rebanner program is now partly written down because the strategy reversed.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: the 'not due to any disagreement' framing in Item 5.02 is the legal-boilerplate construction, but the substantive evidence — full 'without-cause' severance triggers, $4.8M cash + 168K shares, immediate accelerated vesting, replacement by a returning prior-CEO (Sifford was CEO 2012-2019), and the subsequent strategic-direction reversal — strongly implies a board-driven decision to remove a CEO whose strategy was being abandoned. The disclosed cash + share package totals ~$7.8M for a CEO with only 27 months in seat. This is severance-on-disagreement disguised as severance-on-departure. The IMPAIRMENT framing for the rebanner writedowns is structurally honest — they identified the stranded capex and took the charge — but the cumulative cost of the abandoned strategy is ~$24M (FY25 OpInc impact) + $8.3M (Q1 impairment) + $5.3M (CEO severance) = ~$37-40M of one-time charges plus the operational damage from a 6-week store-closure window across 101 stores.

#### [SCVL-07-fy26-guidance-back-half-loaded]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Q1 FY26 PR (5/21/2026): 'We are reaffirming our previously communicated Fiscal 2026 guidance... back-to-school and fall selling periods representing the bulk of our expected annual earnings opportunity.' Original guidance from 3/26/2026: Net sales $1.125-1.147B (-1% to +1% YoY), Adj EPS $1.40-1.60, Gross profit margin ~34% (-260bps), Reductions in Adj SG&A $12-14M, Adj effective tax rate ~25%.
- **f**: Mode A — extract the Q1 FY26 Adj EPS run-rate and back-solve implied Q2-Q4 earnings to test guidance achievability. Mode B — given Q1 Adj EPS $0.23 and FY26 guide $1.40-1.60, what does the residual back-half imply about same-store-sales and margin recovery requirements?
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 PR + 10-K Subsequent Events)`
- **M_value**: Q1 FY26 Adj EPS = $0.23. FY26 guide = $1.40-1.60 = midpoint $1.50. Residual Q2-Q4 = $1.17-$1.37 (midpoint $1.27) over 3 quarters = ~$0.42 avg/quarter. Q1 FY25 comparable was $0.34. Q2 FY25 Adj EPS = ~$0.34 (back-solved from FY25 GAAP $1.90 minus reported quarters). Q3 FY25 Adj EPS = ~$0.66. Q4 FY25 Adj EPS = $0.33 (reported). So back-half FY25 totaled ~$0.99. Implied: FY26 back-three-quarters needs $1.17-$1.37 vs $1.56 in FY25 same period = a -12% to -25% YoY back-half decline still implied at the high end of guide. This is consistent with the -260bps gross margin compression guide (~$30M GP headwind on $1.13B sales) and the $10-15M continuing rebanner investment in FY26. The 'back-half loaded' framing in the Q1 PR is honest (Q2 and Q3 of footwear retail are seasonal peaks) but does not flag that even the back half is expected to be down YoY.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch — guidance math is achievable but implies continuing YoY EPS declines through Fiscal 2026. The 'reaffirming guidance' headline is technically correct and supportive — Q1 came in at $0.23 vs whatever consensus was, the company hit the milestone. But the implied back-half exit run-rate of ~$0.42/quarter annualized is ~$1.68 — below the FY24 EPS of $2.68 and the FY23 EPS of $2.68 — so even successful FY26 execution leaves earnings power well below the pre-rebanner pre-tariff base. Honest framing: the issuer says 'reaffirming guidance,' and the cumulative interpretation is 'still descending earnings, just at a guided pace.'

#### [SCVL-08-inventory-build-and-tariff-exposure]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K: 'Our Merchandise Inventories at the end of Fiscal 2025 were $439.6 million, up approximately 14% compared to the end of Fiscal 2024. We increased our inventory positions this year, taking advantage of opportunistic buys for seasonal and in-demand merchandise. This strategy improved availability of key merchandise and drove margin expansion in Fiscal 2025. We anticipate declines in Merchandise Inventories in Fiscal 2026 in a range of $50 to $65 million.' Risk factors note China tariff exposure: 'changes in trade policies and tariffs impacting China and other countries that are the major manufacturers of footwear.' Q1 FY26 PR mentions 'Supreme Court Tariff' subsequent-events disclosure in the 10-K.
- **f**: Mode A — verify the inventory level and reduction plan. Mode B — is the $439.6M inventory at FYE25 (vs $383M FYE24) a strategic 'opportunistic buy' or a forced markdown waiting to happen given declining comp sales?
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K + Q1 FY26 PR)`
- **M_value**: FYE25 inventory $439.6M vs FYE24 $383M = +$56.6M / +14.8%. Net Sales FY25 $1,135M vs FY24 $1,203M (-5.6%). Inventory:Sales ratio FY25 = 38.7% vs FY24 31.8% — meaningfully elevated. Q1 FY26-end inventory $417.2M = -$22.4M from FYE25 (5 weeks of normalization), down $11.2M YoY. Company guides $50-65M further reduction by FYE26. Q1 FY26 gross margin 33.3% vs 34.5% PY (-120bps), Merchandise margin -140bps 'primarily driven by increased promotional activity' — i.e., the inventory normalization is happening THROUGH markdowns, consistent with the prior 'opportunistic buys' now requiring price concessions to clear. FY26 guidance bakes in ~260bps of gross margin compression vs FY25, which embeds continued markdown pressure for the year. China tariff risk is acknowledged but not quantified.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch on framing. Disclosure is honest — inventory level, sequential reduction, and gross margin compression are all in the documents. But the issuer's framing of the FY25 inventory build as 'opportunistic buys that drove margin expansion' is partially revisionist — the FY25 36.6% gross margin was indeed up 100bps from FY24 35.6%, but FY26's guided 34% is now 260bps lower than FY25, meaning the 'opportunistic' inventory is being cleared at margin-compressing prices in FY26. The forward markdown pressure is real and is baked into guidance. China tariff risk is plausible (footwear is heavily China-sourced) but not quantified in 10-K — the issuer cites 'changes in trade policies' without numerical sensitivity. This is moderate underdelivery on the forward inventory/margin narrative.

Post-tx beneficial ownership at 4,173,529 sh each as of 12/2024 P-tx.
- **f**: Mode B — separate 'gift' transactions (non-economic) from 'sale' transactions (economic) for the 10%+ founder family. Quantify their net economic position change.

- **M_value**: Weaver family combined open-market PURCHASES (24mo): 571,000 sh / $19.36M (both bought 12/2024). Combined open-market SALES (24mo): ZERO. Combined GIFTS (24mo): 8 transactions × 166,666 sh = 1,333,328 sh combined gifted (2 per quarter, every quarter from 6/2025 through 4/2026; pattern also visible in 2024 quarters). At cutoff ~$13-14 stock price, the gifted shares represent ~$17-19M of value moved out of direct ownership to recipients (presumably family / charitable / GRAT structures). Net economic position change: PURCHASES $19.4M — SALES $0 = +$19.4M net buying with the personal balance sheet. The systematic quarterly gifting is a wealth-transfer/estate-planning pattern, not divestment — note the $0 price on every G-coded transaction.
- **interpretation**: Mode B clarification: a naive read of the Weaver Form 4 tape (8 G-code 'dispositions' per 12mo) could be mis-classified as bearish insider activity. The correct interpretation is that the 91-year-old Chairman is executing a multi-year wealth-transfer plan via family/charitable vehicles (consistent with quarterly cadence and identical share counts of 166,666 each time). The economic position has only been INCREASED by the December 2024 $19.4M open-market buy. Combined with the CFO's April 2026 buy ($500K), the named officer + 10%+ holder cohort has $19.9M of net cumulative open-market buying with $50K of trivial selling. This is the rare 'distressed' name where the insider tape unambiguously supports a constructive read.

#### [SCVL-10-governance-staggered-board-and-say-on-pay]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K + 2025 DEF 14A: 'Board of Directors that has staggered three-year terms for its members, supermajority voting provisions, restrictions on the ability of shareholders to call special meetings and to take action by written consent.' 6/27/2025 8-K Item 5.07: 2025 Annual Meeting director-election + say-on-pay results.

- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K + 6/27/2025 8-K Item 5.07)`
- **M_value**: Governance: staggered 3-year board terms, supermajority voting, no shareholder special meetings, no action by written consent — i.e., heavily defensive against takeover. 6/27/2025 vote outcomes: Aschleman 23.77M FOR / 0.59M AGAINST (2.4% against), Guthrie 22.37M FOR / 1.99M AGAINST (8.2% against — notably high), Sifford 23.75M FOR / 0.62M AGAINST (2.5%). Say-on-pay: 22.11M FOR / 2.24M AGAINST (9.2% against — moderately elevated). Deloitte ratification clean (1.3% against). No SC 13D filings, no proxy contest, no activist disclosure in the pre-cutoff window. The 8.2% against on Guthrie and 9.2% on say-on-pay are meaningful (typical large-cap say-on-pay against is 3-5%) but not at activist-trigger levels. By the 2026 annual meeting (June 2026, post-cutoff), shareholders will vote on the proposed Shoe Station Group name change — which now corresponds to an abandoned strategy.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch on governance signals. The 9.2% say-on-pay against vote and the 8.2% against on director Guthrie (who also did a small October 2025 open-market sale, the only insider sale of the year) is slightly elevated and would warrant attention but is well below the 30%+ threshold that triggers ISS/SH-vote red flags. No activist is present (no 13D, no proxy contest). The 2026 annual meeting agenda still includes the Shoe Station Group name change — which is now zombified as the strategy it represented has been abandoned. The Board's '2025-11-13 unanimous' endorsement of the name change combined with the 2026-05-21 strategy reversal is a credibility cost, not a governance crisis. The defensive governance structure (staggered board, supermajority) provides insulation against any short-term agitation while the new interim CEO works through the reset.

## SMPL — The Simply Good Foods Company
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.2
**Sector**: Consumer Defensive  |  **Industry**: Packaged Foods
**Filing**: 0001702744-25-000046_10K.txt  (2025-10-28)

### Summary
SMPL FY25 10-K (filed 2025-10-28, FYE Aug 30 2025) framed Atkins as a managed transition, OWYN as an accretive acquisition, and Quest as a +11% growth engine. The two subsequent fiscal 2026 10-Qs (Q1 filed 2026-01-08, Q2 filed 2026-04-09) materially contradict all three: OWYN was impaired $187M (66% of the $282M June 2024 purchase price) just 20 months post-close after being declared NOT impaired qualitatively in the FY25 10-K; Atkins took a SECOND consecutive $62M impairment (on top of the FY25 $60.9M); and Quest growth has decelerated from +11.1% FY25 to +0.3% in Q2 FY26 (the company's only remaining growth engine stalled). Concurrently, the company executed $188M of debt-funded buybacks in H1 FY26 at an average $19.62 (cutoff price implied ~$13-14), the CEO was fired January 18 2026 and replaced by the prior CEO (Scalzo, 2017-2023), and a $15M restructuring is underway. Form 4 evidence is mixed: independent director James Kilts (former Gillette/Kraft CEO, Centerview founder) bought 80,000 shares for $991K on April 23 2026 (two weeks after the Q2 impairment), and director Clayton Daley Jr (former P&G CFO) bought 10,000 shares for $118K on May 14 2026 — meaningful informed-insider purchases into the drawdown. BUT the new interim CEO Joseph Scalzo SOLD $14.94M as a director in the year before being reappointed, and total 12-month insider sales ($24.7M) exceed purchases ($1.4M) at a 17:1 ratio. Verdict: SHORT-tier — three SEVERE underdeliveries (OWYN impairment, Atkins decay, Quest stall), one RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE (procyclical debt-funded buybacks), one SEVERE on CEO termination/restructuring. The Kilts/Daley purchases at $12 are notable but not large enough in dollar terms to offset $309.9M of cumulative intangible impairment, $188M of value-destroyed buybacks, and a strategy reset that requires the prior CEO to be recalled.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [SMPL-01-OWYN-impairment-of-recent-acquisition]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY25 (filed 2025-10-28) presents OWYN Acquisition (closed June 13, 2024, cash $281.9M, funded by $250M incremental Term Loan) as core to 'lead the nutritious snacking movement with trusted brands' — $137.0M FY25 net sales for the new brand (vs $29.2M short-period in FY24), framed as accretive growth platform. Goodwill/intangible impairment narrative in FY25 10-K was contained to a $60.9M Atkins-only write-down; OWYN tested at fiscal Q4 2025 and 'qualitative assessments did not identify indicators of impairment.'
- **f**: Mode B implied verification: given the issuer's pricing of OWYN at $282M in June 2024 and 'no impairment' result in fiscal Q4 2025, test whether subsequent disclosed performance (10-Qs filed Jan 8, 2026 and Apr 9, 2026) validates the carrying value. Cross-check Q2 fiscal 2026 10-Q intangibles note.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q2 fiscal 2026 10-Q, accession 0001702744-26-000012, filed 2026-04-09, Item 1 Note 4 Goodwill and Intangibles + Item 2 MD&A 'Intangible Assets' + brand-level revenue disaggregation Note 3)`
- **M_value**: Q2 fiscal 2026 10-Q discloses $249.0M loss on impairment in the 13 weeks ended Feb 28, 2026 — $187.0M for OWYN brand & trademarks (66% of original $282M deal value) and $62.0M for Atkins (a second consecutive Atkins write-down, on top of $60.9M FY25). Brand-level Q2 revenue: OWYN $28.1M vs $33.8M prior-year (-16.9%), explicitly attributed to 'poor velocities, including on newly expanded distribution.' Cumulative impairment of acquired intangibles in 20 months since the deal closed = $309.9M ($60.9 + $249.0), more than the $282M cash consideration. Total intangibles dropped from $1,261.6M at Aug 30 2025 to $1,004.8M at Feb 28 2026. The FY25 10-K's October 2025 qualitative assessment of OWYN said 'no indicators of impairment' four months before the company recognized a 66% impairment.
- **interpretation**: Classic 'recent-acquisition impairment' Mode-B catch. The FY25 10-K explicitly tested OWYN at the start of fiscal Q4 2025 (May 31 2025 onward) qualitatively and concluded no indicators existed. Just 20 months after closing, two-thirds of the OWYN intangible has been written down and management uses the language 'poor velocities, including on newly expanded distribution,' which is a polite way of saying the acquired brand failed to scale on the shelf space the company secured for it. This is the largest single driver of the -66% drawdown and is severely under-delivered vs. the FY25 narrative.

#### [SMPL-02-Atkins-decay-trajectory]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K Item 7 Business Trends: 'In recent periods, retail distribution for the Atkins brand has been under pressure ... we are taking actions to bolster the highest performing Atkins products' — framed as a contained, controllable transition. FY25 Atkins net sales were $420.8M vs $491.9M prior (-14.5%); FY25 recognized only a $60.9M Atkins impairment.
- **f**: Verify whether the disclosed 'managed reduction' framing matches actual quarterly trajectory in the two fiscal 2026 10-Qs.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 FY26 10-Q 0001702744-26-000006 + Q2 FY26 10-Q 0001702744-26-000012 brand disaggregation)`
- **M_value**: Atkins quarterly trajectory: FY24 average $123M/qtr → FY25 $105M/qtr → Q1 FY26 (Nov 29 2025) Atkins $90.3M (implied from $169.987M H1 − $79.717M Q2) vs $108.2M (-16.5%) → Q2 FY26 (Feb 28 2026) Atkins $79.7M vs $108.7M (-26.7%). Decline is ACCELERATING, not decelerating. A second $62.0M Atkins-specific impairment in Q2 FY26 (on top of the FY25 $60.9M Atkins impairment) means management's revenue projections used in the FY25 quantitative test were too high — two separate impairments of the same intangible in two consecutive fiscal years is materially worse than the FY25 'contained transition' framing.
- **interpretation**: The FY25 10-K framing of Atkins as a discrete, managed distribution event is inconsistent with two consecutive years of impairment and an accelerating decline rate. Mode A claim (Atkins is declining) is correctly disclosed; Mode B implication (decline rate is stabilizing, write-down captures fair value) is contradicted by Q2 FY26 evidence.

#### [SMPL-03-buyback-procyclicality]  severity = **RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE**
- **R**: FY25 10-K Item 7: 'During the fifty-two weeks ended August 30, 2025, the Company repurchased 1,592,471 shares of common stock at an average share price of $31.95' — $50.9M. Item 5: 'On October 21, 2025, the Company's Board of Directors approved a $150.0 million increase to its existing stock repurchase program' — issued just days before 10-K filing.
- **f**: Trace buyback execution prices through fiscal H1 2026 and cross-reference to the credit-facility action that funded them (insider_vs_calendar + self_text Q2 10-Q Item 2).
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q2 FY26 10-Q Issuer Purchases of Equity Securities table + Cash Flow + Note 6/7 debt note)`
- **M_value**: H1 FY26 (Aug 30 2025 → Feb 28 2026): 9,590,504 shares repurchased for $188.2M cash. November 30 2025 - January 3 2026: 2,230,028 sh @ $19.80; January 4-31, 2026: 1,319,409 sh @ $20.38; February 2026: 1,057,553 sh @ $17.41 (after Jan 8 Q1 earnings). Average H1 buyback price ~$19.62. Treasury stock balance rose from $129.3M to $319.4M. Funding source: $150.0M proceeds from issuance of long-term debt (revolver draw) per cash-flow statement — i.e., debt-funded buybacks. Long-term debt rose from $249.1M to $396.9M (+59%). Stock at cutoff (May 25 2026) is at -66% from peak; even vs the H1 average $19.62 buyback price, the cutoff price is materially lower (Q2 ended Feb 28 at ~$17; impairment announced Apr 9 likely drove additional decline). Of the $200M January 6, 2026 buyback authorization, $182.5M remained at Feb 28 (only $17.5M used post-authorization).
- **interpretation**: Debt-funded buyback at the wrong price is a textbook capital-allocation mistake. The October 21 2025 $150M increase to the buyback program was approved one week before the FY25 10-K was filed, and the January 6 2026 NEW $200M authorization came one day before Q1 earnings (Jan 8) — i.e., the company was telegraphing aggressive buybacks through the period when OWYN was failing and Atkins was decaying. $188M of book equity destroyed in six months while $187M of OWYN intangible was being written down. Not fraud — disclosed transparently — but a severe Mode-B governance signal.

- **R**: FY25 10-K Item 1: 'Experienced leadership team. Simply Good Foods has an experienced team of industry veterans' (no executive transition disclosure in FY25 narrative; FY25 had $3.87M in 'executive transition costs' added back to Adjusted EBITDA without naming the executive).

Scalzo (former CEO 2017-2023) returned as President and CEO on Jan 19, 2026 with an inducement stock-option grant. Ratzan (director affiliated with Centerview Partners) $5.50M; Timothy R. Kraft (Chief Legal Officer) $3.66M. All sales executed before the Q2 FY26 impairment (latest sale Sept 2025 vintage).

CFO Bealer + CCO Clawson buys at $20 in Nov 2025 are smaller and pre-disaster. NEGATIVE: Joseph Scalzo, the NEW interim CEO appointed Jan 19 2026, sold $14.94M of stock as a director in the months before he was reappointed CEO — this is the opposite of the deployment-supporting pattern. Net evidence: the post-impairment director buying is real but smaller in dollar-terms ($1.1M post-Apr-9) than what would justify reversing a SEVERE-tier verdict on its own. Note as a meaningful counter-signal but do NOT escalate to PASS.

AR concentration shifted: Customer 1 from 28% of AR (FY24) → 36% (FY25); Customer 2 28% (FY25 from 34%). Q2 FY26 total AR dropped from $165.0M to $123.5M — consistent with the -9.4% Q2 sales decline. The Atkins distribution loss is explicitly retailer-driven; if Customer 1 or 2 reduces Atkins shelf further (10-Q language: 'large retail presence on-shelf, which is being reduced in the current fiscal year and could be reduced in future periods'), the concentration becomes a single-customer risk vector.
- **interpretation**: Disclosure is honest and quantified. The Mode-B concern is that two retailers control 49% of revenue and are unilaterally driving the Atkins SKU rationalization; SMPL has limited bargaining power. Not severe, but a structural overhang.

#### [SMPL-07-debt-maturity-2027]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K Item 7 Liquidity: 'As of August 30, 2025, the outstanding balance of the Term Facility was $250.0 million ... The outstanding balance of the Term Facility is due upon its maturity in March 2027.' Revolving Credit Facility matures December 16, 2026.
- **f**: Verify maturity wall, leverage trajectory through fiscal H1 2026, and covenant headroom (max total net leverage 6.00:1.00 for revolver, contingent on >30% utilization).

Term Facility $250M still matures March 2027; Revolver matures Dec 16, 2026 (within 9 months of cutoff). LTM Adjusted EBITDA trajectory: FY25 reported $278M. H1 FY26 Adj EBITDA likely declined materially given $326M Q2 revenue with 460bp gross-margin compression and $4.5M restructuring drag (10-Q does not provide explicit Adj EBITDA, but $213M Q2 operating loss + $249M impairment add-back implies operating result roughly half of prior-year run rate). FY25 covenant test: total net leverage 6.00x maximum is non-binding at current ~1.4x net leverage so no immediate breach risk. But the $250M term + $150M revolver = $400M of debt maturing within 18-21 months of cutoff, against deteriorating EBITDA. Q2 10-Q confirms 'we were in compliance with all financial covenants'.
- **interpretation**: Mode A claim (compliance, maturity dates) is accurate. Mode B concern: the company DOUBLED debt-funded-buyback risk into a maturity wall just as EBITDA started deteriorating. Refinancing $400M in 2026-2027 at higher SOFR spreads (term-loan margin now SOFR+2.00%) with weaker EBITDA is a meaningful negative — not severe yet (leverage is still manageable, covenant headroom exists, cash $107M), but the situation degraded faster than the FY25 10-K implied.

Q2 (Feb 28 2026): identical pricing-and-productivity language but Q2 added 'The ongoing conflict in Iran and geopolitical tensions in the region could lead to significant disruption of global energy supplies and increases in global energy prices' — a new risk factor not in the Q1 or FY25 disclosures. Q2 also adds 'OWYN experienced poor velocities, including on newly expanded distribution' as a new business-trend disclosure. The company does not provide explicit FY26 revenue/EBITDA guidance in either 10-Q.
- **interpretation**: Disclosure is honest (tariffs, OWYN velocity, Iran conflict all explicitly added as risks). Mode-B observation: the pricing-action narrative repeated verbatim Q1→Q2 while revenue trend deteriorated from -0.3% to -9.4% suggests the pricing actions are not yet offsetting volume losses — but the company doesn't quantify this gap. #### [SMPL-09-goodwill-no-impairment-at-Q2]  severity = **UNVERIFIABLE**
- **R**: Q2 FY26 10-Q Note 4: 'fair value was greater than its carrying value, resulting in no impairment charges related to goodwill during the thirteen and twenty-six weeks ended February 28, 2026'.

Concluded reporting unit fair value > carrying value. Triggers cited: 'sustained decline in the Company's share price, declines in the Company's market capitalization, and updated future revenue projections'. Importantly, the $589.97M goodwill is at an entity level (one reporting unit) — so all three brands are aggregated for goodwill purposes. The intangibles ($1.005B post-Q2) are separately tested by brand which is why OWYN and Atkins were written down without touching goodwill.

With the share price down ~20% further between Feb 28 2026 (~$17) and cutoff (~$13-14 implied from -66% drawdown), a goodwill impairment in fiscal Q3 (May 30 2026 quarter-end) is plausible. #### [SMPL-10-Quest-the-last-engine]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: FY25 10-K positions Quest as the growth engine: Quest FY25 $863.6M vs $777.4M (+11.1%). H1 FY26 Q1 narrative: 'continued ... declines for Atkins and modest declines for OWYN, which were partially offset by Quest volume-driven growth.'

- **interpretation**: The single most important Mode-B finding for the forward thesis. The entire 'OWYN-failed-but-Quest-grows' bull case rests on Quest continuing to compound at low-double-digit rates. Q2 FY26 +0.3% Quest growth indicates the company's ONLY remaining growth engine has stalled, while Atkins is in -27% accelerating decline and OWYN was just written down 66%. This justifies the -66% drawdown as fundamentally driven, not sentiment-driven, and is the strongest argument that the equity is in a SHORT-tier setup rather than a value reversal.

## TMDX — TransMedics Group, Inc.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.3
**Sector**: Healthcare  |  **Industry**: Medical Devices
**Filing**: 0001193125-26-067032_10K.txt  (2026-02-24)

### Summary
TMDX is a complex Mode-A-honest, Mode-B-corroborated SHORT-LEANING NEUTRAL. The 10-K honestly discloses an ADVERSE ICFR opinion (material weakness on inventory movement, unremediated through Q1 2026), a 10b-5 class action with MTD pending, an active FDA Citizen Petition seeking to revoke OCS Liver PMA approval, and FY25 net income inflated by a $103M non-recurring tax-VA release (Feb 27, 2026 8-K issued specifically to clarify the optical EPS jump). Q1 2026 shows growth deceleration to +21% (vs FY25 +37%) and -72% net-income YoY decline (operating margin -1030 bps). Two of three warm-perfusion competitors acquired by Terumo/Getinge in 15 months. Form-4 evidence is mixed: CEO Hassanein executed ~$3M of open-market code-P PURCHASES in Aug 2025 ($1.98M @ $116-118.68) and Nov 2025 ($1.0M @ $114.00) — the only open-market buying in the trailing 12 months. However, every other officer/director with a discretionary transaction either sold (code S, M/S/M exercise-and-sell, or F+S) or adopted a 10b5-1 sale plan (CMO Khayal, Dec 11 2025, 22,928 shares); the May 22 2026 director Form 4 cluster (7 directors, 2,922 shares each) is annual A-coded RSU grants, not buys. Shareholder vote on stock-plan amendment got 28% against — institutional pushback on further dilution. Cash $461.7M is adequate vs $460M 2028 convertibles. Verdict: SHORT-tier on the auditor-opinion + litigation + regulatory + Q1-deceleration vector; the CEO's open-market commitment is the lone supportive signal but is itself underwater (purchases at $114-$118 now ~50% below water at cutoff). Severity counts: P=1, M=4, S=3, R=0, U=1. Composite: 0.30 (distressed but not collapse-tier — CEO commitment is the floor).

### R/f/M tuples (8)

#### [TMDX-1]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: We generated total revenue of $605.5 million and had net income of $190.3 million for the year ended December 31, 2025. We generated total revenue of $441.5 million and had net income of $35.5 million for the year ended December 31, 2024.
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K Item 7 MD&A + Note 8 Income Taxes + Feb 27 2026 8-K`
- **M_value**: Of the $190.3M 2025 net income, $103.3M (54%) is a one-time non-cash valuation-allowance release on deferred tax assets, recognized in Q4 2025. Pre-tax income was $107.5M. The effective tax rate was -77.0% (benefit, not provision). Underlying 'core' net income excluding the VA release is ~$87M, only ~2.5x FY24, not 5x. TMDX filed a SECOND 8-K on Feb 27, 2026 (3 days after the 10-K) specifically to clarify the impact of the VA release on previously reported Q4 2025 results — itself a disclosure-quality flag suggesting investor confusion required correction.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: The headline 'net income up 5x' framing is technically true but quality-of-earnings impaired. Management's own subsequent 8-K (Feb 27) implies they recognized the VA release was being mis-read. The 10-K itself notes 'our income tax rate will increase in the future' — i.e., the optical EPS jump is unrepeatable. For a name down 54%, the issuer's chosen profitability narrative depends on a non-recurring tax item; the underlying operating story is far less favorable.

The Company did not design and maintain effective controls over inventory movement within its manufacturing network... The material weakness resulted in immaterial misstatements to inventory, accounts payable, cost of net product revenue, selling, general and administrative expenses and research and development expenses, in the interim consolidated financial statements for the quarterly period ended March 31, 2025 and the quarterly and year-to-date periods ended March 31, 2024, June 30, 2024 and September 30, 2024.

Q1 2026 10-Q (filed 2026-05-05) confirms material weakness was NOT remediated as of March 31, 2026. Four prior interim periods required correction. Material weakness specifically touches inventory, AP, COGS, SG&A, R&D — i.e., nearly the entire P&L below revenue.
- **interpretation**: An ADVERSE ICFR opinion on the most recent 10-K is the strongest accounting-quality red flag the auditor framework allows. Material weakness scope (inventory movement) directly intersects with the short-seller allegations (off-label use, NOP economics) and the securities class-action class period (FY2022-Jan 2025). Inventory at a single-product medical-device manufacturer is the central operating asset. Unremediated through Q1 2026.

FDA Citizen Petition co-pending: short seller has petitioned FDA to suspend the OCS Liver PMA approval over alleged off-label use; TMDX has 'responded stating both legal and factual bases for rejection' (not yet adjudicated). MTD has been fully briefed for 5 months without ruling, which in PSLRA practice often correlates with denial probability rather than dismissal (dismissed cases tend to be ruled on faster). Audit Committee commissioned an 'independent review of business practices following allegations raised in a short seller report' — TMDX is paying outside counsel/forensic accountants for an internal investigation, not denying the underlying allegations procedurally.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: The convergence of (a) 10b-5 class action with class period ending exactly at the short seller report, (b) active FDA Citizen Petition seeking to revoke PMA approval, (c) Audit Committee-commissioned independent investigation, (d) unremediated material weakness on inventory, (e) -54% drawdown is the canonical 'enforcement / regulatory / accounting' multi-vector setup. The Citizen Petition tail risk is particularly under-priced — FDA suspension of OCS Liver PMA would impair the highest-margin product line; the issuer's own risk-factor explicitly acknowledges even if FDA rejects the petition 'it may nonetheless impact how surgeons, patients and transplant centers perceive the safety and efficacy.'

- **M_value**: CEO Hassanein bought ~$3M total in two events at $114-$118, AFTER the Jan 2025 short report and AFTER the Feb 2025 class action was filed. Per the framework prompt, this is the 'open-market code-P purchases by C-suite, sized meaningfully, into drawdown' supportive signal — but with three caveats: (a) Both buys came AFTER bad news was already public (not contrarian-against-management-narrative); (b) Sized at ~$3M for a CEO whose total annual compensation/equity is multiples larger — meaningful but not 'all-in'; (c) Stock has since declined another 50%+ from Hassanein's purchase price (cutoff drawdown -54% implies a current price ~$60-$70 vs his $114-$118 buy), so his prior commitment-signal has been substantially impaired by subsequent disclosures. Tamer Khayal (CMO) adopted a Rule 10b5-1 SALE plan Dec 11 2025 for 22,928 shares to commence Mar 16 2026 — i.e., the CMO put a SELLING plan in place 1 week before year-end and after his own December-quarter equity events. The CEO is alone in personal-balance-sheet conviction, and even his commitment is now substantially underwater. Stock-based comp was $30.8M in FY25 (~5% of revenue); R&D and SG&A are scaling faster than revenue. The -54% drawdown likely already reflects part of this, but Q1's -72% net income decline against +21% revenue growth indicates the bear-case operating story is materializing in the income statement.

#### [TMDX-7]  severity = **UNVERIFIABLE**
- **R**: we entered into a credit agreement with Canadian Imperial Bank of Commerce, or CIBC, under which we borrowed $60.0 million. In May 2023, we issued and sold $460.0 million in aggregate principal amount of our Notes... The initial conversion rate is 10.6388 shares of common stock per $1,000 principal amount of the Notes, which represents an initial conversion price of approximately $94.00 per share... cash of $461.7 million as of March 31, 2026 will be sufficient to fund its operations... for at least the next 12 months

- **M_value**: Cash $461.7M vs $460M convertible principal due 2028 — essentially fully cash-covered at maturity if needed, BUT note holders may elect cash or stock settlement (TMDX's option). At a current stock price implied by -54% drawdown (~$60-$70), the converts are well out-of-the-money (conversion price $94) so dilution risk at conversion is low, but ALSO no equity-financing optionality — TMDX cannot easily raise stock at these levels without major dilution. June 8 2026 redemption call right activates immediately after cutoff but only triggers at 130% of $94 ($122.20). The capped call cap price ($141.88) is approximately 2x the current stock price — capped calls are deeply out-of-the-money and provide no economic protection at these levels. Going concern is not at issue, BUT the cash-vs-converts coverage means there is no real refinancing buffer; if operating cash flow deteriorates (Q1 2026 net income $7M, down from $26M), the cash cushion erodes against the 2028 maturity.
- **interpretation**: Mode A pass on liquidity disclosure (cash adequate for 12+ months per management; converts cash-covered). Mode B caveat: the convertible structure means no near-term solvency event, but the company has effectively no equity-raise optionality at current prices, the capped calls are economically worthless at these levels, and the converts mature in May 2028 — meaning ~24 months from cutoff to either refinance or pay down. Combined with operating-margin compression and litigation/regulatory tails, this is a name with constrained financial flexibility into a critical 2026-2028 period.

#### [TMDX-8]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Proposal Three: The shareholders approved the amendment to the Amended and Restated TransMedics Group, Inc. 2019 Stock Incentive Plan, based on the following votes: For 17,265,369 Against 6,758,882 Abstain 39,472 Broker Non-Votes 4,632,985
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `May 21, 2026 8-K Item 5.07 (annual meeting voting results)`
- **M_value**: Plan-amendment proposal got 28.1% AGAINST — well above the 20% threshold that typically triggers ISS/Glass-Lewis investor-engagement obligations. Say-on-pay (Proposal 2) got 4.8% against (low). Director election against-votes were under 7% except Basile at 6.9%. The plan-amendment dissent is the focal proxy signal: shareholders are willing to elect the slate and endorse current pay, but are objecting to additional share-pool dilution at the current valuation. Khayal's 22,928-share 10b5-1 SALE plan and the Feb 2026/May 2026 director-equity grants happened in the same proxy-cycle window.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: 28% dissent on a plan-amendment vote suggests institutional shareholders see the equity-comp dilution as inappropriate given (a) -54% drawdown, (b) material weakness, (c) pending litigation, (d) Q1 deceleration. Combined with the CMO's 10b5-1 sale plan and the absence of officer-level open-market buys, the proxy signal corroborates the broader 'C-suite extraction, single CEO conviction' pattern.
