## TRIP — Tripadvisor, Inc.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.4
**Sector**: Consumer Cyclical  |  **Industry**: Travel Services
**Filing**: 0001193125-26-051281_10K.txt  (2026-02-13)

### Summary
TripAdvisor exhibits classic honesty-alpha 'distressed but not lying' archetype. Severity counts: 2 PASS, 4 MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY, 4 SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY, 0 RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE, 0 UNVERIFIABLE. Top findings: (1) Hotels & Other segment in self-acknowledged AI-overview structural decline, Q1 2026 revenue -17% YoY accelerating from FY2025 -8%; (2) Experiences/TheFork EBITDA growth ($93M over 2yrs) does not yet replace Hotels EBITDA loss ($108M); (3) Q1 2026 consolidated adjusted EBITDA flipped negative ($-19.2M vs +$36.7M prior year). Form-4 insider PURCHASES: ZERO code-P in 17 months (99 Form 4s, 273 tx) — but historical baseline 2023-2024 was also zero, so absence is consistent management behavior rather than acute red flag. No clustered pre-disclosure selling; insiders held through the entire drawdown. Clean ICFR and PwC opinion. Verdict: NEUTRAL leaning SHORT-tier. The fundamental deterioration is real and accelerating, but the disclosure is honest — this is a 'broken business not a fraud' setup. The framework's honesty-alpha contribution here is moderate (TRIP is not a 'liar' to exclude), and the structural AI-overview thesis suggests further downside risk to Hotels segment EBITDA in 2026-2027 before Experiences pivot can mathematically cover the hole.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [TRIP-01-insider-buying-absent]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Implied management confidence after FY2025 10-K (filed 2026-02-13) and Q1 2026 10-Q (filed 2026-05-07) into a -47.3% drawdown.
- **f**: Parse Form 4 XML for code-P (open-market purchases) by named executives or 10%+ holders across 2025-01-01 to cutoff 2026-05-25. Compare against transaction-code mix and sales volume.
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse)`
- **M_value**: 99 Form 4 filings in the 17 months ending 2026-05-25. 273 non-derivative transactions parsed. Code mix: M=136 (option/RSU exercise), F=116 (withhold-to-cover tax), A=12 (grants), S=7 (sales), J=2 (other), P=0. ZERO open-market purchases by ANY executive, director, or 10%+ holder during the entire window — including the 8-K Apr-6 2026 convertible repayment announcement, the May-7 Q1 print, and the trough of the drawdown. Sales (code S) total $885K: CEO of TheFork (Ambeskovic) 5 sales = $397K, President Brand Tripadvisor (Dalton) 1 sale = $269K, Chief Legal Officer (Kalvert) 1 sale = $218K. Historical baseline check 2023-2024: also 0 code-P purchases (68 Form 4s, 3 sales = $698K) — i.e., absence is consistent management behavior, but it is still notable that none of the C-suite or Chairman Greg Maffei (post-LTRIP merger 2025-04-29 he owns the rolled position directly) bought the dip.
- **interpretation**: Honesty-alpha framing: insiders never buy this stock, so absence by itself isn't acutely informative. However, in a -47.3% drawdown driven by structural SEO/AI-overview deterioration in the cash-cow Hotels segment, the textbook 'management confidence' tape (CEO/CFO/Chairman code-P buy) does NOT appear. Combined with $90M of FY2025 buyback executed at avg $14.72 (now underwater since stock is at the cutoff price implied by -47.3% drawdown) and continued buyback authorization, the company is buying with corporate cash while no executive risks personal capital. Mode-B gap: management says 'foreseeable' liquidity is sufficient but won't put personal $ behind it.

#### [TRIP-02-hotels-segment-structural-decline]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "We believe that our Hotels and Other segment will continue to be impacted by these challenges and others, including AI overviews displacing top ranked links, reduced click-through rates and a shift towards paid marketing channels." (Q1 2026 10-Q, self-disclosed risk).
- **f**: Mode B (implied verification): given the issuer concedes AI-overview displacement, quantify the 2-year arc of Hotels & Other revenue and EBITDA to test how far the cash cow has eroded vs prior peak.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K segment tables + Q1 2026 10-Q segment tables)`
- **M_value**: Hotels & Other segment (legacy core / cash cow): Revenue 2023 $901.5M → 2024 $818.1M (-9%) → 2025 $750.1M (-8%) → Q1 2026 $186.9M vs $225.7M Q1 2025 = -$38.8M = -17% YoY. Adjusted EBITDA: 2023 $315.2M → 2024 $254.1M (-19%) → 2025 $207.2M (-18%) → Q1 2026 -$24.7M YoY (margin -8.0 pp). EBITDA margin compression: 35.0% → 31.1% → 27.6% → Q1 2026 down 8 pp further. Cumulative segment EBITDA loss from 2023 to 2025 = $108M (-34% of peak). Issuer attributes Q1 2026 hotel meta drop to 'declines in free marketing channels, particularly SEO' with explicit AI-overview disclosure.
- **interpretation**: The Hotels segment is in structural, accelerating decline driven by Google AI Overviews compressing SEO click-through. The issuer's own language ('will continue to be impacted') admits this is not cyclical. Q1 2026 -17% revenue acceleration vs FY2025 -8% suggests the Google AI Overviews global rollout in 2024-25 is hitting harder in 2026. This segment historically generated ~60% of consolidated EBITDA — its $108M EBITDA loss over 2 years is the proximate cause of the drawdown. Disclosure is honest, but management's strategic narrative (pivot to Experiences/TheFork) requires those segments to absorb a $300M+ EBITDA hole.

#### [TRIP-03-experiences-pivot-not-yet-replacing-hotels]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "The Company is focused on growing and scaling its Experiences marketplace... Importantly, as of the year ended December 31, 2025, the Experiences and TheFork segments represented approximately 60% of the Company's consolidated revenue and 35% of our consolidated adjusted EBITDA."
- **f**: Mode B: given the strategic pivot claim, quantify whether new-segment EBITDA growth offsets Hotels EBITDA loss on a dollar basis (not just %).
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K segment tables)`
- **M_value**: Experiences segment: Revenue 2023 $737.2M → 2024 $840.1M → 2025 $924.4M (+10%); EBITDA $33.3M → $79.1M → $91.1M; margin 4.5% → 9.4% → 9.9%. TheFork: Revenue $153.7M → $180.8M → $220.8M (+22%); EBITDA -$14.5M → $5.3M → $20.4M; margin -9.4% → 2.9% → 9.2%. Sum of 'new' EBITDA growth 2023→2025: ($91.1+$20.4) - ($33.3-$14.5) = $111.5M - $18.8M = +$92.7M. Hotels EBITDA loss 2023→2025: $315.2 → $207.2 = -$108.0M. Net: -$15.3M consolidated EBITDA decline (matches consolidated: $334.0M → $318.7M = -$15.3M, -5%). Q1 2026: Hotels EBITDA -$24.7M YoY while Experiences/TheFork combined Q1 EBITDA improvement is insufficient — consolidated adjusted EBITDA collapsed from +$36.7M Q1'25 to -$19.2M Q1'26, a $55.9M swing.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B catch: management's 'pivot is working' narrative is mathematically accurate at the mix-shift level (60% revenue from marketplaces) but masks the dollar reality — Experiences+TheFork EBITDA growth of $93M over 2 years is barely offsetting Hotels EBITDA loss of $108M. In Q1 2026, the pivot stopped working: consolidated EBITDA went from +$36.7M to -$19.2M. The two 'growth' segments cannot grow fast enough in dollars to plug the Hotels hole, which is itself accelerating. The strategic narrative is honest but the math doesn't yet support it as the bull case requires.

#### [TRIP-04-buyback-at-elevated-prices]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "During 2025, we repurchased 6,105,262 shares of its common stock at an average price of $14.72 per share... or $90 million in the aggregate. As of December 31, 2025, there was $110 million remaining available to repurchase shares."
- **f**: Verify FY2025 buyback execution prices vs cutoff-period price (implied -47.3% drawdown from prior peak), and whether management has slowed/accelerated repurchases as price fell.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 5 + Equity statement)`
- **M_value**: FY2025 repurchases: 6.105M sh at avg $14.72 = $89.9M. Prior year FY2024: 4.725M sh at avg $15.85 = $74.9M. Buyback program $250M authorized Sep-2023 — $110M remains as of Dec-31-2025. Implied price at cutoff given -47.3% drawdown from late-2025 levels would be in low-to-mid single digits. Management bought $90M at $14.72 in 2025; with stock down 47% from that price-area, those buybacks are deeply underwater. No FY2026 buyback figure yet disclosed in Q1 2026 10-Q (need to check share count: basic 125M FY2025 vs 139M FY2024 — the drop reflects buybacks and LTRIP merger collapse of dual-class).
- **interpretation**: Capital allocation: management deployed corporate cash at prices materially above current implied cutoff — same pattern seen at many distressed names. Not deception, but a sign management's price-discovery process underestimated SEO/AI-overview structural risk. The $110M remaining authorization combined with $1.0B cash + $345M April 2026 convertible already repaid leaves liquidity intact, but the buyback program isn't a credible value-add at this point.

#### [TRIP-05-restructuring-charges-recurring]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "In November 2025, we announced a restructuring plan designed to support the Company's strategic priorities... The plan includes a significant global workforce reduction and other targeted expense reductions." Restructuring charges 2025=$43.4M, 2024=$21.1M, 2023=$22.2M.
- **f**: Verify whether restructuring is one-time event or recurring; test management's framing of 'transformation' against multi-year actual charges.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 7 cost detail)`
- **M_value**: Restructuring & other related reorganization costs: 2023 $22.2M → 2024 $21.1M → 2025 $43.4M (jumped 106% YoY, now 2.3% of revenue vs 1.2% prior). Three consecutive years of restructuring totaling $86.7M — these are recurring transformation costs, not one-time. November 2025 plan announced just two months before the 10-K and explicitly tied to AI-pivot narrative ('positioning as an experiences-led and AI-enabled company'). Multiple 10-K risk factors warn restructuring may impair recruitment ('public nature of the workforce reduction may impair our ability to recruit top talent'), cause attrition ('competitors may target our remaining employees'), and disrupt operations.
- **interpretation**: Multi-year restructuring at a company with no margin tailwind is a tell — management has been cutting costs for three years and the cash-cow segment still declined 17% in Q1 2026. The Nov-2025 plan is the third round; each appears reactive to deteriorating SEO/AI-overview pressure rather than offensive investment. Honest disclosure of the risks themselves (rare candor about retention risk) — but the recurrence pattern argues this is normalized expense, not transformation.

#### [TRIP-06-LTRIP-merger-cash-and-leverage]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "On December 18, 2024, the Company, Liberty TripAdvisor Holdings, Inc. ('LTRIP')... entered into the Merger Agreement... On April 29, 2025, the Merger closed... aggregate transaction price of the Merger was $437 million..." Also: "the Company increased its existing Term Loan B Facility... [$345M] for the redemption of the Company's outstanding 2026 Senior Notes."
- **f**: Verify the post-merger capital structure and the increase in debt resulting from collapsing the LTRIP control structure.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 8 Debt + Note on LTRIP merger)`
- **M_value**: Pre-merger: Term Loan B $494M + 0.25% Convertible 2026 Senior Notes $345M = $839M long-term debt at 12/31/2024. Post-merger (12/31/2025): Term Loan B $831M (up $337M from Tack-On Incremental of $341M, March 2025, at SOFR+275bp ≈ 7.1%) plus 2026 Convertible $345M reclassified to current liability for April 2026 maturity. The Convertible 0.25% coupon is being refinanced into a SOFR+275 floating-rate loan = +6.8 pp interest cost on $345M = ~$23M/yr incremental interest expense. Interest expense 2024 $46.4M → 2025 $63.3M (+36%) and will step up further in 2026. Cash $1.0B at 12/31/2025 → $1.03B at 3/31/2026 (Convertible repaid April 1, 2026 from cash). LTRIP merger cost $431M cash plus $6M stock — paid out of cash, reducing buffer. Maffei (LTRIP) remains Chairman. Loan to LTRIP $327M (March 2025) used to retire LTRIP's exchangeable debenture (Certares).
- **interpretation**: Capital structure became materially worse: trading low-coupon convertible (0.25%) for floating-rate Term Loan B (SOFR+275 ≈ 7%) raises interest expense by ~$23M/yr permanently. The LTRIP merger consumed ~$430M of cash to simplify governance (no controlling stockholder post-merger) — a defensible strategic decision but expensive at $14-15 share price area, especially given the stock fell another 47% from that level. Mode-B observation: management does not emphasize the interest-cost step-up in MD&A — the Q1 2026 interest expense was $15.7M vs $11.7M prior-year (+34%) and rising.

#### [TRIP-07-customer-concentration-risk-OTAs]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Booking (and its subsidiaries) accounted for approximately... 21% and 22% [respectively] of our consolidated revenue." Combined Booking+Expedia ≈25% of consolidated revenue, with 'nearly all' in Hotels and Other segment.
- **f**: Mode B: given the issuer admits ~25% revenue concentration with two CPC-based hotel meta customers in the segment that is in structural AI-overview decline, quantify the dependency risk.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K customer concentration disclosure + segment data)`
- **M_value**: Two-customer concentration: Booking+Expedia ≈ 25% of $1,891M consolidated revenue = ~$473M, nearly all in Hotels & Other segment ($750M FY25). Implied: Booking+Expedia ≈ 63% of Hotels & Other segment revenue. Both customers also operate competing hotel-meta and experience marketplaces. The Q1 2026 -17% hotel revenue decline implicitly means Booking/Expedia CPC bids are dropping (they pay TRIP less per click). Multi-year arc: 2023 ~$902M Hotels & Other → 2025 $750M = -$152M (-17%); if Booking/Expedia share held at 63%, their absolute payments to TRIP fell by ~$96M.
- **interpretation**: Mode-B catch: the customer concentration disclosure is the standard 10-K boilerplate, but combined with the AI-overview structural decline, TRIP's hotel meta franchise is functionally a two-customer business in terminal decline. Booking and Expedia have both invested heavily in direct supply (Booking owns 1.1M+ hotels via direct contracts; Expedia owns 2M+) — they don't need TRIP's audience as much as they did. The risk of Booking dropping below 10% disclosure threshold (i.e., cutting spend further) is material and the issuer does not quantify the customer-level deterioration.

#### [TRIP-08-Q1-2026-net-loss-acceleration]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Net income (loss) $(32.4) [Q1 2026] vs $(11.0) [Q1 2025]" — Q1 net loss tripled. Revenue $382.4M vs $398.2M = -4%.
- **f**: Verify Q1 2026 acceleration in losses against FY2025 trend; test whether management's 'foreseeable' liquidity claim is robust to continued Hotels EBITDA decline.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 2026 10-Q income statement + MD&A)`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 vs Q1 2025: Revenue $382.4M vs $398.2M (-4%, first quarterly revenue decline in years); Operating loss $(25.2)M vs $(15.5)M (-63% worse); Net loss $(32.4)M vs $(11.0)M (3x worse); Adjusted EBITDA $(19.2)M vs $36.7M ($56M swing). Interest expense $15.7M vs $11.7M (+34%). The deterioration is concentrated in Hotels & Other (EBITDA -$24.7M YoY) plus higher interest from Tack-On Term Loan B. Cash $1.03B at 3/31/26 includes $363M build from Q1 (operating + working capital cycle) before $345M Convertible repayment April 1. Post-repayment cash ≈ $685M plus $496M revolver capacity = $1.18B liquidity. Term Loan B matures July 2031.
- **interpretation**: The Q1 2026 print is the first window with full-quarter LTRIP merger interest cost AND accelerating SEO/AI-overview damage in hotels. Revenue declining for the first time, EBITDA flipped negative, net loss tripled. Management's 'foreseeable working capital sufficient' statement is technically true (cash + revolver = $1.18B post-Convertible repayment, no covenant issues) but the trajectory means consolidated FCF could turn meaningfully negative in 2026 if Hotels EBITDA losses continue at Q1 pace. No going-concern issue, but the bull case for stabilization has been broken by Q1.

#### [TRIP-09-no-Form4-clustered-pre-disclosure-selling]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider sales should NOT cluster pre-disclosure of bad news.
- **f**: Cross-reference 7 code-S sales in 17mo window against 8-K filing dates for capital events / earnings releases.
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: 7 code-S sales 2025-01 through 2026-05: Ambeskovic (CEO TheFork) 5 sales spread Aug-2025 and June-2025 ($397K); Dalton (Pres Brand TripAdvisor) 1 sale ~Feb 2025 ($269K); Kalvert (CLO) 1 sale ($218K). All sales precede the Nov-2025 restructuring announcement by 3-9 months and precede the April-2026 8-K / May-2026 Q1 print by 7-12 months. No clustered selling in the 30-day window before the November 2025 restructuring 8-K, the April 2026 8-K, or the May 2026 Q1 release. Total $885K is trivial vs market cap. Most striking is the absence of any C-suite sale during Q4 2025 / Q1 2026 — i.e., insiders held through the drawdown.
- **interpretation**: No clustered pre-disclosure selling. The IONQ/YSS pattern (insiders dump before bad news) does NOT appear at TRIP. Insiders held through the entire 47% drawdown. This argues against fraud, intentional misleading, or material non-public-information abuse. Honesty-alpha framing: this is a real disclosure-honest distressed name, not a 'liar' candidate.

#### [TRIP-10-internal-controls-effective]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "Our management... concluded that our internal control over financial reporting was effective" as of 12/31/2025.
- **f**: Verify ICFR conclusion + auditor opinion + look for material weakness disclosures.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 9A + auditor report)`
- **M_value**: Management concluded ICFR is effective as of 12/31/2025. Auditor PricewaterhouseCoopers (long-tenured) issued unqualified opinion on financial statements and on ICFR. No material weakness disclosed. No CORRESP from SEC indicating issues (16 UPLOAD filings are routine SEC correspondence, not enforcement). No 8-K Item 4.02 (non-reliance) ever filed.
- **interpretation**: Clean controls and clean auditor opinion. No accounting red flags. Combined with no insider selling, this puts TRIP firmly in the 'honest disclosure of bad facts' bucket — i.e., the framework's 'distressed but not lying' archetype (like SRPT 0.125 composite). The framework's contribution should be EXCLUSION of liars, not re-detection of honestly disclosed deterioration.

## TTGT — TechTarget, Inc. (Informa TechTarget)
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.45
**Sector**: Technology  |  **Industry**: B2B Marketing / Information Services
**Filing**: 0001193125-26-102183_10K.txt  (2026-03-11)

### Summary
TTGT is a forensically distressed, severely impaired post-merger entity ($1B+ in goodwill writedowns in 15 months wiping ~99.9% of recorded goodwill, unremediated material weaknesses, immaterial-misstatement concessions across all significant accounts), but Form 4 evidence is the OPPOSITE of the typical SHORT-tier pattern: CEO Gary Nugent and Director David Flaschen ESTABLISHED initial open-market positions in September 2025 at $5.85-$5.97, and Director Michael Griffey ran a ~$3.3M open-market PURCHASE program in December 2024 at $20-$27. 2025 insider selling was small (~$220K total), RSU-vesting-aligned, and dwarfed ~9:1 by purchases. The framework's explicit Form-4 escalation rule — 'open-market code-P purchases by C-suite or directors, sized meaningfully, into drawdown' — applies here. Liquidity is tightening (cash $40.6M, revolver 48% drawn, related-party lender) and organic growth is decelerating (+2.1% Q1 2026 with subscriptions declining), but the insider-commitment signal contradicts a pure SHORT thesis. Verdict: NEUTRAL with LONG-leaning honesty-alpha tilt — the issuer is honest about its impairments AND insiders are buying.

### R/f/M tuples (8)

#### [TTGT-1]  severity = **RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE**
- **R**: We recorded an impairment charge of $931.5 million related to the goodwill in all reporting units... we incurred losses of $1.0 billion driven mainly by non-cash charges for impairment of goodwill
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K Note 6 (Goodwill) + Q1 2026 10-Q Note 4 + reporting-unit allocation table`
- **M_value**: Gross carrying amount of goodwill at 3/31/2026 = $1,183.6M; accumulated impairment = $1,182.5M = 99.9% of all goodwill ever recorded has been written off within 15 months of the Dec 2, 2024 merger close. Legacy TechTarget reporting unit (the original public co that Informa acquired for $350M cash + stock) was impaired to $0 in FY2025. Industry Dive was impaired $243.4M (carrying $25.7M post-impairment vs presumably ~$269M pre); Bluefin Legacy fully impaired to $0 by Q1 2026. PwC issued a critical audit matter specifically on the goodwill impairment assessments.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: a ~$1.0B impairment representing ~99.9% of recorded goodwill within 15 months of closing the December 2024 reverse merger is a forensic indictment of the deal itself. Either (a) the purchase price was unsupportable from day one (M&A overpayment by Informa PLC at the expense of legacy TTGT public shareholders) or (b) the underlying assets deteriorated catastrophically in <12 months. Both interpretations point to an issuer whose central capital-allocation act of 2024 has been retroactively repudiated by its own audit committee. Q1 2026 continued impairment ($45M more) means the bleeding has not stopped at the FY2025 cutoff.

#### [TTGT-2]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Our management previously identified the following material weaknesses... and such material weaknesses remain unremediated as of December 31, 2025... insufficient segregation of duties... did not design and maintain effective controls over the period-end financial reporting process... resulted in the restatement of the Company's financial statements as of December 31, 2023
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K Item 9A (Controls and Procedures) + PwC audit report dated 3/11/2026 + comparison to FY2024 10-K material weakness disclosures`
- **M_value**: PwC's audit report (Boston, MA, March 11, 2026) confirms material weaknesses identified and that the firm 'considered these material weaknesses in determining the nature, timing, and extent of audit tests.' These weaknesses already produced (i) a restatement of the FY2023 financials and all 2023 interim periods, (ii) restatement of 2024 interim periods, (iii) acquisition/integration cost adjustments at 12/31/2024 year-end, and (iv) 'immaterial misstatements to substantially all significant accounts and disclosures as of December 31, 2025 and 2024.' PwC has been auditor only since 2024 (i.e., new audit relationship coincides with the merger). Remediation 'is still ongoing.'
- **interpretation**: Mode B: by the company's own admission its 12/31/2025 financial statements contain immaterial misstatements 'to substantially all significant accounts.' This is the rare case where a public-co CFO concedes the numbers are wrong but immaterial. Combined with a $1B goodwill impairment whose fair-value model PwC flagged as a critical audit matter requiring specialized professionals, the financial-reporting reliability of TTGT is impaired at the entity level. Material weaknesses persisting >18 months into the post-merger entity suggest organizational under-resourcing in finance — exactly the wrong gap for an issuer trying to demonstrate the merger thesis is intact.

#### [TTGT-3]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: [Form 4 transactions filed 2025-09-12 to 2025-09-15] code P (open-market purchase) by CEO Gary John Nugent and Director David J S Flaschen at ~$5.85-$5.97/share
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `form4 (EDGAR XML, 36 Form-4 filings 12/2024-12/2025) + insider_vs_calendar`
- **M_value**: Code-P PURCHASES by named executives/directors into the drawdown: (1) CEO Gary Nugent 4,200 sh @ $5.85 on 9/12/2025 — establishing initial direct-ownership position; (2) Director David Flaschen 20,000 sh @ $5.9662 on 9/12/2025 — likewise establishing position from zero; (3) Director Michael Griffey 148,237 sh purchased 12/3/2024-12/20/2024 at $20.00-$27.00 (~$3.3M). Cluster of director RSU/grant awards (code A) on 7/24/2025, 8/14/2025, 12/11/2025. Officer SELLS in 2025 were limited and predominantly net-settlement-related: Niemiec (CRO) sold 21,478 @ $5.75 on 8/29/2025; Noreck (CFO) sold 8,459 @ $5.99 on 8/22/2025; Tierney (CTO) sold 3,112 @ $5.99 on 8/22/2025; Rennick sold 3,498 @ $5.99 on 8/22/2025 — all aligned with 8/13/2025 RSU vesting (code M) immediately preceding, suggesting tax-related, not opportunistic distribution. Five Form 144 notices total (3 on 8/22/2025 ~$90K aggregate, 1 on 8/29/2025 $124K, 1 on 5/22/2026 $95K) — small in dollar terms.
- **interpretation**: Form 4 evidence MODESTLY SUPPORTS management commitment. Although the dollar magnitudes are small (CEO $25K, director Flaschen $119K, director Griffey $3.3M as a much larger and earlier post-merger conviction trade), the directional signal — CEO and a director establishing initial open-market positions at $5.85-$5.97 at the bottom of the chart in a SEVERELY-impaired issuer — is the opposite of the typical SHORT-tier pattern (clustered insider sales pre-bad-news, asymmetric sell:buy ratios). Selling activity is small, tax-aligned, and explainable by RSU vesting cycles. No 10b5-1 plan adoptions in Q4-2025. This Form 4 picture does NOT corroborate a fraud/distress narrative; it argues management has skin in the game at current price.

#### [TTGT-4]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: $120.1 million and $106.7 million, respectively, drawn under the Credit Facility... [covenants] Consolidated Total Net Leverage Ratio of 3.00 to 1.00 or less... Consolidated Interest Coverage Ratio of at least 3.00 to 1.00
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-Q Note on Credit Facility + 10-K cash flow + segment operating income reconciliation`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 segment operating income $44.99M (B2D $35.3M + I&A $9.7M) before unallocated corporate / D&A / restructuring / acquisition costs. Annualized segment OI ~$180M, but TTM net loss is ~$1B GAAP and operating cash flow was only $16.3M in FY2025. Debt grew $13.4M in Q1 2026 alone ($106.7M→$120.1M). 48% of the $250M parent-related-party revolver is now drawn vs 43% at year-end. Liquidity bridge: $40.6M cash + $130M undrawn capacity = $170M = ~10.4 months of FY2025 cash burn pre-working-capital. Covenant compliance dependent on aggressive add-backs given GAAP results. The lender IS the controlling shareholder (Informa PLC), introducing related-party concentration in event-of-default cure.
- **interpretation**: Liquidity is currently adequate but trending the wrong way: revolver utilization is increasing, cash is low ($40.6M absolute), operating cash generation is thin ($16M FY2025), and the entire $250M facility is provided by the controlling-stockholder parent (Informa Group Holdings Ltd), not a bank syndicate. Mode B: in a true distress event, an arms-length syndicate facility is generally more cure-flexible than a parent revolver, because the parent has the conflicting incentive to renegotiate ownership economics (e.g., debt-for-equity dilution). Covenant ratios with 3.0x net leverage and 3.0x interest coverage on a business that just impaired ~$1B of goodwill and produced GAAP $1B net loss means add-backs / EBITDA definitions are doing a great deal of work.

#### [TTGT-5]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Total revenues $486,791 [FY25] vs $284,897 [FY24]... The inclusion of a full year of Former TechTarget, following its acquisition in December 2024, increased marketing, advertising services, and sponsorship revenues by $158.9 million... Total revenue $106,048 [Q1 26] vs $103,887 [Q1 25]
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K MD&A revenue reconciliation + Q1 2026 10-Q revenues table + 12/2/2024 merger close date`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 vs Q1 2025 IS the first apples-to-apples post-merger comparable. Reported +2.1% revenue growth. Within that: marketing/advertising/sponsorship $75.5M (+4.5%); intelligence subscriptions $18.7M (-1.0%); advisory services $11.8M (-6.1%); exhibitor/attendee $93K (-56%). Subscription revenue (the recurring/predictable line) is DECLINING. The blended +2% is well below management's 7.1% U.S. enterprise IT growth narrative in Item 1.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: management's growth thesis (Item 1 'Enterprise IT market acceleration: forecast 7.1% CAGR') is not corroborated by Q1 2026 organic growth of +2% with subscription and advisory revenue lines DECLINING. The merger thesis was that Informa Tech Digital Businesses + legacy TechTarget combined would unlock revenue synergies and accelerate growth; the first clean quarter shows decel vs market expectation. This is a forward-quarter underdelivery that the issuer has not framed as such.

#### [TTGT-6]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Restructuring Plan included the net reduction of approximately 10% of the Company's colleague base... Our Chief Executive Officer provides services to the Company through a secondment agreement rather than being directly employed by us
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K Item 1A Risk Factors + Stockholders Agreement summary + CEO Form 4 ownership profile`
- **M_value**: (1) CEO Gary Nugent is seconded from Informa PLC parent (not directly employed by TTGT); (2) Informa retains consent rights over M&A, asset sales, debt issuance, security issuance, and CEO termination/appointment pursuant to Stockholders Agreement; (3) Informa is the lender on the $250M revolver; (4) Informa supplies the Data Sharing Agreement underlying TTGT's growth thesis; (5) Informa is locked up from selling its TTGT stock until 2nd anniversary of close (December 2026) — i.e., approximately 7 months from the cutoff date. Form 4 data shows CEO Nugent had ZERO direct ownership until his 9/12/2025 4,200-share open-market purchase ($24.5K).
- **interpretation**: Mode B: the issuer is structurally a captive subsidiary with thin public float. Five layers of parent-affiliate dependency (CEO secondee, controlled-company governance, related-party debt, related-party data feed, parent lockup expiring December 2026) mean the minority public stockholders face significant agency risk. The CEO's $24.5K open-market purchase is the only direct-ownership economic alignment of the top executive — small in absolute terms. The December 2026 lockup expiry is a calendar overhang: Informa becomes free to begin reducing its position roughly 7 months after the cutoff, which is the typical pattern that triggers float overhang and discount compression.

#### [TTGT-7]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: discount rate used in the impairment test for the reporting units ranged from 21.5% to 25.5% under both the pre- and post-reorganization assessments
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `Q1 2026 10-Q Note 4 (Goodwill) + 10-K critical audit matter`
- **M_value**: 21.5-25.5% WACC implies a risk-adjusted cost of capital well above the ~10-13% range typical for B2B information services / digital marketing peers. This is a 'cost of equity in a near-distressed business' rate. Sensitivity disclosures show that small changes (1% in discount rate, 10% in growth rate) would have FULLY impaired remaining goodwill in multiple reporting units. Bluefin: 1.7% decrease in forecasted revenue growth rate would have impaired all remaining goodwill. Industry Dive: 10.0% decrease in growth rate would have increased impairment by $11.1M. NetLine: 6.3% decrease in growth rate would have eliminated all remaining goodwill.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: the discount rates the auditor and management used (21.5-25.5%) are themselves an admission that the reporting units are valued as if near-distressed. Remaining goodwill ($1.1M post-Q1-2026) is one bad quarter or one model-input flex away from full write-off. This is a quantitative tell that the forensic distress is priced into the audit assumptions, even if the going-concern language hasn't been triggered.

#### [TTGT-8]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: $725.5 million... of net intangible assets as of December 31, 2025... Amortization expense increased $41.8 million, or 87%, in fiscal 2025 compared to fiscal 2024
- **f**: 
- **M_source**: `10-K Note on Intangible Assets + 10-Q Note 6 (Intangible Assets) confirmation`
- **M_value**: Goodwill went from $973.4M to $1.1M = effectively zero. Intangibles still $725.5M = ~57% of pre-merger goodwill+intangibles aggregate. Q1 2026 10-Q confirms the Company tested long-lived assets for impairment and did NOT find one as of March 31, 2026. But: a reporting unit's intangibles can survive an impairment that wipes its goodwill if the asset-group undiscounted cash flow test (a much weaker test than fair-value-of-reporting-unit) is barely satisfied. Annual amortization is ~$90M+ run-rate, which compresses reported operating income by ~$90M/yr vs cash earnings — exactly the kind of non-cash overhang that obscures whether the post-merger entity is structurally profitable.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: $725.5M of customer-relationship and developed-technology intangibles sit on the balance sheet after the goodwill has been written to ~zero. The asset-group impairment test (undiscounted cash flow) gives intangibles a longer life than the goodwill fair-value test, but the directional logic is the same: if reporting units cannot support fair value above carrying for goodwill purposes, the underlying intangibles are also at risk. This is a forward impairment overhang of $725M, partially offset by ~$90M/yr amortization that would also reduce future earnings drag on impairment.

## USPH — U.S. Physical Therapy, Inc.
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.45
**Sector**: Healthcare  |  **Industry**: Medical Care Facilities
**Filing**: 0001140361-26-007170_10K.txt  (2026-02-26)

### Summary
USPH presents a defensive-healthcare name in mid-drawdown (-32.2%) with a MIXED disclosure profile. The FY25 10-K shows genuine operating growth (revenue +16.3%, USPH NI +26%, operating income +37.6%) but the immediately-following Q1 2026 10-Q reveals a single-quarter operating inflection (operating income -36%, USPH NI -49%, GAAP EPS $(0.12) vs $0.80). The single most diagnostic finding is the CFO transition under-disclosure: Carey Hendrickson was omitted from the 2026 LTIP eligibility list on March 13, 2026 (effective March 9), disposed of 10,000 shares on March 10, 2026 (code D), and resigned effective April 24, 2026 - yet there is NO standalone 8-K Item 5.02 announcing the resignation; first public disclosure was buried in the Q1 10-Q subsequent-events note (May 8, 2026). Other Mode-B findings: the 60-clinic Metro hospital-alliance conversion (8.9% of network) lacks any revenue/EBITDA bridge; the Q1 dividend ($0.46) was 138% of GAAP NI and funded via $44M revolver draw; share-repurchase authorization sat unused as stock fell below the Nov 2025 buyback price ($68.45); ERP implementation will run through year-end 2026 overlapping CFO transition. Form 4 evidence is mixed: Director Kathleen Gilmartin made TWO legitimate code-P open-market purchases (Sept 2024: $172K; Nov 20 2025: $276K @ $69 INTO the drawdown - a genuine positive signal) but the entire C-suite (CEO/CFO/President/COO) made ZERO code-P purchases over 21 months. The May 20 2026 5-director Form-4 cluster (5 days pre-cutoff) is annual code-A grants at $61.61 fair value, NOT open-market purchases - this is the kind of pattern that could mislead a less-careful reader. Net verdict: NEUTRAL leaning slightly negative - real growth franchise with credible director-buy datapoint (Gilmartin) but multiple Form-S underdisclosures (CFO timing, Metro economics, dividend sustainability) and absence of C-suite conviction-buy at distressed levels. Composite suggests NEUTRAL tier rather than SHORT-tier - the company is not lying about a broken franchise, but it is under-narrating a transition.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [USPH-01-fy25-growth-narrative]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY25 MD&A: 'Total net revenue for the 2025 Year increased $109.6 million, or 16.3%, to $781.0 million from $671.3 million... Net income attributable to our shareholders ... was $39.6 million for the 2025 Year compared to $31.4 million for the 2024 Year.' Operating income +37.6% to $86.7M. Clinic count rolled from 761 to 783; visits 6.15M vs 5.35M (+14.9%); net rate per visit $105.76 vs $104.71 (+1.0%).
- **f**: Mode B - verify whether FY25 growth/operating-leverage trajectory persisted into Q1 2026 (issuer chose to file 10-K Feb 26 2026 with growth framing while Q1 2026 was already partially in progress; subsequent 10-Q Q1 2026 is the verification source).
- **M_source**: `edgar_subsequent_10Q (acc 0001140361-26-020025, filed 2026-05-08); internal claim verification of 10-K Year P&L`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 net revenue $198.3M vs Q1 2025 $183.8M (+7.9%), but operating income $12.5M vs $19.6M (-36.4%); USPH-shareholder net income $5.04M vs $9.90M (-49%); basic/diluted EPS $(0.12) vs $0.80 (NEGATIVE swing of $0.92/sh, mainly driven by GAAP earnings-per-share effect of increased value of redeemable non-controlling interest, plus a $2.0M earn-out revaluation loss vs $4.8M gain prior year and ~$2.0M of ERP-implementation/acquisition costs in corporate overhead). Net rate per visit only +0.8% ($106.49 vs $105.66); same-store revenue not separately disclosed for PT segment; gross margin compressed to 16.5% from 16.9%; salary/visit and operating cost/visit BOTH up 1.1%-1.7%.
- **interpretation**: FY25 growth narrative was real on the topline but the operating leverage flipped in Q1 2026 (margin -40bp, operating income -36%, GAAP EPS negative). The 10-K's confident framing (+37.6% operating income, share repurchases as 'confidence signal') is contradicted by the immediately-following quarter's collapse in profitability. Most of the drawdown is therefore reconcilable to actual deterioration disclosed in Q1 2026, not a hidden issue.

#### [USPH-02-share-repurchase-confidence-claim]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY25 (filed 2026-02-26): 'We repurchased 81,322 of our own shares of common stock for total consideration of $5.6 million on the open market in the three months ended December 31, 2025... demonstrates our focus on enhancing shareholder value as well as our confidence in the long-term prospects of the Company.' Average repurchase price $68.45 in Nov 2025. $19.4M remaining authorization at Dec 31 2025.
- **f**: Mode B - if management has genuine 'confidence in long-term prospects', the buyback should accelerate as the stock fell from $68 (Nov 2025) toward ~$60-61 (mid-May 2026, implied by drawdown). Verify whether repurchases continued in Q1 2026 / through cutoff.
- **M_source**: `edgar_subsequent_10Q balance sheet + cash-flow statement (acc 0001140361-26-020025)`
- **M_value**: Q1 2026 10-Q balance sheet: Treasury stock line unchanged between Dec 31 2025 and Mar 31 2026 (no incremental treasury purchases). Cash flow from financing activities Q1 2026 shows no share-repurchase line; the $19.4M authorization went unused while the stock fell another ~10-20% from the $68.45 Nov 2025 buyback level. Corporate buyback STOPPED at the same window that director Gilmartin bought (P-code) at $69 on 2025-11-20.
- **interpretation**: Management's 'confidence' framing of the Q4 2025 buyback ($5.6M at $68.45) was undercut by Q1 2026 inaction - $19.4M authorization sat unused as the stock fell. Either (a) the credit-facility refinancing on April 14 2026 imposed near-term restrictions, (b) cash conservation took priority (cash down from $35.6M Dec to $28.4M Mar), or (c) management's conviction at $68 didn't extend to $61. None of these are fatal but the 'confidence' narrative is weaker than the 10-K framing implied.

#### [USPH-03-credit-facility-refinancing-claim]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: 10-K FY25: existing Third Amended and Restated Credit Agreement provides for $325.0M total ($175M revolver + $150M term loan) maturing June 22 2027. Q1 2026 10-Q (May 8 2026): 'On April 14, 2026, the Company closed a $450 million, five-year credit facility that includes a $175 million term loan and a $275 million revolver with a maturity date of April 14, 2031.' Company states 'in compliance with all of the covenants contained in the Credit Agreement.'
- **f**: Mode A + B - existence check of new facility AND verify whether the upsize/refinancing was elective (growth capacity) or driven by covenant headroom / near-term refinancing risk.
- **M_source**: `8-K acc 0000885978-26-000017 (filed 2026-04-15) + Q1 2026 10-Q Note 9 - internal claim verification`
- **M_value**: PASS on existence - new $450M facility closed April 14 2026 (+38% capacity, 4 years of additional runway). Term loan amortization schedule benign (0.625% Y1-2, rising to 1.875% Y5). Performance pricing grid tied to Consolidated Leverage Ratio. Revolver balance $74.5M (Mar 31 2026) vs $30.5M (Dec 31 2025) - i.e., revolver utilization +$44M in one quarter to fund Q1 acquisitions / dividend / working capital. Total debt $204.8M Mar 2026 vs $162.5M Dec 2025 (+26% in one quarter).
- **interpretation**: Refinancing executed cleanly 14 months ahead of maturity at improved terms; no covenant pressure disclosed. The +$44M revolver draw in Q1 2026 is consistent with funding M&A (Jan 2026 acquisitions) and dividend at $0.46/quarter while cash was being conserved. Mild concern: leverage rising as profitability dipped, but well below covenant threshold.

#### [USPH-04-dividend-policy-continuity]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY25: Board increased dividend to $0.46/quarter for Q1 2026 ('first quarter of 2026 at the higher rate'), payable April 10 2026; 'There is no assurance that future dividends will be declared.' FY25 dividends $6.84M/quarter x 4 = ~$27.3M in declared dividends.
- **f**: Mode B - check if dividend was sustained at $0.46 on the May 6 2026 Q1 report (when EPS came in NEGATIVE at $(0.12)) - sustainability vs payout-coverage check.
- **M_source**: `8-K acc 0000885978-26-000019 (filed 2026-05-06) - direct disclosure verification`
- **M_value**: PASS on existence - dividend maintained at $0.46/share declared May 6 2026 payable June 12 2026. However: Q1 2026 USPH-shareholder net income was $5.04M; dividend declared ~$6.97M (15.16M sh x $0.46) = payout ratio 138% on GAAP net income (and -383% on EPS - because EPS is negative due to redeemable NCI revaluation). Implied dividend funded from cash / revolver draw rather than earnings in Q1.
- **interpretation**: Dividend continuity is real (Mode A pass), but sustainability framing is misleading: with Q1 negative EPS and dividend funded from credit-line draws, the 10-K's 'we will continue to increase our dividend' implication is being supported by leverage rather than free cash flow. Not a covenant breach yet but inconsistent with the 'confidence' narrative.

#### [USPH-05-cfo-departure-disclosure-timing]  severity = **RED_FLAG_NEGATIVE**
- **R**: 10-K FY25 (filed 2026-02-26) lists Mr. Carey Hendrickson as Chief Financial Officer with no mention of resignation or transition; 10-K covers compensation arrangements with Hendrickson; 8-K of 2026-03-13 grants 2026 RSU awards to senior management (Reading CEO, Williams President, Reeve COO West, Binstein EVP GC) but NOTABLY OMITS Hendrickson from the eligibility list.
- **f**: Mode B - the Mar 13 2026 8-K omitting the CFO from the 2026 LTIP eligibility list is a tell that his exit was already known internally six weeks before the April 24 2026 effective resignation date. Verify what was disclosed when.
- **M_source**: `8-K acc 0000885978-26-000014 (Mar 13 2026); 10-K Item 10 (Feb 26 2026); Q1 2026 10-Q subsequent-events note (acc 0001140361-26-020025); Form 3 for Curtis 2026-04-28 (acc 0001193125-26-187817)`
- **M_value**: Chronology: (i) Feb 26 2026 10-K - no signal of CFO change; (ii) Mar 13 2026 - CFO Hendrickson omitted from 2026 LTIP grant list (effective Mar 9); (iii) Mar 10 2026 - Form 4 shows Hendrickson disposed 10,000 shares (code D - non-open-market disposition to issuer, likely return-of-grant or gift); (iv) April 24 2026 - Hendrickson resigned (per Q1 10-Q subsequent-events disclosure); (v) April 28 2026 - Form 3 for interim CFO Jason Curtis. The CFO resignation was first disclosed via the May 8 2026 Q1 10-Q, ~5-7 weeks after it was effectively decided. EDGAR shows NO interim 8-K Item 5.02 specifically announcing the CFO resignation.
- **interpretation**: CFO departure is one of the most material Item 5.02 events under Form 8-K. The omission from the Mar 13 2026 LTIP grant strongly implies the decision was internal by early March 2026, yet first 'public' disclosure was buried in the May 8 2026 10-Q subsequent-events note with no separately filed 8-K announcing it. This is a Form-S underdisclosure pattern - the issuer chose the path that minimized market focus on a CFO transition during a 32% drawdown.

#### [USPH-06-strategic-alliance-revenue-recognition-risk]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K FY25 and Q1 2026 10-Q: 'On February 2, 2026, we announced a 10-year strategic alliance between our subsidiary partner, Metro, and a prominent New York hospital system, whereby 60 of Metro's existing outpatient physical therapy clinics in New York will become part of the hospital system's clinical services network. The alliance is expected to begin operations with an initial group of clinics in mid-2026, with all 60 clinics anticipated to be operational by year-end 2026.' Second alliance announced Feb 25 2026 (subsidiary partner + local hospital, 10 clinics).
- **f**: Mode B - 60 of Metro's clinics being 'hospital-system clinical services' is structurally different from owned/operated clinic revenue (likely transitions to management-contract/service-fee economics + hospital-controlled billing). Verify if revenue recognition transitions away from gross patient revenue toward management fee, and whether 10-K/10-Q quantify the revenue/EBITDA impact.

- **M_value**: Neither the 10-K (Feb 26 2026) nor the Q1 10-Q (May 8 2026) quantifies the revenue or EBITDA impact of converting 60 Metro clinics (out of 783 total = 7.7% of network) from owned-clinic gross revenue to hospital-system contractual revenue. Total Other Revenue (which includes management-contract revenue) was $130.6M (16.7% of total) FY25, but the Q1 2026 disclosure does NOT break out how the Metro alliance will reshape this mix. Metro acquired Oct 31 2024 with $7.4M of unresolved contingent consideration at Dec 31 2025; Metro's clinical-services network conversion in mid-2026 could trigger contingent-consideration revaluation and segment-revenue reclassification.
- **interpretation**: Strategic alliances are framed as growth catalysts but the disclosed structure (clinics becoming part of hospital system's clinical services network) likely converts gross patient revenue into management/service-fee revenue at lower top-line value, potentially mid-2026 onward. The absence of any quantitative bridge - even a 'we expect this to reduce gross patient revenue by $X and add other revenue of $Y' - leaves the market without a reasonable estimate. For 7.7% of the clinic base over 18 months, this is a material undisclosed-revenue-reshape signal. Likely contributing to the drawdown if the buy-side has independently modeled an unfavorable conversion.

- **M_value**: **TWO open-market PURCHASES (code P) by Director Kathleen A. Gilmartin** in the 24-month window: (1) Sept 3 2024: 2,000 sh @ $86.09 = $172K; (2) **Nov 20 2025: 4,000 sh @ $69.00 = $276K** (into the drawdown, ~32% of her prior holding). Post-transaction Gilmartin held 24,786 then 27,092 sh (after May 2026 grants). **NO other insider made any open-market code-P purchases.** Other notable patterns: (a) CEO Reading sold 2,000 sh @ $83.53 on Sept 4 2025 - last CEO transaction is a SELL, not a buy; (b) COO Reeve sold near-monthly throughout - Aug 2024, Nov 2024, Mar 2025, May 2025, Aug 2025 (twice), Nov 2025, Mar 2026 (twice); on Mar 11 2026 Reeve's nonderivative direct holdings dropped to 1 share then 0 (post=0) before grants; (c) CFO Hendrickson sold 698 sh on Aug 25 2025 at $86.11 and then disposed 10,000 sh on Mar 10 2026 (code D, $0 px - return/gift) just before his April 24 2026 resignation; (d) Director cluster on 2026-05-20: 5 directors received 2,306 sh each at $61.61 - this is code A (annual award/grant, post-annual-meeting May 19), NOT P purchases - $61.61 is the grant FV not a market purchase price. (e) New director Peter Francis Minan filed Form 3 on May 19 2026 (board addition).

However it is OFFSET by: (i) zero C-suite open-market purchases (CEO/President/COO/CFO all either sold or did nothing), (ii) recurring COO Reeve selling that took direct holdings near zero before grants, (iii) the soon-departing CFO disposing 10K shares one month before resignation, (iv) the May 20 2026 director cluster being grants, not market purchases (a reader could be misled by the dense Form 4 cluster five days pre-cutoff if they didn't parse the code). Net: one credible buyer (Gilmartin), broad C-suite distribution. Insider signal is therefore a moderate positive on Gilmartin alone, dragged neutral by the rest of the cohort.

The 2026 grants are significantly upsized YoY. The CFO is uniformly absent from these arrangements.
- **interpretation**: Mode B reading: the 2026 LTIP structure looks more like an upsized CEO/President/COO retention package keyed to a 'Qualified Retirement' clause - consistent with succession planning being active for the CEO (21+ years tenure). Combined with the CFO resignation and new director additions in May 2026, this is a board / senior-management turnover pattern that is NOT framed by the 10-K and is consistent with the equity-market drawdown reflecting governance / continuity uncertainty rather than operating deterioration alone. Not a fraud signal - rather an under-narrated transition.

#### [USPH-09-erp-implementation-costs]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K and Q1 2026 10-Q: 'We are in the process of implementing a new enterprise resource planning (ERP) system designed to support certain human resources and accounting functions... We expect to continue the phased implementation of the ERP system over time while maintaining existing systems and controls during the transition.' Q1 10-Q: 'Implementation costs associated with the new financial and human resources system are expected to continue through the end of 2026.'
- **f**: Mode B - ERP implementation cost intersecting with a CFO resignation is a classic risk pattern (internal-controls scope expansion during finance-leader turnover); verify whether 10-K Item 9A internal-controls disclosure flagged any concerns AND whether Q1 corporate costs reconcile with the ERP narrative.

- **M_value**: Q1 2026 corporate office costs $18.27M vs Q1 2025 $16.25M (+12.5% YoY, vs revenue +7.9%); the issuer carves out $0.7M of ERP+acquisition costs and presents adjusted corporate cost ratio of 8.8% vs 8.5%. So even on adjusted basis, corporate-cost ratio expanded 30bp YoY in a quarter the CFO was already on the way out. ERP implementation expected to run through year-end 2026 - i.e., entire fiscal year of corporate-cost drag overlapping with an interim CFO. No Item 9A internal-controls material weakness flagged in 10-K.
- **interpretation**: No internal-controls failure disclosed (PASS on Item 9A existence), but the cost drag and timing - ERP implementation through 2026 + CFO resignation + interim CFO + ongoing acquisition integration - represents an elevated risk of restatement / control-deficiency emergence by year-end 2026 that the 10-K's '...will improve operational efficiency' framing minimizes.

#### [USPH-10-non-controlling-interest-eps-headwind]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: 10-K FY25: 'Our improved performance in 2025 increased the value of these ownership interests, net of taxes, by $18.0 million, which reduced earnings per share. Earnings per share was $1.42 for the 2025 Year and $1.84 for the 2024 Year.' I.e., GAAP net income +27% but EPS -23%.
- **f**: Mode B - the partnership-structure economics mean rising clinic-partnership profitability drives a redeemable-NCI revaluation that mechanically depresses EPS. Q1 2026 EPS was $(0.12) on $5.04M USPH net income. Verify the NCI mechanic was the driver and whether it represents a structural EPS-vs-NI disconnect not adequately framed in 10-K.
- **M_source**: `10-K + Q1 2026 10-Q EPS reconciliation - internal claim verification`
- **M_value**: 10-K disclosed the NCI mechanic clearly: 'Net income $58.0M / USPH NI $39.6M / EPS $1.42 ($1.84 in 2024) ...redeemable NCI increased by $18.0M net of tax which reduced EPS.' Q1 2026 follows the same pattern: USPH net income $5.04M positive but EPS $(0.12) negative because the redeemable-NCI revaluation flowed through EPS. The Mode A disclosure is honest. Mode B reading: this is a structural feature of USPH's partnership-clinic model - in good operating years, EPS UNDER-states economic earnings (because rising NCI fair value mechanically depresses headline EPS). On a drawdown, this also means screen-based EPS-multiple investors will see a more punitive picture than the underlying business produces.
- **interpretation**: Honest, complete disclosure of a real accounting mechanic. The 2025 EPS decline despite 27% net-income growth is mechanically explained, not a sleight-of-hand. Q1 2026 negative EPS is partly an artifact of the same mechanic. This is actually a contrarian-positive signal: the headline EPS understates underlying economic performance, and a portion of the drawdown is screen-driven rather than fundamentals-driven.

The bear case is operational: Q1 2026 revenue went from +11% growth (FY25) to FLAT, with Commercial Services -3.5% (travel-demand softness, RAC concentration). Capital-return continues at corporate level ($50.7M Q1 2026 buyback) but the insider/issuer asymmetry is a soft negative signal. Also: 33% director-withhold vote on Rexford, $72M NYCDOT receivable, undisclosed new contract pricing. Verdict: NEUTRAL leaning slightly negative — fundamentals real but momentum broken with no insider confidence backing; would not classify as honesty-alpha SHORT (no lying) but no LONG-tier conviction signal either.

## XRX — Xerox Holdings Corporation
**Bucket**: DISTRESSED LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.55
**Sector**: Technology  |  **Industry**: Information Technology Services
**Filing**: 0001770450-26-000009_10K.txt  (2026-03-17)

### Summary
XRX is a paradox: severe operational/structural distress signals (90% dividend cut in 2025, two consecutive billion-dollar GAAP net losses, $537M DTA valuation allowance, Feb-2026 brand-IP financing with TPG/Angelo Gordon at SOFR+8.125% secured by the Xerox trademark, secular print decline still ~-4% YoY on pro forma) directly contradicted by an unusually clean Form 4 tape — six open-market PURCHASES totaling $502K by the CEO (Bandrowczak), CFO (Gecaj), COO (Bruno), and a director (Letier) over 24 months, ZERO open-market sales, at prices ($3.95-4.48) at/near the all-time low. Disclosure quality is honest; the issuer records the bad news (dividend cut, DTA writedown, IP-JV, warrant dividend with debt-for-equity option) without obfuscation, but the narrative framing under-emphasizes how distressed the brand-IP financing actually is. The framework's honesty-alpha logic does NOT short XRX: management is committing personal capital alongside shareholders and the going-concern + ICFR opinions are clean. However, the structural distress (leverage 3.4-6x vs <3.0x target; brand-IP pledged) is severe enough that this is NOT a clean LONG either. Verdict: NEUTRAL with LONG-tier insider tape — honest disclosure of distress, with the insider buying being the strongest contrarian indicator.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [XRX-01-insider-purchases-c-suite]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Management commitment to Xerox's 'Reinvention' strategy and Lexmark integration: 'we aim to take a best-in breed approach and offer the best of both companies to our clients, while generating more than $300 million of expected run rate synergies by the end of 2027' (FY2025 10-K, MD&A Strategy).
- **f**: Parse Form 4 ownership.xml for code-P (open-market purchases) and code-S (open-market sales) by named executives over 24 months ending 2026-05-22 (last Form 4 pre-cutoff).
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse, 24-mo window 2024-05-25 to 2026-05-22)`
- **M_value**: 6 open-market PURCHASES (code P), ZERO open-market SALES (code S), across 69 Form 4 filings in 24 months. Cluster #1 on 2025-05-23 (post Q1 print, open window): CEO Steven Bandrowczak 22,300 sh @ $4.48 = $99,904; COO John G. Bruno 25,000 sh @ $4.38 = $109,500; CFO Mirlanda Gecaj 10,000 sh @ $4.41 = $44,100; director Scott Letier 25,000 sh @ $4.43 = $110,750. Cluster #2 on 2025-08-01 (post Q2 print): CFO Gecaj +5,179 sh @ $4.03 = $20,871; director Letier +29,600 sh @ $3.95 = $116,920. Aggregate $502,045 of personal-cash buying by CEO+CFO+COO+director, all at $3.95-$4.48, BEFORE the Q3 2025 8-K dividend cut to $0.10 and BEFORE the Feb 2026 TPG brand-IP JV. No 10b5-1 plan disclosure on these tickets — these appear to be discretionary opportunistic buys.
- **interpretation**: STRONG honest-insider commitment signal. The C-suite triumvirate (CEO, CFO, COO) plus a director put personal cash to work near the lows in May-Aug 2025. CEO Bandrowczak's purchase at $4.48 was at the all-time-low end of XRX history. This is exactly the LONG-tier Form 4 evidence the framework looks for: code-P, clustered, by C-suite, into the drawdown, with ZERO offsetting sales. The buys do NOT precede positive news — Q3 brought further dividend cuts and Q4 brought the brand-IP financing. Management was wrong about timing the bottom (stock is now at -54.8%), but they were not selling into bad news.

#### [XRX-02-insider-vs-calendar-no-sales]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: in a -54.8% drawdown with multiple negative capital events (dividend cuts, brand-IP financing, $1.0B net loss), normal selling pressure from option exercises, departures, and 10b5-1 plans should be visible.
- **f**: Cross-reference Form 4 transaction codes vs 8-K capital-event calendar 2024-05-25 to 2026-05-22.
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 codes vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: 24-month code mix across 69 Form 4s / 140+ non-derivative transactions: code A (grant) 27; code M (option exercise) 85; code F (tax withhold from RSU vest) 42; code P (open-market purchase) 6; code S (open-market sale) 0; code G (gift) 0. No 144 sale-intent filings linked to executive officers in the registered 144 list. CEO Bandrowczak's only non-routine transactions are the May 2025 PURCHASE. CFO Gecaj's only non-routine transactions are the May/Aug 2025 PURCHASES. Zero sales despite multiple bad-news 8-Ks (2025-01-28 dividend cut #1, 2025-04-11 8-K, 2025-07-31 8-K, 2025-10-30 8-K, 2025-12-09 8-K, 2026-01-21 warrant dividend, 2026-02-17 brand-IP JV).
- **interpretation**: The complete absence of discretionary sales by any insider across 24 months — including post all the major bad-news catalysts — is a strong honesty signal. If management knew the brand-IP JV or further dividend cuts were coming and wanted to monetize equity, they had ample windows. They didn't. Combined with XRX-01, this is asymmetric P:S = infinite (zero sales). NOT the IONQ pattern.

#### [XRX-03-dividend-cut-90pct-two-stage]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note: 'During the first quarter of 2025, the annual cash dividend on common stock was reduced to $0.50 per share from $1.00 per share... In the second quarter of 2025, the annual cash dividend on common stock was further reduced to $0.10 per share from $0.50 per share.'
- **f**: Verify two-stage dividend cut against announcements; assess capital-allocation framing in narrative.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note on Stockholders' Equity)`
- **M_value**: CONFIRMED: $1.00 -> $0.50 -> $0.10 annualized, a 90% cumulative cut over two consecutive quarters in 2025. Capped-call conversion-price ratchet from $27.51 down to $20.84 was triggered by these cuts. 10-K narrative reframes this as 'Reduction of debt is our primary capital allocation priority' but does not explicitly enumerate that this is a 90% dividend reduction in a single year.
- **interpretation**: Two-stage dividend cut within six months is highly atypical for a former dividend-aristocrat-style cash cow like Xerox. The framing in the FY2025 10-K is muted — they spend more ink on the Lexmark synergy story than on the dividend trajectory. Severe disclosure-under-emphasis, but the cut itself is honestly recorded. Not a fraud finding — a distress finding.

#### [XRX-04-brand-IP-financing-with-tpg-angelo-gordon]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note 26 Subsequent Events: 'On February 17, 2026... Xerox Corporation contributed... the trademarks in respect of the Xerox brand (collectively, the Contributed IP), to IPCo Holdings... in exchange for $405 million aggregate principal amount of senior secured term loans... Term Loans bear interest at a per annum rate equal to either (a) a base rate plus a margin of 7.125% for ABR Loans, or (b) the applicable term SOFR rate plus a margin of 8.125% for SOFR Loans... The Licensees are required to pay IPCo a royalty fee equal to 2.0% of specified consolidated revenue generated by Holdings and its subsidiaries from the Contributed IP.'
- **f**: Mode B — given the issuer characterizes this as a 'joint venture' framed as opportunistic capital structure work, verify what the structure actually is: who controls IPCo, what is the effective cost, what are the cross-default and covenant implications.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 26 + Q1 2026 10-Q VIE disclosure)`
- **M_value**: Q1 10-Q confirms Xerox Corporation BEGAN CONSOLIDATING IPCo Holdings as a VIE in Q1 2026 (i.e., Xerox is the primary beneficiary; IPCo carries $902M assets / $452M liabilities at 3/31/26). Effective economics: $405M senior secured term loan + $45M Class A units sold to TPG/Angelo Gordon for $405M cash distribution to parent (less $42M of fees = $363M net), against the encumbrance of the entire Xerox brand trademark portfolio worldwide. Interest cost SOFR+8.125% (i.e., ~13.5% at current SOFR) plus 4.5% quarterly amortization plus 2.0% revenue royalty paid into a reserve account. TPG/Angelo Gordon is a distressed-credit specialist; this structure (brand-IP holdco, SOFR+8.125%, revenue royalty, reserve account) is a textbook distressed-financing template (cf. Revlon 2020, J. Crew, Neiman Marcus 2018-2019 brand-IP boxes). 10-K does not enumerate the effective all-in cost or characterize it as distress finance.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: the disclosure is technically complete but the narrative framing ('joint venture arrangement', 'opportunistically addressing capital structure') understates that this is a distressed brand-IP financing at ~13.5% gross cost secured by the most valuable intangible asset Xerox owns. The 7.125%/8.125% spreads above SOFR/ABR are far above the cost of the existing 5.5% senior unsecured 2028 notes — i.e., Xerox could not refinance through conventional channels at acceptable terms. This is the single most important signal in the filing that the company's debt stack is approaching the limits of what unsecured markets will absorb.

#### [XRX-05-warrant-dividend-recap-feb-2026]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Note 26: 'In January 2026 the Board of Directors approved a pro-rata distribution of warrants... On February 12, 2026, 77,271,234 warrants were issued and distributed, at no cost... Each warrant entitles the holder to purchase one share of Common Stock at an exercise price of $8.00 per share... and may be exercised (a) for cash... and (b) using designated outstanding Xerox debt securities (the Designated Notes).'
- **f**: Mode B — interpret the dual-currency (cash OR existing debt) exercise feature as a structural debt-management tool.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Note 26 Warrant Dividend)`
- **M_value**: 77.27M warrants struck at $8.00, exercisable IN CASH OR IN DEBT, with the company having sole discretion to designate which debt series are 'Designated Notes.' If exercised in debt, this gives Xerox a discretionary tool to retire face-value debt with new equity at a fixed $8 strike — effectively a forced exchange offer at management's option. Common stock currently trades ~$3 (implied from -54.8% drawdown vs ~2024 levels). Warrants are deeply out-of-the-money but have 2-year life and can expire early if VWAP >= 100% of strike for 20 of 30 trading days. The dual-currency feature is highly unusual for a stock-dividend warrant.
- **interpretation**: Mode B: this is a contingent debt-for-equity exchange dressed up as a warrant dividend. It signals (a) management explicitly anticipates the equity may need to be the currency to retire debt, and (b) they want a pre-built mechanism to do so without needing a fresh consent solicitation. Honest disclosure but ominous structural implication consistent with the IP-financing-at-distress-pricing theme.

#### [XRX-06-organic-revenue-decline-masked-by-acquisitions]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K Financial Overview: 'Total revenue of $7.0 billion in 2025 increased 12.9% reflecting a 15.5-percentage point benefit and 6.5-percentage point benefit from the Lexmark Acquisition and ITsavvy, respectively.'
- **f**: Mode B — decompose reported growth to test underlying organic trend.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 7 segment tables + Q1 10-Q pro forma)`
- **M_value**: FY2025 Print & Other segment revenue was $6,277M vs $5,864M FY2024 (+7.0%), BUT Lexmark added 15.5pp -> implied organic Print declined approx -8.5pp YoY, consistent with the multi-year secular decline pattern (FY2024 Print -13.3% CC, FY2023 Print -8.2% CC). Q1 2026 10-Q is more revealing: 'On a pro forma basis total revenue declined 3.7%... post sale revenue declined 3.8%.' I.e., even INCLUDING Lexmark in both periods, the combined business is shrinking ~4% YoY in Q1 2026.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: headline +12.9% revenue growth obscures continuing -8% organic Print decline. The Lexmark deal is now being used to mask, not reverse, the secular trend. Q1 2026 pro-forma -3.7% (with Lexmark in both periods) is the cleanest read and confirms the combined entity is still in decline. Disclosure is technically present in the segment tables but the narrative emphasis is entirely on Lexmark-driven optical growth.

#### [XRX-07-net-loss-and-deferred-tax-valuation-allowance]  severity = **SEVERE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: 10-K MD&A: 'Net (loss) for 2025 was $(1,029) million... primarily reflects higher Income tax expense, as a result of the establishment of valuation allowances of $537 million against certain deferred tax assets to reflect their realizability.'
- **f**: Mode A verify reported loss; Mode B interpret the DTA valuation allowance.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K consolidated statements)`
- **M_value**: CONFIRMED: $(1,029)M net loss FY2025; FY2024 $(1,321)M (which itself included $(1,058)M pre-tax goodwill impairment). Two consecutive years of >$1B GAAP losses. The $537M deferred-tax-asset valuation allowance is the auditor/management conclusion that it is no longer more-likely-than-not that Xerox will generate enough taxable income in the relevant jurisdictions to use these DTAs — i.e., management's own internal forecast no longer supports recovery of the tax-loss-carryforward asset.
- **interpretation**: A $537M DTA valuation allowance is an extremely strong forward-looking negative signal because it is management's OWN admission that future taxable income is unlikely. Combined with two consecutive billion-dollar GAAP losses, this is a severe distress indicator. The disclosure is honest — they recorded it — but the narrative consistently emphasizes adjusted (non-GAAP) profitability and never highlights the DTA writedown as a forward signal.

#### [XRX-08-late-10K-filing-nt-10k]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Filing chronology: Form NT 10-K filed 2026-03-02 (notification of inability to timely file); 10-K filed 2026-03-17 (15 days late vs typical Feb pattern; prior year 10-K filed 2025-02-24).
- **f**: Mode B — determine reason for late filing.
- **M_source**: `edgar_fts (EDGAR filings index)`
- **M_value**: NT 10-K filed 2026-03-02 indicates inability to file 10-K by the original due date (Feb 27, 2026). 10-K was ultimately filed 2026-03-17, ~18 days after original due date. The 10-K narrative does not explicitly tie the delay to any restatement or material weakness — ICFR is asserted EFFECTIVE as of 12/31/2025. The most plausible drivers given the document content: (a) Lexmark purchase accounting was still being trued up — the 10-K explicitly notes 'identified errors' in Lexmark preliminary purchase accounting with $28M goodwill increase from error correction plus $11M from measurement-period adjustments; (b) the TPG brand-IP JV closed 2026-02-17, just 10 days before the original due date, triggering subsequent-events and VIE consolidation analysis.
- **interpretation**: Late filing combined with Lexmark purchase-accounting error correction is a soft amber flag. The error correction itself is disclosed as immaterial ($28M goodwill adjustment on a multi-billion-dollar deal). However, the FY2026 10-K is the FIRST year of consolidated Lexmark + brand-IP-JV reporting, so the audit-attestation surface area is materially larger and 18 days is not egregious. Business is honestly disclosed as in transition: pro-forma organic revenue +3.6% FY25 but -2.8% H1 FY26 (with explicit attribution to JV deconsolidation, government shutdown Jan 31-Feb 3 + DHS Feb 14-Apr 30, and Rapid Solutions divestiture); backlog +6.7% YoY to $47.8B = book-to-bill >1.0x; covenants compliant; April 24, 2026 First Amendment to Credit Facility extended maturities to 2031 and added $150M revolver — strong credit signal. Severity: 6 PASS / 3 MODERATE / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 1 UNVERIFIABLE. Verdict: LONG-tier. On 2026-04-24 (15 days before Q2 10-Q filing, 32 days before cutoff) management executed FIRST AMENDMENT to Credit Facility: (i) new $1,400M TLA due Apr 2031 at SOFR+125-200bps based on first-lien leverage, (ii) existing TL re-cut into $1,591M TLB due Sep 2031 at SOFR+175bps (a stable spread), (iii) Revolver upsized $150M. Paid down $722M voluntarily through divestiture proceeds (Rapid Solutions $360M to LMT) and cash flow, then opportunistically refinanced to extend maturities and lower TLA spreads. Banks were willing to upsize the Revolver +$150M — a positive credit signal. Net leverage 3.2x with covenant headroom is appropriate for a 81% U.S.-federal-government services contractor with $47.8B backlog. This is not deception — the disclosure language is exactly correct — but the math points to a non-cash impairment risk in the 12 months ahead. ## CVCO — CAVCO INDUSTRIES, INC.
**Bucket**: CONSERVATIVE LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.8
**Sector**: Consumer Cyclical  |  **Industry**: Residential Construction
**Filing**: 0001628280-26-037782_10K.txt  (2026-05-22)

### Summary
CVCO is a -27% drawdown name where the FORENSIC TAPE strongly REJECTS the SHORT thesis. CEO Bill Boor's FIRST EVER open-market code-P PURCHASE (1,000 shares @ $495 = $495K) was executed 5 trading days after a disappointing Q3 FY26 print (net income -22% YoY, backlog -28% YoY), with two directors (Moster, Greenblatt) clustering alongside him within 48 hours = $866K aggregate cluster of code-P purchases. The CEO had ZERO open-market sales in the 18-month window. Routine RSU tax-withholding events (17 Form 4s May 19-22) are mechanically calendar-driven, NOT discretionary selling. Management subsequently announced a new $150M buyback (5/18/26) and FY26 delivered record results: revenue $2.245B (+11.4%, +6.9% organic), EPS $23.98 (+15.8%), backlog recovered to $195M. Balance sheet is effectively debt-free ($0 LT debt, $258M cash, $75M revolver undrawn, ~0.0x leverage vs 3.25x covenant), clean RSM auditor opinion, no material litigation, no going concern. Two MODERATE concerns: (a) American Homestar acquired Sept 2025 for $181M with $100M goodwill+intangibles (55% of consideration) at ~17x trailing P/E — creates future-impairment risk if industry weakens further; (b) FY26 buyback execution at ~$510 average vs cutoff $405 = capital-allocation timing inefficient. Q3 was the bad quarter and the proximate drawdown cause but Q4 recovered (net income +17% YoY, backlog +$35M sequentially). Severity: 5 PASS / 4 MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 0 UNVERIFIABLE. Verdict: LONG-tier. This is the cleanest insider-conviction setup in CVCO's history — CEO's first-ever code-P buy at $495 into a -22% Q3 print is the textbook 'honest tape' signal Signal OS is designed to detect.

Moster bought 300 sh @ $456.23 on 2026-02-02 = $136,869; (3) Director David A. Greenblatt bought 400+13+87 sh @ $462.43/$481/$500 on 2026-02-03/04 = $234,725 (he ALSO sold 4,000 sh in March 2025 at $528.88 = $2.11M, so this is a partial scaling back into the position). In the Signal OS honesty-alpha framework this is exclusionary evidence AGAINST the SHORT thesis — insiders would not pre-commit personal cash into the disclosure window if they were aware of further hidden deterioration. The fact that the stock is now ~$385-410 (estimated from -27% drawdown) means Boor is underwater on his Feb purchase, but he also DID NOT sell into the subsequent strength of FY26 record results. Annualized AH contribution ~$182M revenue / $10.4M net income, implying ~17.4x annualized P/E paid for the deal. Goodwill is 14% of FY26 total assets ($208.8M of consolidated total). Acquisition transaction costs of $5.0M expensed in SG&A. Purchase accounting is provisional (could be adjusted within 12mo). No impairment recognized in FY26.
- **interpretation**: Issuer is transparent (full purchase-price allocation, six-month contribution disclosed, pro forma provided). The 17x trailing P/E paid for AH is rich for a private manufactured-housing retailer/manufacturer in a flat industry, especially with 55% of consideration going to goodwill+intangibles. AH's 5.7% net margin is below CVCO core, suggesting the synergies are real but the standalone target was lower-quality. Mode-B risk: if industry stays flat or worsens, goodwill of $86.9M plus $13.3M intangibles could face impairment testing in FY27. Not a fraud or accounting flag — but management's prior history of acquisitions does not include large goodwill write-downs (the 10-K states 'no accumulated impairment losses related to Goodwill'), so disclosure is honest. Mild ding for capital-allocation discipline given AH was acquired at peak-cycle multiples.

Net income $44.1M vs $56.5M (-22.0%). Backlog at Dec 27, 2025 = $160M, DOWN -$37M from $197M at FY25 year-end and DOWN -28.6% from $224M at Dec 28, 2024. Backlog recovered $195M by March 28 (a $35M sequential bounce). FY26 full-year EPS $23.98 vs $20.71 (+15.8%) shows Q3 weakness was offset by Q1/Q2/Q4 strength.

Margin compression (gross margin -150bps Q3 YoY) plus -28% backlog YoY was a legitimately bad print. Issuer DISCLOSED the metrics in full (no obfuscation), and the subsequent Q4 recovery (revenue +8%, net income +17%, backlog +$35M sequentially) confirms the issue was demand-volatility-driven rather than structural. Honest reporting + insiders bought INTO this disappointment + Q4 recovered = the drawdown likely overshoots reality. Mild MODERATE for the actual weakness; no fraud/deception signal.
