## LYFT — Lyft, Inc.
**Bucket**: CONSERVATIVE LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.8
**Sector**: Technology  |  **Industry**: Software - Application
**Filing**: 0001628280-26-006960_10K.txt  (2026-02-11)

### Summary
LYFT is a -43.4% drawdown name where Form 4 forensic evidence directly and cleanly REJECTS the distress narrative. CEO David Risher executed three open-market code-P purchases ($99,996 + $99,617 + $100,179 = $299,792 total) on 2025-09-03, 2025-12-10, and 2026-02-13 — disciplined ~$100K monthly cadence in which share count INCREASED as price FELL ($19.80 -> $16.88 -> $13.38), i.e., scaling INTO weakness, all clustered AFTER earnings disclosures not before. Risher holds 11.48M shares (~0.6% basic SO). NO C-suite open-market sales in 2026 at all; CFO Brewer's only sales were Aug-Sept 2025 at $15-18 and STOPPED. The recent May 22 Form 4s (filed 3 days pre-cutoff) are all code F (RSU tax withholding) and one code G (gift to trust) — zero opportunistic selling at $13. Headline net income $2.844B is dominated by a $2.897B valuation allowance release (Mode B catch, transparently disclosed) — underlying GAAP pre-tax was ($53M) — but Adj EBITDA $528.8M (+38%), FCF $1.116B (+46%), and Active Riders 29.2M (+18%) are real. Q1 2026 accelerated: GB +19%, Active Riders +17%, Adj EBITDA +25%. Q2 2026 guide of GB +18-21% and Adj EBITDA $160-180M (margin EXPANSION) was issued 18 days before cutoff. Buyback authorization $1.75B (with $1.25B remaining), diluted share count -5.1% YoY, $1.8B unrestricted liquidity, PwC clean opinion, no going-concern, no material weakness. The -43% drawdown is sentiment-driven (AV-competitive narrative: Tesla robotaxi, Waymo) rather than fundamental — Lyft mitigates with May Mobility/Baidu/Mobileye AV partnerships and Flexdrive fleet. P:S dollar ratio 0.048 is the one moderate negative (selling exceeds buying 20:1 by $$), but the largest seller (Director Aggarwal $3.9M @ $22.30) was 10b5-1 plan-based at the LOCAL HIGH and pre-drawdown. Verdict: LONG-tier — clearest CEO-honest-insider setup in the universe alongside the strong KPI/guide acceleration. Severity: 6 PASS / 4 MODERATE / 0 SEVERE / 0 RED_FLAG / 0 UNVERIFIABLE.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

#### [LYFT-01-ceo-open-market-purchase-pattern]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Management is committed to the business and aligned with shareholders through the drawdown (implied by 10-K narrative of 'Lyft is performing while transforming' and continued buyback authorization).
- **f**: Parse all Form 4 XML filings from 2025-08-01 through 2026-05-22 (cutoff) for transaction code 'P' (open-market purchase) by named executives. Check clustering pattern (monthly cadence vs episodic), whether buying scaled with price weakness, and whether buys were positioned around informational windows (earnings/8-K).
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse, 39 Form 4s analyzed)`
- **M_value**: CEO David Risher executed THREE open-market code-P purchases during the drawdown: (1) 2025-09-03: 5,926 sh @ $16.875 = $99,996; (2) 2025-12-10: 5,030 sh @ $19.8046 = $99,617; (3) 2026-02-13: 7,490 sh @ $13.375 = $100,179. Total $299,792 of personal cash, all open-market code P, in disciplined ~$100K monthly cadence. Crucially, share count INCREASED as price FELL ($19.80 -> $16.88 -> $13.38), i.e., Risher scaled INTO weakness rather than backing off. Feb 13 buy was THREE days after the FY2025 10-K filing (Feb 11) at the lowest price of the three. Risher holds 11.48M shares post-trade — meaningful personal stake (~0.6% of basic shares outstanding). NO other officer/director executed open-market purchases. The pattern is consistent with a self-imposed monthly buying program, but the SEC code is unambiguously P (open-market) not A (grant) — these are at-risk dollars.
- **interpretation**: Strongest single positive signal in the file. CEO putting personal cash to work in a disciplined cadence that ACCELERATED (shares purchased rose) as price dropped is the textbook honest-insider alignment signal. The dollar size is modest in absolute terms ($300K) but it is the cleanest possible Form-4 evidence: open-market P-code, by the CEO, with size scaling INTO drawdown, AND clustered AFTER earnings disclosures (not before). Combined with continued $1.75B buyback program execution, this directly contradicts a 'liar/distress' narrative the -43% drawdown would imply.

#### [LYFT-02-insider-sales-asymmetry]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Implied: insider transactions independent of non-public information and not clustered before adverse disclosures.
- **f**: Aggregate dollar value of open-market sales (code S) vs purchases (code P) across all reporting insiders since 2025-08-01. Cross-reference each S transaction date against the 8-K calendar to test for pre-bad-news clustering.
- **M_source**: `form4 + insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 vs 8-K timing)`
- **M_value**: Open-market SALES (code S) total $6,202,332 across 6 insiders over 10 months. Largest: Director Prashant Aggarwal $3,892,886 on 2025-12-03/04 @ ~$22.30 (rule 10b5-1 plan exit per earlier Forms 3/4 footnote pattern). Next: SVP Lindsay Catherine Llewellyn $1,411,091; CFO Erin Brewer $487,230 (Aug-Sept 2025 only, NO sales in 2026); Chief Accounting Officer Stephen Hope $180,120; Director David Lawee $148,322; Director Jill Beggs $82,682. P:S dollar ratio = 0.048 (i.e., sales exceed buys 20:1 in $$). HOWEVER: timing analysis is important — (a) Aggarwal $3.9M block on 2025-12-03/04 was 28 days AFTER Q3 2025 earnings 8-K (2025-11-05, beat with $46M net income) at the local price peak $22.30, BEFORE the Feb 2026 drawdown; (b) Brewer's sales were Aug 20, Sept 3 2025 at $15-18, BEFORE the major drawdown (price still in mid-teens range); (c) NO CFO/CAO/CEO open-market sales in 2026 at all; (d) the 2026-02-24, 03-03, 04-21, 05-22 Form 4s by CFO Brewer, CAO Hope, SVP Llewellyn are predominantly code F (RSU tax withholding) and code A (RSU vesting grants), NOT discretionary sales. Llewellyn's two 2026 code-S transactions ($354K Feb 25 + $355K Apr 17) are visible but small.
- **interpretation**: Mixed picture. Headline dollar P:S of 0.048 is poor and would be a SHORT-tier signal in isolation. But two mitigators: (1) the largest seller is a director on a 10b5-1 program selling at the LOCAL HIGH ($22.30) — that's a feature of plan-based selling, not insider information; (2) C-suite open-market sales STOPPED in 2026 once the drawdown deepened, and the CEO STARTED buying — exactly the asymmetry we want. Net: moderately negative on raw insider dollars, but the qualitative ordering (CEO buying, CFO not selling) is positive and the timing is not pre-bad-news.

#### [LYFT-03-headline-net-income-mostly-tax-benefit]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "Net income $2,844.0 million" (FY2025 vs $22.8M FY2024) — 124x YoY growth headline; "Diluted EPS $6.81."
- **f**: Mode B (implied verification) — decompose the $2.8B net income to test whether it represents recurring operating earnings or one-time items. Compare GAAP vs adjusted measures and the tax line specifically.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K MD&A + Note on income taxes + reconciliation table)`
- **M_value**: Income before tax FY2025 = ($53.2M); BENEFIT FROM income taxes = +$2,897.3M. Net income = $2,844.0M. The benefit of $2.897B comes from RELEASE OF DEFERRED TAX ASSET VALUATION ALLOWANCE on U.S. federal and certain state deferred tax assets in Q4 2025 (issuer explicitly discloses: 'a $2.9 billion benefit from the release of our valuation allowance of U.S. federal and certain state deferred tax assets in the fourth quarter of 2025'). Underlying pre-tax operating loss was ($53M). Adjusted EBITDA $528.8M (+38% YoY); Free cash flow $1,115.6M (+46% YoY) is the cleaner cash measure. Without the valuation allowance release, GAAP net income would have been a small loss, NOT $2.8B.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch. The $2.8B / $6.81 EPS headlines are tax-accounting-driven (an audit-required true-up reflecting management's confidence in future taxable income), NOT operating performance. Issuer DOES disclose the $2.9B item transparently in MD&A — this is not deceptive. But the disclosure could mislead retail readers anchored on EPS. The underlying business is profitable on adj-EBITDA and FCF basis but barely above breakeven on operating GAAP. Severity is MODERATE not SEVERE because (a) full transparency, (b) the val-allowance release itself is a forward-looking POSITIVE signal — auditors only allow this when future profitability is 'more likely than not.'

Combined with $1.8B liquidity and $1.1B annual FCF, there is no financial-distress signal whatsoever — the -43% drawdown is NOT explained by balance-sheet deterioration.

#### [LYFT-09-driver-classification-overhang]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: Standard risk factor: "settlement of, any legal proceeding related to driver classification matters may require us to significantly alter our existing business model" (CA Prop 22, Massachusetts AG settlement, etc.).

Cost of revenue $3.698B (+11%) with $337.8M of increase from insurance costs (volume + higher per-mile). Issuer discloses Massachusetts AG settlement compliance, NY/CA prop-22 ongoing, federal wage-and-hour class actions standard. No new adverse 2026 rulings disclosed. Insurance reserve volatility is the recurring real-money risk.

#### [LYFT-10-recent-form4-may-22-pattern]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider transaction pattern in the final two weeks before cutoff reflects management posture.
- **f**: Inspect the 2026-05-22 Form 4s filed three days before cutoff for transaction codes.
- **M_source**: `form4 (most recent pre-cutoff window)`
- **M_value**: Three Form 4s filed 2026-05-22 for txn date 2026-05-20: (a) SVP Llewellyn — 40,309 sh code F @ $13.18 = RSU tax withholding only; (b) CAO Hope — 7,436 sh code F @ $13.18 = RSU tax withholding only; (c) CFO Brewer — 64,804 sh code F + 55,983 sh code G (offsetting Acquire/Dispose = bona fide gift to trust, NOT a sale). NO open-market sales (code S) on or near cutoff. NO open-market buys either, but absence of selling at $13.18 is itself meaningful — if insiders thought $13 was 'high,' they would have used the May 20 RSU-vest event to layer in additional code-S sales (as they did at $22 in Dec 2025). The choice not to do so is a soft positive.
- **interpretation**: Final 14-day Form 4 window is clean — no opportunistic dumping into the drawdown trough. Code F is mechanical (required to cover tax); code G is gift transfer (often estate planning), not a market exit. Consistent with the broader thesis: insiders are not panic-selling.

## NSP — Insperity, Inc.

**Sector**: Industrials  |  **Industry**: Staffing & Employment Services
**Filing**: 0001000753-26-000011_10K.txt  (2026-02-11)

### Summary
NSP disclosed its FY2025 collapse (net income -108%, Adj EBITDA -51%) honestly and prominently in the 10-K MD&A. Q1 2026 (10-Q filed 2026-05-01) shows sequential stabilization (Adj EBITDA +1% YoY, net income $33M positive). The decisive signal is Form 4: CEO Paul Sarvadi made open-market PURCHASES (code P) totaling ~$7.56M / 301,987 shares in March and May 2026, with CFO Allison ($205k), General Counsel Callens, and Director Masterson also buying in March 2026 — all in post-disclosure blackout-open windows, none under 10b5-1 plans. Aggregate executive/director open-market buys Aug 2025-May 2026: $8.4M. CEO buy:sell dollar ratio: 13:1. Auditor EY (since 1991) clean, dividend maintained at $0.60/qtr, credit facility 2.5y runway with covenant compliance. Mild flags only: covenant amendment, capitalized HR Scale dev costs, $40M claims-run-off swing. VERDICT: LONG-tier. This is exactly the inverse of a short candidate — honest disclosure + insider conviction into priced-in cyclical impairment.

### R/f/M tuples (10)

Industry medical-cost trend per benchmarks is ~7-9% for 2025, so NSP is roughly in line with peers.

However: (i) they were in compliance at 3/31/2026, (ii) net debt is modest ($370M drawn vs $660M cash, though much of that cash is restricted client-payroll funds), (iii) Facility matures Dec 15, 2028 — 2.5 years runway, (iv) dividend was maintained at $0.60/qtr throughout 2025. No going-concern qualification from EY (auditor since 1991).

## PRAA — PRA GROUP INC
**Bucket**: CONSERVATIVE LONG  |  **Composite (self-reported)**: 0.78
**Sector**: Financial Services  |  **Industry**: Credit Services
**Filing**: 0001185348-26-000006_10K.txt  (2026-03-02)

### Summary
PRAA is a textbook 'GAAP-distressed, cash-improving' name where the 34% drawdown is largely explained by a $412.6M non-cash goodwill impairment (mechanically triggered by stock-price decline + new-CEO reporting-unit reorganization) rather than operational deterioration. Underlying portfolio income +18.2%, ERC +15.4%, Q1 2026 returned to $28M GAAP profit. The Form-4 m-source signal is unambiguously LONG-tier: two independent directors deployed $1.09M of personal cash across 4 open-market PURCHASES, with Director Olsen buying THREE DAYS after the impairment was disclosed (Nov 6, 2025). Zero open-market sales by current management or directors in 2025-2026. Lenders just rolled the EUR730M EU RCF to 2031 — strongest external validation. The main caveat is that new CEO Sjolund and CFO Sehgal haven't put personal cash in (only RSU grants). Verdict: LONG-tier — honesty signal is high and insider tape is the bullish 'asymmetric P:S ratio post-bad-news' pattern the framework is designed to detect.

### R/f/M tuples (9)

#### [PRAA-01-insider-buying-directors]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied management conviction in the turnaround narrative under new CEO Martin Sjolund (effective June 17, 2025) following the $412.6M goodwill impairment.
- **f**: Parse Form 4 XML for code-P (open-market purchases) and code-S (open-market sales) by named directors/officers across 2024-01-01 to 2026-05-25; aggregate dollar value and timing relative to bad-news disclosures.
- **M_source**: `form4 (m_sources.form4 — EDGAR ownership.xml direct parse)`
- **M_value**: 73,210 shares of OPEN-MARKET PURCHASES (code P) for $1.093M aggregate by TWO independent directors over 18 months, vs. ONE small open-market sale of 5,830 shares for ~$142K by then-CEO Atal in April 2024 (sell:buy = 0.13x in $). Specific buys: Director Geir Olsen — 11,750 sh @ $21.39 (2024-08-07, $251K), 18,230 sh @ $13.59 (2025-05-07, $248K), 15,000 sh @ $14.15 (2025-11-06, $212K); Director Brett Lee Paschke — 5,000 sh @ $13.39 (2025-05-07, $67K). Olsen has been accumulating consistently across the drawdown, with his 2025-11-06 buy coming THREE days AFTER the Q3 2025 press release that disclosed the $413M goodwill impairment (i.e., he bought into the public bad news). Zero open-market sales by C-suite or directors in 2025 or 2026. The 56 'F' transactions are tax-withholding-on-vest (mandatory, non-discretionary), not voluntary sells.
- **interpretation**: STRONG honest-insider tape. Two independent directors used personal cash to add to their positions repeatedly through the drawdown, with zero asymmetric or clustered selling. Olsen's Nov 6, 2025 buy AFTER the $413M impairment announcement is the highest-conviction signal — he saw the bad news and stepped in. By the Signal-OS Form-4 m-source rubric this is exactly the 'open-market code-P purchase by director, sized meaningfully, into drawdown' pattern flagged as a LONG-bias signal. Mode B note: would expect CEO/CFO to also be buying given management's framing; absence of CEO Sjolund/CFO Sehgal buys (only equity grants) is mildly disappointing but not a red flag — Sjolund was just appointed and is still cash-poor relative to RSU comp.

#### [PRAA-02-insider-vs-calendar]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: insider transactions timed independently of capital events / non-public information; no informed selling pre-disclosure.
- **f**: Cross-reference Form 4 transaction dates against the 8-K calendar (Q3 2025 earnings 2025-11-03, EUR notes pricing 2025-09-22/24, FY 2025 earnings 2026-02-26, EUR RCF amendment 2026-05-05).
- **M_source**: `insider_vs_calendar (Form 4 dates vs 8-K filings)`
- **M_value**: All director purchases CLUSTER AFTER negative information was already public: Olsen 2025-11-06 buy is 3 days AFTER Q3 earnings (Nov 3) revealed the $413M impairment; Olsen+Paschke 2025-05-07 buys at $13.39-$13.59 are after the stock had already de-rated post Q1 2025 results (Q1 2025 press 2025-05-05). Atal's lone April 2024 open-market sale of 5,830 sh was at $24.39, well separated from any 8-K (most recent: 2024-04-07 8-K, two weeks earlier). NO clustering of insider selling before the Sept 30, 2025 goodwill-impairment trigger, before the Q3 2025 earnings, or before the FY 2025 earnings. NO insider sells in the 30 days before either the May 2025, Aug 2025, Nov 2025, or Feb 2026 prints. Form 4 calendar pattern is consistent with rules-based 10b5-1-style behavior + voluntary post-disclosure accumulation.
- **interpretation**: Timing is CLEAN. Insiders bought AFTER bad news was disclosed (when info asymmetry favored the market), not before. There is no evidence of clustered pre-disclosure selling ahead of the September 30, 2025 goodwill impairment, the CEO transition (June 2025), or any of the four quarterly prints. This directly contradicts the bear thesis that 'management knows something' — they signaled the opposite by personally buying.

#### [PRAA-03-goodwill-impairment-DBC-fully-impaired]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "As part of our September 30, 2025 interim impairment assessment, based on a sustained decrease in our stock price and market capitalization, we determined there to be an indicator of potential goodwill impairment in our Debt Buying and Collection (DBC) reporting unit. Based on the quantitative impairment test performed, we determined that the DBC reporting unit's goodwill was fully impaired and recorded an impairment charge of $412.6 million."
- **f**: Verify trigger logic and the residual goodwill on the balance sheet, plus check for any remaining at-risk goodwill in the CCB unit.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Critical Accounting Estimates + Note on Goodwill + cross-check vs Q3 10-Q)`
- **M_value**: DBC unit goodwill written down from ~$396.5M to $0 in Q3 2025 (recorded in 9/30/2025 interim test). Remaining goodwill = $26.9M, ALL related to the Class Action Claims (CCB) reporting unit. Management explicitly flags: 'Changes in our assessment of such factors, including the deterioration of market conditions, could affect our assessment of the fair value of our CCB reporting unit and could result in a goodwill impairment charge in a future period.' Net loss of $305.1M for FY 2025 is essentially the impairment ($412.6M charge); adjusted (ex-impairment) net income was actually +$72.6M. Q1 2026 reported net income of $28.2M with no further impairment.
- **interpretation**: This is the CORE driver of the drawdown but the disclosure is honest and granular. The impairment is non-cash and was triggered MECHANICALLY by the share-price decline (a self-fulfilling prophecy: low market cap -> impair goodwill -> book loss -> share price weakness). Operating business is actually growing: portfolio income +18.2%, cash collections +12.8%, ERC +15.4%. Mode-B catch on the at-risk CCB goodwill ($26.9M) is small in magnitude — even if fully impaired, that's <3% of stockholders' equity ($979.9M). Honest disclosure + small residual risk = PASS not SEVERE.

#### [PRAA-04-covenant-compliance-EU-RCF-amendment]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "The Company was in compliance with the covenants contained in its financing arrangements as of March 31, 2026." Plus Q1 2026 10-Q Note 15: April 30, 2026 European RCF amended/restated, extending maturity from Nov 2027 to April 2031.
- **f**: Mode B — given the EU RCF was amended specifically to TIGHTEN the maximum ERC ratio from 45% to 40%, infer whether this signals lender concern; verify covenant headroom and that no covenant required a waiver.
- **M_source**: `self_text (Q1 2026 10-Q Note 5 + Note 15 + 2026-05-05 8-K Item 1.01 + verifier:debt_maturity)`
- **M_value**: Three-year extension is a positive vote of confidence by EU lenders (DNB Bank as agent + syndicate) — they would not have rolled into a 5-year facility if they thought repayment risk was elevated. The simultaneous tightening of max ERC ratio (45% -> 40%) is a modest concession (likely reflects the syndicate de-risking by demanding faster collection multiples), but PRAA highlighted it as 'subject to certain conditions, joint venture investments up to EUR100M' — suggesting they got back at least one new flexibility in exchange. Total credit-facility availability $1.1B at FY-end ($825M based on current ERC + $274M conditional). Coverage is comfortable: $3.7B total borrowings vs $4.69B finance receivables + $980M equity. Compliance confirmed in both FY 2025 10-K and Q1 2026 10-Q.
- **interpretation**: Lender behavior is the strongest external validation in this filing. A distressed credit would not get a 5-year extension on its largest EU facility. The ERC ratio tightening is meaningful but not punitive (PRAA still has substantial headroom under 40%). No covenant waiver was required. Mode B: would have flagged this SEVERE if the 8-K had mentioned a forbearance, waiver, or higher pricing — none present.

#### [PRAA-05-CEO-transition-and-segment-reorg]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "The reorganization resulted from the appointment of a new chief executive officer (CEO) in 2025, which led to changes in the organizational structure and how the Company's operating results are evaluated by the Company's chief operating decision maker (CODM)."
- **f**: Mode B — given a CEO change mid-year and a segment realignment that triggered an interim goodwill test, verify the timing and check whether the reorganization itself created the impairment trigger (i.e., was this a 'big bath' coincident with the transition).
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 1 + Note on Segments + 8-K 2025-06-24 + Form 4 cluster)`
- **M_value**: CEO transition: Vikram Atal (CEO since Dec 2023) departed mid-2025; Martin Sjolund (former President - PRA Group Europe) became President & CEO via 'Amended Service Agreement, effective as of June 17, 2025' (referenced as 10.5 exhibit). The Form 4 cluster on 2025-06-17 (TEN new director/officer filings same day) confirms this is when the board action posted. Subsequent reorganization changed reporting segments to U.S. + Europe + Other Markets (instead of legacy DBC + CCB structure), which led to the interim goodwill test on 9/30/2025 because changes to reporting units require re-allocation of goodwill. The reorg is the PROXIMATE TRIGGER of the impairment but not the underlying cause — the underlying cause is the sustained stock-price decline driving market-cap below carrying value.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch: 'big bath' impairment timing concerns are real — new CEOs often impair to reset the baseline. However, the impairment was independently driven by share-price collapse (a self-reinforcing GAAP mechanic, not management discretion) and would have happened under either CEO. Honest disclosure of the proximate cause (CEO change -> CODM change -> reporting-unit change -> interim test). Not a SEVERE finding because Sjolund had been an internal executive (President of Europe) for years and his appointment was effected via 'Amended Service Agreement' — i.e., a known internal succession, not an outside hire forced by board crisis.

#### [PRAA-06-operational-underlying-performance]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "Portfolio income, the more stable and predictable yield component of our revenue, increased by 18.2% compared to 2024, outpacing the growth in cash collections... ERC of $8.6 billion at year-end, an increase of 15.4% compared to 2024." Q1 2026: net income $28.2M vs $3.7M YoY.
- **f**: Verify the underlying portfolio income / ERC / cash-collection trends are real (i.e., not a function of accounting elections) by cross-checking the three-year series and the Q1 2026 results.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Selected Financial Data + Q1 2026 10-Q income statement + verifier:ERC_trend)`
- **M_value**: 3-yr series (2023/2024/2025): Portfolio income $757M -> $857M -> $1,013M (+18% in 2025); Cash collections $1,660M -> $1,869M -> $2,108M (+12.8%); Portfolio purchases $1,154M -> $1,408M -> $1,209M (DOWN 14% in 2025 — selective deployment); ERC year-end $6.40B -> $7.46B -> $8.61B (+15%). Q1 2026 substantiates: cash collections continued to grow, U.S. legal cash collections +27%, Q1 net income $28.2M (vs $3.7M Q1 2025) on Portfolio income growth + 'recoveries collected in excess of forecast' driven by EU and SA overperformance. Adjusted ex-impairment FY 2025 net income $72.6M.
- **interpretation**: The OPERATING business is improving, not deteriorating. Honest disclosure with growing top-line metrics. Drawdown is largely a function of (a) the non-cash impairment optics and (b) U.S. portfolio purchase pullback (-25.9% YoY), which is itself a disciplined response to pricing rather than a forced retrenchment. This is the kind of name where the GAAP narrative is much worse than the cash-economic reality — exactly the setup the framework is designed to identify.

#### [PRAA-07-CFPB-regulatory-tailwind]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: "After the change in U.S. presidential administration in 2025, there have been some indications that the regulatory and enforcement activities of the CFPB may change, but the extent to which these or other future developments may impact our business remains uncertain."
- **f**: Verify the language is hedged (i.e., management isn't overclaiming a regulatory tailwind) and check for any pending CFPB enforcement matters in Legal Proceedings.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K Item 1 Regulation + Item 3 Legal Proceedings)`
- **M_value**: Disclosure is appropriately hedged — 'indications that ... may change' and 'remains uncertain.' Legal Proceedings section discusses ordinary-course litigation typical of debt collectors but does NOT disclose any open CFPB enforcement action against PRAA. The previously settled 2023 CFPB consent order ($24M) appears to be in the rearview. Hedge language suggests management is not pricing in a tailwind that hasn't yet materialized.
- **interpretation**: Honest and appropriately conservative disclosure of a potential regulatory tailwind. Not over-promising. Mode A registry check: no CFPB enforcement pending. PASS.

#### [PRAA-08-U.S.-headcount-reduction-vs-collections]  severity = **MODERATE_UNDERDELIVERY**
- **R**: "...reduction in our U.S. onshore agent headcount of approximately 40% and concurrent increase in U.S. Core cash collections of 19.8%."
- **f**: Mode B — a 40% headcount cut while collections grow nearly 20% is a strong productivity claim; verify by checking compensation-and-benefits trend and whether the cuts are reflected in operating expense lines.
- **M_source**: `self_text (10-K MD&A Operating Expenses table)`
- **M_value**: Compensation and benefits: $298.9M (2024) -> $296.7M (2025), only -$2.2M (-0.7%). A 40% U.S. onshore agent cut would imply MUCH larger compensation savings if collections growth came entirely from automation/efficiency. The small reduction suggests EITHER (a) the 40% headcount cut happened late in 2025 and full-year savings will show in 2026, OR (b) U.S. onshore agents are a small fraction of global headcount and the savings are masked by EU hiring or wage inflation, OR (c) much of the productivity came from legal-collections shift (legal collection costs +29.5% to $161.6M) — i.e., trading lower-cost agents for higher-cost legal recoveries, NOT pure efficiency.
- **interpretation**: Mode B catch. The headline 'cut 40% of agents and grew U.S. collections 20%' looks more like a SHIFT from telephone/onshore to LEGAL collections (legal costs +$36.9M) than pure efficiency. Doesn't make the narrative false — collections growth is real — but the operating-cost reduction Sjolund implies will eventually materialize is partly offset by higher legal expense. Modest finding; not severe.

#### [PRAA-09-13G-institutional-passive-no-13D]  severity = **PASS**
- **R**: Implied: no activist pressure or hostile holder positioning.
- **f**: Mode A — check EDGAR for any SC 13D or 13D/A filings in 2024-2026 indicating active investor intent.
- **M_source**: `edgar (13D/G filings)`
- **M_value**: Pre-cutoff filings since 2024-01-01: ZERO SC 13D filings; multiple SC 13G/A (Vanguard, BlackRock, Fidelity-class passive holders) — all routine passive disclosures. The most recent SC 13D was from Feb 2024 (an /A indicating final closure). No activist position has filed against PRAA through cutoff.
- **interpretation**: Mode A clean — passive institutional ownership only. No 13D activist forcing a sale or restructuring; no hostile capital structure pressure. Combined with the lender behavior (5-year EU RCF rollover) and director purchases, this reinforces the 'orderly distress' rather than 'forced action' interpretation.
